Persist measurements, and add `--estimate`: capacity for a new or changed deployment, scaled by GPU memory bandwidth from a measured deployment running the same model and engine, without sending load.
- [ ] **LLM Gateway Tuning: Render and Apply Configuration With Rollback (P2 - Medium, Issue #478)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Recommended weights still have to be copied into the gateway ConfigMap by hand. `max_input_tokens` and the per-replica Ollama entries are maintained by hand as well, and a test only checks that the entries match the replica count.
  - *Deliverable*: Render a diff of the gateway configuration: weights, `max_input_tokens` derived from each backend's manifest (vLLM `--max-model-len`, `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH`), and one entry per Ollama replica. With `--apply`, roll out the gateway, run the sweep again, and roll back if throughput drops.
- [ ] **Reasoning Budget Tuning for the Review Pool (P2 - Medium, Issue #479)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: In the tuner's cost pass, gpt-oss 20B spends its whole 200-token budget, much of it on reasoning, where the Qwen models answer in about 120 tokens. Reasoning effort is not set per deployment. The Ollama nodes may be spending serial time on reasoning a review does not need, or cutting findings short when the budget is too small.
  - *Deliverable*: Measure findings and verification outcomes against reasoning effort (low, medium) and completion budget for each reasoning deployment. Set `reasoning_effort` and `max_tokens` per deployment in the gateway.
- [ ] **Review Page Size Against Quality and Speed (P2 - Medium, Issue #480)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The analysis task's 16K window sizes review pages to fit the smallest backend, about 9,800 tokens. Every server stays eligible, but files split into more pages, and each page adds persona and verification calls. Larger pages would go only to the 64K and 44K backends.
  - *Deliverable*: Sweep the analysis context window (8K, 16K, 32K) with the performance and quality baselines, and choose the size with the best time per verified finding.
- [ ] **Embeddings Through the LLM Gateway, With Embedding Model Evaluation (P2 - Medium, Issue #481)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The embedding task still calls one Ollama node directly (`embeddinggemma:300m`), while the gateway now serves embeddings from every Ollama node (`devops-embedding` is bge-m3, and `ollama/*` passes any model through). Retrieval quality has never been compared on this repository.
  - *Deliverable*: Point the embedding task at the gateway. Compare bge-m3, embeddinggemma and nomic-embed-text on retrieval over the indexed repository (`devops ai rag`, and the `devops ai benchmark` embedding runner): recall at k on a small labelled query set, indexing time and vector size. Re-index with the best model.
  - *Constraint*: Changing the model means re-indexing Qdrant, since vector sizes differ (768 and 1024).
- [ ] **LLM Cache Effectiveness During Reviews: Response Cache and Prefix Cache (P2 - Medium, Issue #482)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Two caches can save review work: devops-cli's LLM response cache (`devops ai cache status`) and vLLM's automatic prefix cache, which can skip reprocessing a persona's static system prompt on every page. Neither is measured during reviews. The tuner showed how much prefix reuse matters: identical prompts made vLLM's prompt processing nearly free.
  - *Deliverable*: Report response-cache hit rates per review run, and vLLM prefix-cache hit rates (`vllm:prefix_cache_hits` over `vllm:prefix_cache_queries`) per backend. Check that persona prompts keep static text ahead of page content so the prefix is shared, and fix any ordering that defeats it.
- [ ] **Review Load Spreads Across the Whole LLM Pool (P1 - High, Issue #545)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: During a live review the 32B vLLM was busy in 12 of 13 samples, the 14B vLLM in none and the Ollama nodes in 0 and 1; at a stage's end all four calls sat on one server. Three causes combine. `simple-shuffle` routes by weight (5:3:1:1), not load. Pre-call checks drop the 14B server from prompts over its 12,288-token limit, which numbered pages and whole-file verification reach, sending about 71% to the 32B server. And a review keeps few calls in flight: 4 files at once, a file's pages in sequence, one call at each stage's tail.
  - *Deliverable*: Route by load (LiteLLM load-aware strategies weighed against the burst problem in the gateway config, or client-side dispatch). Keep prompts within the smallest window or send that backend only what fits. Review a file's pages in parallel and size concurrency from the pool's capacity. Measure each backend's busy share and review wall time before and after, with #546's metrics.
- [x] **LLM Serving and GPU Metrics in Prometheus (P1 - High, Issue #546)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Prometheus scrapes no `vllm:*`, `litellm_*` or GPU metric, so each backend's load, queue and latency cannot be seen or kept; #545's imbalance was found by exec-ing `nvidia-smi` in each pod. The shipped `llm-stack.json` dashboard queries generic `http_requests_total` names nothing exposes, so it is empty.
  - *Delivered*: Prometheus scrapes both vLLM servers and the gateway through monitors that kube-prometheus-stack creates, with the network policies to match. LiteLLM's Prometheus callback reports each deployment's requests, failures and latency, which covers the Ollama nodes. NVIDIA's DCGM exporter runs on every GPU node with the NVIDIA runtime and no added capability. `llm-stack.json` is rebuilt on the metrics these serve. `devops ai gateway load` reports each backend's requests, mean calls in flight, vLLM busy share and queue, and each GPU's use over a window.
- [x] **devops-cli Telemetry Never Reaches the Collector (P1 - High, Issue #547)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: devops-cli exports OTLP to `telemetry.endpoint`, `http://localhost:4318` by default, where nothing listens: the collector is a ClusterIP service. Jaeger has no services, no `devops_cli_*` metric reaches Prometheus, and the `devops-cli.json` and `ai-spend.json` dashboards are empty. The exporter fails silently.
  - *Delivered*: The collector is a NodePort, and `devops telemetry connect` finds it through kubectl, checks it answers and saves the endpoint, with no host or port in the repository. Failed exports are counted and reported to an interactive user once a day. Exports in flight are drained at exit rather than cancelled. The collector keeps metric names as sent, so `devops_cli_command_total` no longer arrives as `devops_cli_command_total_ratio`. Checked through a port-forward: devops-cli's traces reached Jaeger and its metrics reached Prometheus. Dashboard metrics nothing sends over OTLP are #564.
- [x] **Review, Findings and AI Spend Metrics Reach Prometheus (P2 - Medium, Issue #564)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: With telemetry reaching the collector (#547), the dashboards still queried review and findings metrics nothing emitted, and AI spend metrics only `devops server` exposed. Every metric was sent as a gauge from a short-lived process, so counters and histograms could not accumulate.
  - *Delivered*: Counters and histograms are sent as OTLP deltas from a per-host resource, and the collector's `deltatocumulative` processor keeps running totals. Reviews send their wall time and findings by persona, severity and status. AI calls count requests, tokens and spend by backend and model as they happen. Both dashboards read only sent metrics, checked by a test, and `docs/TELEMETRY.md` is generated from the one list of sent metrics.
- [x] **Review Verification on Its Own Model Task (P1 - High, Issue #486)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: One LLM client served persona review and verification. Faster `devops-review` weights (#476) let top-severity false positives through verification, and the hypothesis was that a stronger verifier would restore the check.
  - *Delivered*: `ai.tasks.verification` is layered on the analysis task and used by both verification call sites. Unset, analysis verifies. Validated live with the tuned weights and the 32B model verifying: reviews were slower (median 284 s against 229 s), one stalled on an uncapped verification reply (#494), nearly every candidate was rejected, and the registry-mirror HTTP false positive still passed. The false positive comes from the verification prompt, not the model (#413, #475). The setting ships unset.
- [x] **Audit Every Mechanism That Discards or Invents Findings, With a Regression Harness (P0 - Critical, Issue #509)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: A finding passes through deterministic pre-verification checks, the hallucinations catalog and its learning loop, LLM verification and debate, merging, reranking, suppression, redaction and the report's filters. Each can discard a real defect or keep a false one. The missing-symbol check invalidated real "missing check" findings and taught them to the catalog (#500). On a synthetic corpus of 10 known defects, the personas found 7 and 1 survived verification (#415).
  - *Delivered*: Four audits, one per layer, found about 60 mechanisms that change a finding's fate. The consequential ones are fixed in #500, #512, #513, #514 and #515, and the rest are tracked in #498, #499, #516 and #519. A CI regression harness drives 23 real defects from the synthetic corpus and the audit's reproductions through every deterministic layer to the report, keeps distinct defects apart, and requires nine known false alarms to stay caught. Reintroducing an audited defect fails it. On the 10-defect corpus, still-reported injections rose from 1 to 6.
- [x] **Findings Lost Between the Model Reply and the Report (P0 - Critical, Issue #512)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: From the #509 audit. Scanner findings are overwritten by persona findings, one malformed field drops a whole reply, and titles sharing half their words merge distinct defects at any line. Real titles can be blanked by sanitizing. A persona can set its own status, and redaction rewrites code (hashing calls, token sources) before any model sees it. Every loss is silent.
  - *Delivered*: Scanner findings stay beside persona findings through review and failure; OSV is queried only for exact versions, and host threat intel enters verification unverified. A reply keeps each valid finding despite a malformed field, a synonym or nested key, stray braces or a quoted `<think>`. Filler words no longer merge distinct defects, and distant findings merge only with near-identical titles naming the same symbol. Real titles survive sanitizing, personas cannot set their own status, redaction leaves code expressions alone, and BLOCKER no longer becomes MEDIUM. On the live corpus, reported injections rose from 1 to 2 of 10.
- [x] **Verification Discards Real Findings on Substring Triggers and Malformed Verdicts (P0 - Critical, Issue #513)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: From the #509 audit. Deterministic checks fire on substrings: "sse" in "processed", "if none of the roles", a bare "syntax". A verdict with `invalidated_criteria_matched` beats `verified: true`, uncertainty hides the finding, and verdicts can bind to the wrong finding.
  - *Delivered*: Verdicts are read as meant: string flags, "none" criteria, and contradictory or uncertain verdicts no longer discard a finding. Verdicts bind by title, never by a shared line alone. Each deterministic check fires on its own claim, and a miscounted line past the end of a file drops the line instead of the finding. The runtime floor is the reviewed project's own, and real secrets in tests or behind redaction markers are kept. Twelve real findings the old checks discarded now survive.
- [x] **Static Scan Reports a Clean Result When Its Scanners Are Not Installed (P2 - Medium, Issue #516)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: A review prints "Static analyzers completed (0 finding(s) detected)" after naming six analyzers, when only Bandit is installed. Semgrep, Trivy, kube-linter and Pluto are skipped silently, and Gitleaks falls back to its built-in patterns. A reader takes that as a clean scan.
  - *Delivered*: Each analyzer is recorded as ran, built-in patterns, not installed or no files. The console names the analyzers that ran and, on a separate line, those not installed. The session report has a Static Analyzers table, and `profile.json` and benchmark summaries record the states, so a benchmark shows when runs differed. The unused stage modules that still print the old message are filed as #530.
- [x] **Remove the Review Stage Modules the Pipeline Never Calls (P2 - Medium, Issue #530)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Six of the seven `run_*_stage` functions in `ai/review/stages/` had no caller: they duplicated the pipeline's logic and drifted from it, such as the static scan stage's misleading "completed" message. Tests imported them, which kept them looking alive.
  - *Delivered*: The five unused stage modules and `run_reporting_stage` are removed. Their session-directory and file-path containment checks moved onto the pipeline, which lacked them: session directories are checked before writing, and review file paths stay inside the target and its repository.
- [x] **Hallucinations Catalog Learns From Unreliable Invalidations and Never Forgets (P1 - High, Issue #514)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: From the #509 audit. The catalog learns from LLM verdicts, weak checks and evaluation replays. It rewrites builtin entries with learned words, and it never expires or lists what it learned. Some categories' ground truth only checks that the file parses.
  - *Delivered*: Only deterministic invalidations with ground truth, and people, teach the catalog. Model verdicts and evaluation replays no longer do. Builtin entries are never widened, persisted or shadowed. `devops review hallucinations list` and `remove` manage learned entries. Syntax ground truth needs a syntax claim, symbol and header entries defer to their deterministic checks, mutable defaults check the cited lines, and CWE-400 steps aside for untrusted input. Reviewers are shown builtin entries only.
- [x] **Review Prompts Apply This Repository's Rules to Every Project (P1 - High, Issue #515)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: From the #509 audit. The shared prompts carry several of this repository's rules: its Python version rules, network exemptions, house style rules and roadmap mandates. They also require linked context that the generation prompt never supplies, and keep lockfiles out of review. Build files and templates are reviewed as documentation.
  - *Delivered*: A project keeps its own review rules in `.devops/review.md`, read nearest-first for personas and verifier alike, and the shared prompts hold only general rules; devops-cli's house rules live in its own file. The verifier separates refutation from mitigation, and reports mitigated findings with the mitigation shown. Personas report missing checks with their assumption stated, and suggestions go in the summary. A PR review reads the PR head's files, each file gets its own pages, known names decide classification before content, and lockfiles reach the scanners. Live, verifier invalidations fell from 39 to 16 on the corpus.
- [x] **Pinned Open-Source Sample Repositories Across Languages (P1 - High, Issue #502)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: devops ai tooling is meant for any technical project, but every live check so far has used this Python repository and one set of Ansible playbooks. The AST engine claims TypeScript, JavaScript, Go, Rust, Java and HCL, and nothing exercises that on real code.
  - *Delivered*: A catalog of 16 permissively licensed repositories covers all 12 categories. Each is pinned to a full commit SHA, with its SPDX licence, licence files and review paths, and the model enforces that policy. `devops review samples fetch` takes only the pinned commit at depth one into `.data/samples/` (`data.samples_dir`) and verifies the commit, licence files and paths. `devops review samples list` shows the catalog and what is fetched. All 16 fetched from GitHub in 17 s (59 MB); tests fetch from local repositories, so CI stays offline.
- [x] **Synthetic Defect Templates for Application Languages (P1 - High, Issue #503)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The synthetic defect generator (#415) injects into Python, YAML and Dockerfiles only, so recall cannot be measured on other languages.
  - *Delivered*: A template pairs file suffixes with a finder per language. TypeScript/JavaScript, Go, Rust, Java, C# and C/C++ gain dropped bounds guards and the new `drop-error-check`, dropped awaits (TS/JS, C#), disabled TLS verification, widened file modes, and C/C++ `unbounded-string-copy`. A guard goes only as a whole statement whose body only exits, with no `else` and no braceless parent, and a Go error check only when `err` is read again. On the pinned samples, every C and JavaScript mutation compiles, 76 of 77 C++ mutations compile (the other trips fmt's compile-time format check), and tree-sitter finds no new parse errors in the rest. A corpus over eight samples injected 91 defects.
- [x] **Synthetic Defect Templates for Infrastructure Code and Documentation (P2 - Medium, Issue #504)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Infrastructure code and documentation get reviewed too, but the generator covers only a few YAML and Dockerfile patterns.
  - *Delivered*: Twelve new templates, with shell, Dockerfile and documentation finders added to `disable-tls-verify` and `widen-file-mode`. Terraform: `expose-public-access`, `disable-encryption`, `open-ingress`. Dockerfiles: `run-as-root`, `unverified-download`. Shell: `drop-strict-mode`, `unquote-expansion`, `pipe-to-shell`, `curl --insecure`. Kubernetes: `enable-host-network`, `mount-host-path`, `drop-resource-limits`. Docs: insecure example commands inside code blocks and `contradict-documented-default`. Every site on the pinned samples still parses (HCL, YAML, `bash -n`, each Dockerfile `RUN`), and a corpus over them injected 31 defects.
- [x] **Validate devops ai Tooling Across the Sample Repositories (P1 - High, Issue #505)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Nothing shows whether AST parsing, repomaps, analysis and reviews work on projects that are not Python: which parser runs, whether pages and labels are right, and whether the reviewer finds defects in each language.
  - *Delivered*: `devops review samples validate` saves a JSON report per category. It records each file's parser and symbols, the file analysis and the repository map. With `--review` it adds a scored review of the category's defect corpus, and it names the problems it finds. The first full run (64 minutes, default persona) found 28 of 109 injected defects and kept 22. Its failures are filed: bare-name locations (#535), a verifier that invalidates what its reason confirms (#536), injections inside comments (#537), tree-sitter never installed (#538), no AST for YAML, Dockerfiles and Helm (#539, beside #506), C++ labelled C (#540), and a documented-default template that cannot be judged without code (#541).
- [x] **Findings Located by a Bare File or Function Name Lose Their File (P1 - High, Issue #535)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The first sample validation (#505) found 36 of 208 candidate findings located by a bare file name (`Dockerfile:7`) or a function name (`getBodySize:18-24`). They were tied to no file, so readers could not find them, corpus scoring could not match them and deterministic checks could not read them. Resolving bare file names alone raised that run from 28 to 33 injections found.
  - *Delivered*: The per-file review ties each page's bare locations to the file under review, keeping lines or targets, when they name that file or a symbol the page shows. Another file's name, and symbols the page lacks, are left alone and counted in the payload's `unanchored_locations`. In a live run over four sample categories, 21 of 75 candidates were anchored and none was left without its file, where the first run had 36 of 208.
- [x] **One Data Directory Across Git Worktrees (P1 - High, Issue #553)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: A relative data directory resolved against the current git worktree, so each worktree kept its own reviews, benchmarks, corpora, sample validations, caches and catalog, and removing a worktree deleted them; the first full sample validation (#505) was lost that way. The spend ledger resolved against the current directory.
  - *Delivered*: Relative data paths resolve against the main worktree, found through a linked worktree's `commondir`, and absolute paths are kept. Every data output uses one resolver, the spend ledger included; checked-in input datasets still resolve in the current worktree.
- [x] **Defect Generator Injects Into Code Examples Inside Block Comments (P1 - High, Issue #537)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The first sample validation (#505) found 9 of 78 brace-language injections inside multi-line comments, all dropped awaits in ky's JSDoc examples. The finders blanked one-line comments only, so documentation counted as code and the corpus scored reviewers on defects that do not exist.
  - *Delivered*: Block comments spanning lines are blanked for every brace-language finder and the Terraform block tracker; a `/*` or `//` inside a string opens no comment. TLS, file-mode and Terraform substitutions apply only outside comments. On the pinned samples no site falls inside a comment. TypeScript `drop-await` sites fell from 99 to 44 and `drop-bounds-check` from 15 to 12; other languages are unchanged.
- [x] **Verifier Invalidates Findings Its Own Reason Confirms (P1 - High, Issue #536)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The first sample validation (#505) found all three unpinned `:latest` image findings invalidated although the verifier's reason confirmed the defect: it filed the finding's own verification criterion, or its fix, as the invalidation it matched. They were 3 of the run's 6 found-then-dropped injections.
  - *Delivered*: A matched invalidation criterion that restates the finding's claim or fix, or a reason that states the claimed condition without negating it, no longer invalidates; the finding stays unverified and reported. Genuine refutations still invalidate. The run's misread verdicts are regression cases in the #509 harness.
- [x] **The AST Engine Never Uses Tree-Sitter: Its Grammars Are Not Installed (P1 - High, Issue #538)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The first sample validation (#505) found every TypeScript, JavaScript, Go, Rust, Java and HCL file read by the regex fallback, because tree-sitter was never a dependency; 16 of 31 TypeScript and 10 of 37 Rust files yielded no symbols.
  - *Delivered*: tree-sitter and the official grammars are dependencies, TypeScript and TSX load by their own entry points, and the native walk names arrow-function declarators, Go types, Rust enums and macros, Java enums, records and annotations, and top-level Terraform blocks. Every sample file in those languages now parses with tree-sitter: TypeScript symbols rose from 54 to 194 and Rust from 763 to 1,552, and the only files without symbols declare nothing.
- [x] **Gateway Provider and Per-Task AI Settings From the CLI and Environment (P2 - Medium, Issue #456)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Routing a task to the gateway took an edit of `config.yaml`: `devops ai config` rejected `gateway`, `devops config set` handled only two-level keys, and `DEVOPS_CLI_AI_TASK_*` overrides failed the same way and were silently dropped.
  - *Delivered*: `devops ai config` accepts `gateway` and takes `--task` to set one task's provider and model. `devops config set` takes keys of any depth, typed by the field, and refuses unknown ones. Environment overrides apply, or raise an error naming the variable.
- [x] **AST Support for C#, C/C++, Shell and Markdown (P2 - Medium, Issue #506)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The AST engine maps Python, TypeScript, JavaScript, Go, Rust, Java and HCL. C#, C, C++, shell and Markdown files yield no symbols, so repomaps, context packing and review grounding see nothing in them.
  - *Delivered*: tree-sitter grammars for C#, C, C++, bash and Markdown. A `.h` header is C++ when its content is. C and C++ names come through declarator chains, so a header's prototypes behind include guards, `extern "C"` and export macros are listed. C# members, shell functions and Markdown headings (a new `heading` kind) are extracted, and regex fallbacks cover each language. Checked on serilog, cJSON, fmt, nvm and the kind docs.
- [x] **Review Pages Number Their Source Lines (P1 - High, Issue #499)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Review pages give the model source text without line numbers, and a part of a split file starts mid-file with no offset, so every reported line range is the model's own count. The synthetic defect corpus (#415) caught it: an injected `mkdir(mode=0o777)` at line 248 was reported at lines 106–108 and 112–115. Locations drive patching, duplicate consolidation by line overlap and the verifier's out-of-range check.
  - *Delivered*: Every page line carries its line number in the file, parts of split files and diffs included (new-file numbers; removed lines none). Split pages overlap and repeat the file header or diff preamble, including when the pipeline re-splits them for a smaller window. The verifier finds a finding's excerpt by number in any part of the file and reads the whole file numbered, and the prompts ask personas to cite the numbers. On the 10-defect corpus, injections found at their line rose from 4 to 7 of the 7 found, and the defect at line 248 was reported at 248-250 instead of 127-130; prompt tokens per review rose 3.1%.
- [x] **Missing-Symbol Check Leaves "Missing Check" Findings Alone (P1 - High, Issue #500)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The deterministic check for false "symbol not defined" claims runs on any finding containing the word "missing", then invalidates it if a symbol it names exists. A finding about a missing check always names the function lacking it, so real defects are invalidated without a model, and `auto_record_invalidated_finding` teaches them to the hallucinations catalog. On the synthetic corpus (#415) it invalidated 4 findings of injected defects, among them "Missing inheritance depth limit in load_policy".
  - *Delivered*: The check runs only on claims that a name does not exist: import and name errors, "is not defined", "cannot be imported", or a missing import or code-like name. Replayed over the #415 corpus review, none of the 9 findings it had invalidated was such a claim, and all 9 now go to the verifier. Live, it invalidated none, against 9 before. Recall after verification stayed at 1 in 10, because the verifier model drops the rest (#475).
- [x] **Text Output Functions Run Only When They Take the Text Alone (P1 - High, Issue #510)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `uv check` began fetching ty v0.0.84, which rejects calling a `TextOutput` function with the text alone, and the gate failed on every branch. The call was wrong in practice too. A function taking a `RunContext` raised into a bare `except`, so the reply went unparsed, and an async function returned an un-awaited coroutine as the parsed model.
  - *Delivered*: The repair path calls a text output function only when it is synchronous and takes the text alone, and otherwise leaves the reply to the schema-free path. `uv check` passes with ty v0.0.84. Pinning ty (`uv check --ty-version`) remains an option, traded against the latest-tools policy.
- [x] **A Shipped Release Closes Its Epic While the Roadmap Header Lags (P2 - Medium, Issue #521)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Release epic #297 stayed open after v0.2.22 shipped, with its tag, GitHub release and closed milestone. The epic sync closed an epic only when its roadmap header said Completed, and each release had marked the previous one done, one step late. Eight delivered v0.2.22 entries were also left unticked.
  - *Delivered*: The epic sync treats a closed milestone as a completed release. The roadmap marks v0.2.22 Completed and v0.2.23 the active release, and ticks the eight delivered entries with their PRs. Running the sync closed #297 at 20 of 20 deliverables.
- [x] **Finding Locations Keep Paths With `+`, `@`, `~` or `%` (P2 - Medium, Issue #498)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `canonicalize_finding_location` matches paths with `[a-zA-Z0-9_\-./\\]`. A path outside that class falls through to a fallback that keeps its first path-like fragment, so every finding on files under a corpus named `playbooks+core+...` was saved as `.data/reviews/corpora/playbooks`, with no file and no lines.
  - *Delivered*: The location patterns share one path character class that accepts letters and digits of any script and `+`, `@`, `~` and `%`. Paths such as `include/c++/`, `node_modules/@scope/`, `logo@2x.svg`, `~/.config/` and `100%-coverage.md` keep their file and lines: alone, as ranges, normalized from `#L` anchors and `, lines`, and extracted from prose.
- [x] **Evaluation Run Store: Every Benchmark and Evaluation Saved and Shared (P1 - High, Issue #554)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Benchmarks and evaluations are meant to be repeated over the life of the tool, but only some keep results, each in its own shape: review profiles and benchmarks, corpora and sample validations under the data directory. `corpus score`, `ai gateway tune` and `ai prompt-eval` keep nothing, and `ai benchmark` saves reports only to its own directory. Nothing is shared between workstations: Valkey is unreachable from them and keeps its data on an `emptyDir`.
  - *Delivered*: Each run is one JSON record under `data.runs_dir`. It holds the version and commit, the setup and its fingerprint, the subject and its key, and the results. Review setup covers models, page size and each gateway pool's backends and weights from `/model/info`. Review benchmarks, sample validations, corpus scores (each injection's outcome), gateway tunes, prompt evaluations and AI benchmarks all write one. A dedicated, password-protected Valkey (`valkey-runs`, PVC, append-only, NodePort) holds the shared index. `devops ai runs connect` finds it and stores its password in the keyring, and `devops ai runs reindex` rebuilds it. Runs are kept locally, with the reason, when the index is unavailable.
- [x] **Evaluation History, Comparison and Regression Checks (P1 - High, Issue #555)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Runs are compared by hand: score tables side by side, or JSON diffed in scratch scripts. A result is useful over the life of the tool only if the next run can be set against it.
  - *Delivered*: Subcommands under `devops ai runs` (`list`, `show`, `compare`, `baseline (set|list|show)`, `check`) to inspect historical runs, compare setups and metrics (recall, tokens, wall time, backend shares), manage explicit baselines across local storage and the shared Valkey index, and evaluate runs against tolerance limits to gate CI and release choreography.
- [x] **Defect Template Well-Formedness Check as a Command (P2 - Medium, Issue #556)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Templates were verified by hand for #503, #504 and #537: every site applied to the pinned samples and checked with tree-sitter, gcc, g++, node, `bash -n`, python-hcl2, PyYAML and a block-comment tracker. That caught nginx's control-flow checksum, Hugo code shortcodes and JSDoc injections, and should run whenever a template or sample changes.
  - *Delivered*: Standalone `devops review templates check` (alias `sweep`) and `devops review templates list` commands sweeping defect templates over fetched samples, reporting sites per template and category, verifying mutations with multi-language checkers (gcc, g++, node, `bash -n`, python-hcl2, PyYAML, AST/tree-sitter), flagging comment collisions, honestly reporting missing checkers as `NOT_RUN`, and persisting runs in the evaluation run store (`Mechanism.TEMPLATE_SWEEP`) for regression checks and release gating.
- [x] **Backend Activity in Review Profiles and Benchmarks (P1 - High, Issue #557)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: #545's imbalance was measured with a script sampling `nvidia-smi` and vLLM queues during a review. Profiles count calls per backend, not how long each was busy or how many ran at once, so a benchmark cannot show whether a routing change spread the load.
  - *Delivered*: Profiles record per backend and stage the calls, busy seconds (the union of calls), call seconds and peak and mean concurrent calls, from each call's duration and serving backend. Benchmark summaries keep each backend's median busy share and peak, and the benchmark table shows them. Direct model requests report their duration. The pool's busy share over a window moved to #546, whose metrics it reads.
- [x] **Roadmap Sync Reads Each Item's Priority From Its Own Tag (P1 - High, Issue #583)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `devops gh sync-roadmap` took each new issue's priority label from the first keyword found anywhere in the item's header or body, P0 words first, so "Blocker" in a title or "critical" or "higher" in a description overrode the item's own tag. After the vibes gap entries landed, 19 of 189 open items would have been filed with the wrong label: 17 as P0 and 2 as P1.
  - *Delivered*: The priority comes only from the item's own `(P0 - ...)` to `(P3 - ...)` tag, inside the bold title or directly after it; only the digit counts, and title words and description text never change it. An untagged item keeps the P1 default. The keyword table is gone, and a test over the live roadmap asserts that every open item has a tag and derives exactly that priority.
- [x] **Gate Checks the Worktree It Runs In, Not the Main Checkout (`devops ci`) (P0 - Critical, Issue #582)** — delivered in #585, corrected in #612:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `_get_project_root` (`src/devops_cli/commands/ci.py:95-99`) returns `find_top_level_repo_root()` (`src/devops_cli/core/repo.py:45-59`), which walks every parent of the working directory and keeps the top-most one holding `.git` or `pyproject.toml`. Every check then runs there (`ci.py:150`, `:213`), and changed-file test selection uses the same root (`ci.py:667`). Claude Code and its workflow tool create agent worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/`, inside the main checkout, so `devops ci` and the `devops-ci-pre-push` and `devops-ci-test-changed` hooks gate the main checkout's files instead of the branch being committed or pushed. Pushing this roadmap change from such a worktree failed lint, typecheck and `uv check` on another session's uncommitted files, which the pushed branch does not contain. The reverse case passes silently: a broken branch in a worktree clears the gate whenever the main checkout is clean. `main_worktree_root` (`repo.py:62`) already reads a linked worktree's `.git` file so that every worktree shares one data directory (#553); the gate needs the opposite answer.
  - *Deliverable*: Add a resolver that stops at the nearest enclosing worktree: the first directory whose `.git` is a `gitdir:` file with a `commondir`, or a `.git` directory. Use it for the commands that verify the current tree (`ci`, `test`, `uv`, `release`, `scan complexity`), keep `main_worktree_root` for shared data, and print the resolved root in the gate header so a wrong root is visible. Test from a real `git worktree add` nested inside a repository: a lint error present only in the worktree fails the gate, and one present only in the main checkout does not.
  - *Constraint*: The top-most rule dates from v0.1.8 and about twenty modules call it (`analyze`, `cleanup`, `repomap`, `ast/graph`, `benchmark` and others), plausibly so commands run inside repositories cloned under `repos/` find the workspace root. Changing `find_top_level_repo_root` itself would move all of them; add the gate resolver separately and audit the other callers on their own. A submodule's `.git` file has no `commondir` and must keep resolving as it does today. `main_worktree_root` starts from the top-most root, so it cannot detect a worktree nested inside the checkout either; the audit should cover it.
  - *Measured*: From a worktree at `.claude/worktrees/roadmap-vibes`, `find_top_level_repo_root()` returned the main checkout. The pre-push `uv run devops ci` then failed `lint`, `typecheck` and `uv_check` on `src/devops_cli/ai/schema_reflection.py` and `src/devops_cli/ai/response_repair.py:390`, while `ls src/devops_cli/ai/schema_reflection.py` in the worktree reported no such file. The same branch checked out outside the checkout resolved to itself.
  - *Corrected*: The Deliverable above said to stop at the first `.git` directory, and #585 did, which contradicted the Constraint: clones under `repos/` and their worktrees moved onto themselves, their shared data into the clone, and a stale nested worktree still gated the main checkout. #612 keeps them on the workspace and checks stale worktrees.
- [x] **Worktree Gate Resolution Keeps repos/ Clones on the Workspace and Checks Stale Worktrees (P1 - High, Issue #612)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: #585's resolver stopped at the first directory with its own `.git`, so a clone under `repos/` and each worktree of it resolved to the clone, for the gate and for shared data, which landed in the clone's working tree. A nested worktree whose git directory was pruned, or whose checkout moved, still gated the main checkout. A cached verdict and single checks such as `devops ci test` never named the root they judged, and `commands/ci.py` kept an import-time root.
  - *Delivered*: `find_worktree_root` stops at the nearest linked worktree, live or stale, and otherwise keeps the workspace root, so the main checkout, clones under `repos/`, their worktrees and submodules resolve as before #585; `main_worktree_root` shares the workspace's data with them again. The gate names its root before the cache lookup and for every single check, warns from a stale worktree with the fix for each cause, and resolves its root on each call. An acceptance probe over eleven real layouts matches.
- [x] **Code-Reading AI, IaC and Scan Commands Use the Worktree They Run In (P1 - High, Issue #613)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: From a worktree nested in the checkout, `devops ai repomap`, `devops ai diagram arch`, `devops ai ast graph` (empty, because the checkout's `.gitignore` excludes `.claude/`), `devops review auto-fix`, `devops tf` deploys and Kube-linter locations all acted on the main checkout.
  - *Delivered*: Each reads the worktree it runs in. `devops tf` also stops an auto-approved deploy, and asks before an interactive one, when the main checkout's provider directory holds local resource state the worktree lacks, since `git worktree add` copies no untracked `terraform.tfstate` and `apply` would plan to create every resource again; a remote backend never stops it.
- [ ] **Mitigated Findings Re-Verified When Their Perimeter Changes (P2 - Medium, Issue #587)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: A MITIGATED finding (`src/devops_cli/ai/review_schema.py:370-371`), set by the verifier (`ai/review/verification.py:1134-1149`) or `devops review verify --status MITIGATED` (`commands/review.py:896-908`), is `reportable=False` and dropped from the merge recommendation (`review_schema.py:793`). Nothing records what mitigates it: the updates dict at `verification.py:1171-1181` discards the verifier's `reason`, and no field names the mechanism or its file. The only file-keyed cross-session store (`ai/review/common_hallucinations.py:966` `auto_record_invalidated_finding`) writes only on INVALIDATED. Neither `devops review` (`ai/review/runner.py:1583-1645`, `:1646-1680`) nor `devops pr check-readiness` (`commands/pr.py:1304-1393`) intersects the diff with mitigations. vibes `patterns/kinetic-exploit-probes-and-invariant-leashing.md` calls this mitigation perimeter decay; `observations/systems/30-kinetic-falsification-and-the-ephemeral-exploit-harness.md` §2.3: a gateway 10MB ceiling bypassed by a later streaming endpoint while the finding sat mitigated.
  - *Deliverable*: In order. First, record the mitigation: add `mitigating_mechanism: str | None` (vibes `patterns/epistemic-seam-and-mitigated-defect-auditing.md`) and `perimeter_files: list[str]` to `Finding`; make the verifier prompt (`ai/tasks/verify_finding_system.md`) name both for `mitigated: true`, else degrade to UNVERIFIED (also `reportable=False`, `verification.py:1154-1155`); stop setting `mitigated=True` on INVALIDATED (`verification.py:1136-1138`). Give `devops review verify --status MITIGATED` repeatable `--perimeter <path>`, a mitigation reason field replacing `invalidation_reason`, and optional `--regression-test <path>`, which `core/coverage_index.py:187` and `core/test_selection.py:112` (via `commands/ci.py:665-667`) already re-select on perimeter change. Human-accepted mitigations go to a tracked ledger like `ai/review/common_hallucinations.json`; model-only verdicts stay session-local. Second, measure before enforcing: `devops review branch|pr` and `_evaluate_pr_blockers` intersect the diff with ledger `perimeter_files` and warn 'N mitigated findings whose perimeter changed', naming each, no exit-code change; no `InvariantLeashRegistry` port. Executable leash probes (pytest per finding, `pytest-timeout` as new pinned dev dependency, untrusted-repo probes only inside `docker/sandbox.py:135` `WorkloadSandboxRunner`) follow Executable Verification Criteria separately.
  - *Constraint*: Locality: `.gitignore:63` ignores `.data/*` and `_get_reviews_base_dir` (`ai/review/review_environment.py`) resolves sessions locally, so CI has nothing to intersect; the warning reads only the tracked ledger, separate from the hallucination catalog (glob-keyed, suppression polarity). Polarity: vibes `tools/kinetic_probe.py` maps exit 0 to 'confirmed' and non-zero to 'refuted', so an import failure reads as 'mitigation holds'; collection/import errors must re-open the finding, and an unresolvable perimeter path is reported, not dropped. Until the flag is repaired (`verification.py:1138` True on INVALIDATED, `commands/review.py:904` False), consumers key on `status == MITIGATED`. The 'Executable Verification Criteria' item's 'bounded subprocess sandbox (`review_environment`)' does not exist (no subprocess call there, no `setrlimit` in `src/`; only `WorkloadSandboxRunner` caps resources); correct that entry. A model-written probe is registered only after human acceptance via `devops review verify` -- the epistemic-seam rule `verification.py:1142-1144` already breaks, writing the hallucination catalog from the model's `invalidated_criteria_matched` unguarded though the 'Model-in-the-Loop Prompt Benchmarking' item treats only human-verified records as trustworthy.
  - *Measured*: `grep -rIl -i` for leash, InvariantLeashRegistry, perimeter_files and mitigating_mechanism across src, tests, .github, scripts and pyproject.toml: 0 files each. `wc -l src/devops_cli/ai/review/review_environment.py`: 51. `grep -rn 'setrlimit\|RLIMIT' src/ | wc -l`: 0. `find .data/reviews -name findings.json | wc -l`: 0 in this checkout (only `sample-validations` exists), so local MITIGATED incidence could not be measured; the second deliverable is what measures it.
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/kinetic-exploit-probes-and-invariant-leashing.md`, `tools/kinetic_probe.py`, `observations/systems/30-kinetic-falsification-and-the-ephemeral-exploit-harness.md`, `patterns/epistemic-seam-and-mitigated-defect-auditing.md`
- [ ] **GenAI Span Attributes Checked Against the Pinned Semantic Conventions (P1 - High, Issue #588)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `telemetry/tracer.py:435` calls `record_llm_metrics` 'standard OpenTelemetry GenAI attributes', yet emitters (`ai/client/unified.py:63-93,365-372,484-491,782-806`, `ai/client/structured.py:232`, `ai/direct.py:104`) write 30 distinct `gen_ai.*` keys with no convention named. Against `opentelemetry-semantic-conventions` 0.65b0 (installed by `opentelemetry-sdk`, undeclared at `pyproject.toml:37`), 9 of 30 are defined and all 9 deprecated (3 renamed, 6 moved); 21 are private keys in its namespace. `gen_ai.provider.name` is never emitted; old and new token keys are dual-written (`tracer.py:439-443`, `unified.py:83-87`). `tests/test_telemetry.py:293-300` pins the deprecated and private names as 'GenAI standard attributes'. `telemetry/logfire.py:217` instruments `pydantic-ai-slim`, which emits `gen_ai.provider.name`, so one request reaches Logfire in both vocabularies. vibes hit the same defect (`observations/systems/19`: a wrong key is accepted by every collector and renders as no traffic); `tools/semconv_snapshot.py` records the GenAI conventions moved repository with no tagged release.
  - *Deliverable*: Two PRs; report before rename. PR 1: `devops telemetry semconv` (and a report-only test) classifies every `gen_ai.*` literal in `src/`, keys and span names, as defined, renamed (with replacement), moved, private or general-OTel. `refresh` runs `weaver registry resolve` on `open-telemetry/semantic-conventions-genai` at a pinned commit (weaver on PATH or pinned image, else refuse), recording the commit in committed `telemetry/semconv_genai.json`; stdlib-only `verify` checks commit equals pin and every literal classifies, wired into `devops ci` beside `docs` (`commands/ci.py:370-378`). From vibes take only the JSON shape and two rules ('moved' is not 'deprecated'; unparseable deprecation prose is refused), not its resolver. Declare `opentelemetry-semantic-conventions` in `pyproject.toml` for general attributes. PR 2: the test fails on any renamed or private `gen_ai.*` key; `gen_ai.system`→`gen_ai.provider.name`; usage keys (`unified.py:83-87`, `tracer.py:439-443`) route through `RunUsage.opentelemetry_attributes()`, dropping `prompt_tokens`/`completion_tokens`; `gen_ai.server.address`→`server.address`; TTFT→`gen_ai.response.time_to_first_chunk`; undefined measurements move to `llm.*` (`unified.py:66-75`); `gen_ai.chat`/`gen_ai.stream` adopt a registry span type or are declared out of scope; `record_llm_metrics` (uncalled in `src/`) becomes the single seam or is deleted; `tests/test_telemetry.py:293-300` changes in the same commit, no dual-writing retained.
  - *Constraint*: A prefix allowlist or a second hand-written table like `_ATTRIBUTE_NORMALIZATION` (`tracer.py:454-475`, no GenAI entries) is what produced the drift. The installed package is not the oracle (its `gen_ai` constants are all deprecated as moved); snapshot from the GenAI repository at a recorded commit. Weaver output stability across refreshes is unverified; if unstable, commit only the reduced table. No in-tree dashboard or alert references `gen_ai.*` (0 hits under `k8s/`); external ad-hoc queries are a release-note item. The report is the rename's completion proof; renaming without it, or keeping old keys beside new, adds a third vocabulary.

Script diffing those 30 against `opentelemetry.semconv._incubating.attributes.gen_ai_attributes` (0.65b0 via `importlib.metadata`): defined=9 (renamed=3, moved=6), undefined=21. `grep -rn gen_ai.provider.name src | wc -l`: 0. `grep -rn gen_ai k8s | wc -l`: 0.

- [ ] **Thinking-Stream Sanitizer: Initial State, Unopened-Tag Count and Thought Cap (P2 - Medium, Issue #590)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Two hand-rolled FSMs implement one think-block sanitizer: `ThinkingStreamProcessor` (`src/devops_cli/ai/thinking_stream.py:51`, used by chat at `commands/ai.py:881-884`) and `StreamingTokenProcessor` (`src/devops_cli/ai/client/streaming.py:23`, behind `sanitize=True` at `client/unified.py:793-796`, unused in `src/`). Both start only in EMITTING (`thinking_stream.py:74`, `streaming.py:58`), so a closing tag seen while emitting passes through as text (`_process_outside_think` seeks only the open tag, `thinking_stream.py:135-157`); `strip_think_blocks`/`extract_think_blocks` (`thinking_stream.py:15-40`), the review pipeline's path (`review/runner.py:457`, `client/structured.py:50,104`, `response_repair.py:53`), match only open…close pairs. Neither caps the thought buffer (`streaming.py:64-69` bounds only the whole stream) nor reports EOF inside a thought: `streaming.py:176-191` resets `in_think` and `tests/test_ai_streaming.py:128-137` asserts that erasure. vibes `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md:173` records this design leaking a whole chain of thought when the template pre-opens it; `examples/streaming-reasoning-sanitizer/sanitizer.py` fixes it with `starts_thinking`, `unopened_close_tags`, `unclosed_stream` and a 65,536-character cap (line 18). Today's pool (`k8s/llm/gateway/configmap.yaml:17-96`) is safe only because adapters re-wrap out-of-band reasoning as complete blocks (`streaming.py:252,279,299`); the leak goes live with the first DeepSeek-R1/Qwen3-class model served by vLLM without `--reasoning-parser` (none at `k8s/llm/vllm/deployment.yaml:68`) or reached via the `ollama/*` pass-through (`configmap.yaml:103`). Neither #475 nor #479 plans such a model: a latent defect to close before any pool change.
  - *Deliverable*: Maintained control first: vLLM `--reasoning-parser` for any DeepSeek-R1/Qwen3-class deployment (Ollama's `thinking` field is already consumed, `streaming.py:252`), making the sanitizer defence in depth. Then three PRs: (1) one FSM core behind `ThinkingStreamProcessor`'s callbacks and the `StreamingTokenProcessor` adapter (both public re-exports, `ai/__init__.py:904-905`, `ai/client/__init__.py:51-52`; no deletion); add `unopened_close_tags`, `unclosed_stream` and `discarded_thought_chars` to `to_dict()` and the `gen_ai.stream` span (`unified.py:780`); run `strip_think_blocks`/`extract_think_blocks` through the core. (2) `initial_state` (`emitting`|`thinking`) and `thinking_tags` via `get_model_thinking_tags`/`supports_thinking`, overridable per model in `ModelProfileSpec` through `_apply_profile_spec` (`ai/profiles/__init__.py:62`), not the gateway configmap; default `emitting`. (3) `max_thought_chars`, default 65,536, discarding and counting, plus split-tag property tests under the Structured Fuzzing item (which adds Hypothesis). Port from vibes only the two regression tests (`test_sanitizer.py:142-158`) and metrics shape; add one closing-tag-only test through `strip_think_blocks`.
  - *Constraint*: pydantic-ai 2.35.0 does not cover the mechanism (`_parts_manager.py:200,214` matches a tag only as a whole delta; `_thinking_part.py:24-26` emits an unclosed thought as `TextPart`): adopting it would regress split-tag handling 42 tests in `tests/test_thinking.py` and `tests/test_ai_streaming.py` cover. A wrong `initial_state` is worse than none (`thinking` on a never-closing model suppresses the whole answer), hence counters ship first, default `emitting`, per-model override. The cap discards and counts, never raises; 65,536 is the vibes default, to revisit against #479's budgets. `tests/test_ai_streaming.py:128-137` must assert `unclosed_stream is True` instead of `in_think is False`.
  - *Measured*: `python -` feeding `['Deciding what to commit.', '</think>', 'git commit -m x']` into `ThinkingStreamProcessor(show_thinking=False)`, `StreamingTokenProcessor()` and `strip_think_blocks(''.join(chunks))`: all return `'Deciding what to commit.</think>git commit -m x'`, thought empty. `'<think>' + 'a'*1_000_000` then `flush()`: both hold a 1000000-char thought; `ThinkingStreamProcessor.in_think` True, `StreamingTokenProcessor.in_think` False. `python -m pytest tests/test_thinking.py tests/test_ai_streaming.py -o addopts="" -p no:cacheprovider --collect-only -q`: 42 tests. `importlib.metadata.version('pydantic-ai')`: 2.35.0.
  - *Source*: vibes `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md` (the 'state machine that cannot be told where it starts' bullet), `examples/streaming-reasoning-sanitizer/sanitizer.py`, `examples/streaming-reasoning-sanitizer/test_sanitizer.py` — initial state, unopened-tag count, unclosed flag and cap; `patterns/streaming-reasoning-isolation-and-token-budgeting.md` — cap only; `observations/devops-cli/13-streaming-reasoning-token-parsers-and-think-block-sanitization.md` — cap only, and stale about devops-cli enforcing it.
- [ ] **Reasoning-Token Share in the Spend Ledger and Provider-Reported Stream Usage (P2 - Medium, Issue #591)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Nothing records reasoning tokens. Two FSMs isolate `<think>` blocks (`ai/thinking_stream.py:51`, `ai/client/streaming.py:23`), and each provider reasoning delta is rewrapped in its own `<think>…</think>` (`streaming.py:252,279,299`). `LLMResponse` carries `thinking` text beside prompt/completion/total tokens (`ai/client/models.py:84-87`); the ledger (`ai/spend/ledger.py:33-35`), both token exporters (`ai/spend/prometheus.py:86-87`, `ledger.py:486-487`) and the span token attributes (`ai/client/unified.py:82-89`) have no reasoning field. Stream spend is hand-counted: `_record_stream_spend` runs `count_tokens` over the joined chunks (`unified.py:809,833`). Both stream callers (`ai/agents/agent.py:1110`, `commands/ai.py:942`) keep the default `sanitize=False` (`unified.py:738,766`), so reasoning and its wrapper tags inflate completion tokens with no split. #479 infers "much of it on reasoning" with no record behind it. vibes `patterns/streaming-reasoning-isolation-and-token-budgeting.md` budgets what reasoning may cost but caps only a character buffer (`max_thought_chars`).
  - *Deliverable*: First, probe the gateway once on the `devops-review` gpt-oss entries (`k8s/llm/gateway/configmap.yaml:85,92`; the Qwen entries do not reason), stream and non-stream, and record whether `usage.completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` arrives and sits inside `completion_tokens`. PR 1, stream usage from the provider: request `stream_options: {include_usage: true}` on the OpenAI-compatible stream and read its final usage chunk, Ollama's final `eval_count` and Claude's `message_delta` usage; `count_tokens` remains only as a fallback flagged estimated. This fixes the overcount alone. PR 2, reasoning end to end: `ai/client/openai.py`, the gateway path reviews use, reads `reasoning_tokens` (it reads only three usage fields at `openai.py:134-137`) into `LLMResponse`; `track_request_spend` and a ledger column added in place like `served_by` (`ledger.py:53`) carry it; `devops_cli_ai_tokens_total` gains `type="reasoning"`, which `ai-spend.json:381` already splits; spans use `GEN_AI_USAGE_REASONING_OUTPUT_TOKENS` from opentelemetry-semantic-conventions, pinned directly; `devops ai cost report` adds a reasoning-share column, including `--by backend`. `ai/direct.py` also reads `resp.usage.details`, where pydantic-ai maps the same field. Claude and native Ollama report no count, so estimate over `LLMResponse.thinking`, labelled. No per-route budget: #479 sets `reasoning_effort` and `max_tokens` per deployment, and any alert belongs there once share is measured.
  - *Constraint*: Reasoning is a subset of completion, never added to it, or spend double-counts; no dashboard may sum across `type`. Ollama returns one `eval_count` beside a separate `thinking` string, so whether it includes thinking is checked before any estimate is split out. Estimated rows are labelled in the report and kept out of #479's comparison. Per-stream character counters belong to the drafted Thinking-Stream Sanitizer: Initial State, Unopened-Tag Count and Thought Cap; a second set here is worse than none. Enforcing a budget by lowering `max_tokens` reproduces the findings-cut-short failure #479 describes.
  - *Measured*: `grep -rn reasoning_tokens src | wc -l` gives 1, a function name in a knowledge-base markdown file. `grep -rn 'include_usage\|stream_options' src` finds nothing. `.venv/bin/python probe/stream_count.py` (synthetic one-word reasoning deltas wrapped as the clients wrap them, then content deltas) counts 321 tokens on the `sanitize=False` spend path against 59 of reasoning text and 20 of content.
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/streaming-reasoning-isolation-and-token-budgeting.md`, vibes `examples/streaming-reasoning-sanitizer/sanitizer.py`, vibes `observations/devops-cli/13-streaming-reasoning-token-parsers-and-think-block-sanitization.md`
- [ ] **bubblewrap Confinement for Criteria Commands and One Sandbox Policy (P0 - Critical, Issue #592)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Executable Verification Criteria landed (#432): `execute_criterion_command` (`src/devops_cli/ai/review/review_environment.py:230-295`) runs model-authored commands in the reviewed repo for findings reaching `verification.py:1229`, confined only by a string allowlist and a process group. Its docstrings promise a "closed read-only allowlist" (`:193`) and a "bounded subprocess sandbox" (`:231`), yet the validator accepts reads anywhere (`cat /etc/passwd`, `find /`), writes via `python3 -c`, `python3 -m pip install`, and `pytest`, which runs the reviewed branch's code. Separately, container controls are literal SDK kwargs that disagree: `sandbox/engine.py:231-235` sets `read_only` and a `noexec` tmpfs; `docker/sandbox.py:266-270`, the runner behind `devops docker` and `devops test`, sets neither; `commands/sandbox.py:183-188` prints a third hand-typed copy. vibes renders one frozen policy with per-engine argv tests (`examples/ephemeral-container-sandbox/sandbox.py:59-76,266-303`).
  - *Deliverable*: PR1: adopt bubblewrap behind a narrow `HostSandbox` seam and route `execute_criterion_command` through it: `--unshare-all --new-session --die-with-parent --clearenv` with an explicit environment, `--ro-bind` of `/usr` (plus `lib`/`lib64`/`bin` symlinks) and the repo root, `--tmpfs /tmp`, no `$HOME`, `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` or session bus, so keyring, `gh` and `devops` credentials stay unreachable. Live tests: the child reads the repo, cannot list `/home` or write the repo, and `connect` to a non-loopback address fails. Add `bubblewrap` to `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` (installed today only incidentally) and document it as a host requirement. PR2: a frozen Pydantic `SandboxPolicy` in `sandbox/models.py` consumed by both `_build_create_kwargs` and `HostSandbox`, tested for identical security kwargs from both builders; delete the uncalled `SandboxNetworkConfig.to_docker_args` (`models.py:278`) and its tests. The drafted Sandbox Enforcement Audit (`devops sandbox audit`) reads DECLARED from this policy; whichever lands first deletes the dry-run literal. The harness `Shell` opts in once it has a production caller.
  - *Constraint*: Fail closed with a `CriterionExecutionResult` error, never unconfined, when `bwrap` is missing or namespaces are refused. `bwrap` network is all-or-nothing and Landlock filters TCP ports, not addresses, so criteria get no network and private-range or metadata egress denial stays in the proxy and NetworkPolicy tiers, never reported as host-enforced. Do not port `generate_bpf_c` or `generate_tetragon_policy`: BPF LSM is not in this kernel's boot LSM list, and the cluster integration is Falco (`k8s/security_stream.py`), which detects rather than denies. Denials are not SARIF: they have no source location. No `docker run`/`podman run` argv renderer: execution stays on the Docker SDK. No maintained Python Landlock binding exists; revisit when one does.
  - *Measured*: `uv run --no-sync python` over `validate_criteria_command` accepted `cat /etc/passwd`, `find / -name '*.pem'`, `python3 -c` with `write_text`, and `python3 -m pip install foo`. `bwrap --unshare-all --ro-bind /usr ... --ro-bind <ws> /work`: repo read succeeds, `ls /home` exits 2, write fails read-only, TCP connect errno 101. `zcat /proc/config.gz | grep -E '^CONFIG_(BPF_LSM|LSM)='`: `CONFIG_BPF_LSM=y`, `CONFIG_LSM` without `bpf`. PyPI JSON for `landlock` via `urllib.request`: latest 1.0.0.dev5 (pre-release).
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/kernel-enforced-lsm-sandbox-containment.md`, `examples/ephemeral-container-sandbox/sandbox.py`, `examples/ephemeral-container-sandbox/test_sandbox.py`, `examples/ebpf-lsm-kernel-gate/lsm_gate.py`, `examples/ebpf-lsm-kernel-gate/README.md`, `observations/systems/34-ebpf-lsm-kernel-gates-and-dynamic-containment-fuzzing.md`
- [ ] **Base-vs-Head Symbol Delta and Removed-Symbol Finding Exemption in Reviews (P1 - High, Issue #593)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: devops-cli measures structure at one revision only: per-function complexity in `security/complexity.py:141`, top-level symbols in `ai/ast_cache.py:20`, a regex Low/Medium/High bucket in `ai/analyze/outlines.py:163`. `devops ai analyze branch` knows each file's added/modified/deleted status (`commands/analyze.py:372-397`) but analyzes head content only, and the review pre-analysis leaves `change_type` at `existing` (`ai/review/pipeline.py:755`). The delivered construct validator invalidates a finding whose cited construct is absent from the head file (`ai/review/construct_validator.py:395`) and records it as a false positive (line 343), so a finding about a symbol the change removed is discarded: a probe finding "removal of `legacy_helper` breaks callers" came back INVALIDATED. How often real reviews hit this is unmeasured. vibes `repomap_cache.py:326-400` scores a refactor by cosine distance over eight AST features, with bands at 0.05 and 0.15 that `README.md:42-44` states without calibration data.
  - *Deliverable*: Three PRs. (1) In `_process_single_branch_file_meta` (`commands/analyze.py:134`), for modified Python files, parse `git show <base>:<path>` and head in memory and record on `FileAnalysisMeta` symbols added, removed and retained: top-level from `_extract_symbols_from_tree`, methods from `repomap._extract_class_methods` per `ClassDef` (`parse_file_symbols` reads only from disk). Review reads it through the cached analysis (`ai/analyze/cache.py:181`), not a second git walk. `_deterministic_pre_verification` (`ai/review/verification.py:1179`) passes the removed set to `validate_construct_location`, which labels such findings "cites removed symbol" and re-anchors them to the diff hunk instead of invalidating them. The review summary and session JSON show the delta and that count. (2) Per-function complexity before and after through a content-taking entry to `security/complexity.py`, shared with Cyclomatic Complexity Debt Reduction; a library backend is lizard (`analyze_source_code` takes text; 1.24.0, 2026-08-19), not radon (last release 6.0.1, 2023-03-26). (3) PR reviews, fetching `pull.base.sha` and `pull.head.sha`, since `_prepare_pr_content` has only the API diff (`ai/review/runner.py:1718-1719`). Port only the vibes symbol report.
  - *Constraint*: The vibes vector as specified is blind here: its two logarithmic dimensions dominate the unit vector and the five per-line ratios stay at or below 0.045, so every measured pair, unrelated modules included, scored PRESERVED. A gate on it would certify a rewrite as a refactor, worse than no gate. No scalar score, threshold, persona routing or auto-apply verdict ships in this task; any score needs its own task and a labelled corpus. The exemption must stay narrow: a symbol absent from both base and head still goes through invalidation.
  - *Measured*: vibes `EmbeddingDriftAuditor` on `git show` extracts (`python3 probe_vectors.py`): `commands/devcontainer.py` at `b7f2a2e7^` against unrelated `ai/gateway.py` at `bc510aac`, no symbol shared, scored `D_C = 0.004639`, PRESERVED; the real devcontainer change to `bc510aac` scored `D_C = 0.000024`; ln(1+lines) was `0.81–0.86` of every unit vector. lizard `analyze_source_code` on the same devcontainer pair (`python3 -c` over both extracts): functions above CCN 10 went from `10` to `11`.
  - *Source*: vibes `examples/valkey-l2-repomap-cache/README.md`, `examples/valkey-l2-repomap-cache/repomap_cache.py`, `examples/valkey-l2-repomap-cache/test_repomap_cache.py`, `observations/systems/04-content-addressed-two-tier-caching-and-embedding-drift-audits.md`

  - *Context & Rationale*: The ledger stores provider, `served_by`, model, tokens and cost per call (`src/devops_cli/ai/spend/ledger.py:24-40`) but not what the call was for: `request_type` holds transport labels (`src/devops_cli/ai/client/unified.py:449`, `src/devops_cli/ai/direct.py:124`) and `AIConfig.for_task` keeps no task name (`src/devops_cli/config/settings.py:408-429`). `LLMRouter` serves only the advisory `devops ai route` (`src/devops_cli/commands/ai.py:1350`); only tests call `GatewayRouter.resolve_model`. `devops ai harness offload` passes `frontier_tokens=0` and prints 100%, target met, with no model call (`src/devops_cli/commands/ai_harness.py:79-107`). `ai_spend_report` and `resource://ai/spend` pass `--group-by`, which `devops ai cost report` rejects (`src/devops_cli/ai/mcp/server.py:2831-2857`). Never record local AST work as spend. Step (3) lowers `devops ai cost report` totals and the #564 spend metrics for local calls; announce it. Any surviving selector keeps the per-role floors (`src/devops_cli/ai/capability.py:47`) and keeps security-critical work local. Port nothing from vibes' router or leaderboard.
  - *Measured*: `uv run python -c "from devops_cli.ai.harness.slots import TokenSavingsSummary as T; print(T.calculate(frontier_tokens=0, offloaded_tokens=14))"` prints `savings_percentage=100.0`. One million `qwen2.5-coder:7b` prompt tokens cost 0.15 USD in the ledger table (`uv run python -c "from devops_cli.ai.spend.pricing_data import DEFAULT_INDUSTRIAL_MODEL_PRICING as D; print(D['qwen2.5-coder:7b'].calculate_cost(1_000_000, 0))"`) and 0.0 in the router (`uv run python -c "from devops_cli.ai.router import LLMRouter; print(LLMRouter._estimate_cost(None, 'ollama', 'qwen2.5-coder:7b', 1_000_000))"`). `grep -rn "request_type" src --include='*.py' | grep -v "^src/devops_cli/ai/spend/\|request_type=" | wc -l` prints 0.

- [ ] **Measured Per-Deployment Capability Probes Replacing Parameter-Count Failover Floors (P2 - Medium, Issue #595)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: #430 added parameter-count floors (reasoning >=30B, coding >=7B) in `src/devops_cli/ai/capability.py`. They are inferred from the model name, and a name it cannot parse passes (`capability.py:105`). The gate runs in `GatewayRouter.trigger_failover` (`src/devops_cli/ai/gateway.py:509`) and in `ConstellationManager.failover`. `trigger_failover` is a manual CLI path that no live request uses. Live failover is LiteLLM's static `fallbacks` (`k8s/llm/gateway/configmap.yaml:136-138`). There, `devops-reasoning` (32B AWQ) falls to `devops-coder` (14B AWQ), the edge the 30B floor forbids. `devops-coder` falls to the 32B model, then to `devops-chat` (7B). Only the `chat` task uses these fallbacks. Reviews use `devops-review`, which has no fallback but shuffles across three models. `probe_backend` (`gateway.py:624`) checks health only. `gateway tune` measures capacity and cost only. vibes' 0%-vs-85%+ figures describe a 70B tier that is not deployed here, and they have not been measured.
  - *Deliverable*: Slice 1 is report-only: a capability pass in `devops ai gateway tune`. Extend the stdlib in-pod runner (`gateway_bench.py`) to return reply text. Its `run_level` keeps only `usage.completion_tokens` today. Send vibes' four probes (5-field JSON, 10-tool choice, deep recall, explicit prohibition) in the production prompted format. Do not use native `tools`/`response_format`, because reviews do not use them. Score on the CLI side with `pydantic_evals`, which the pinned `pydantic-ai` already installs: a `Dataset` of `Case`s, deterministic evaluators and `evaluate(repeat=N)`. Also record whether `response_repair.fix_llm_response` had to repair the reply. Persist results per deployment id in #477's store. Slice 2 comes with #478: floors read these records instead of parameter counts. The rendered config drops below-floor `fallbacks` targets and flags below-floor `devops-review` deployments. Slice 3 adds a client-side floor check on `served_by` (#474). Out of scope: task-class ordering driven by 429 responses. Local offload of mechanical sub-agent work already exists (`src/devops_cli/ai/harness/slots.py:133,363`).
  - *Constraint*: A gate only in `GatewayRouter` never touches real traffic. Probing a model group averages three models into one number. A 32K recall probe exceeds `devops-coder`'s `max_input_tokens: 12288` (`configmap.yaml:33`). Size it per deployment, as `deployment_prompt_tokens` (`gateway_tune.py:243`) already does. Four prompts are a smoke test and do not replace #475's whole-review baseline. Record pass counts over repeated temperature-0 trials. Report a deployment that was never probed as unmeasured rather than passing. Change no routing until slice 1 has run.
  - *Measured*: `grep -c 'model_name: devops-review' k8s/llm/gateway/configmap.yaml` gives 4 deployments. `awk '/model_name: devops-review/{f=1;next} /model_name:/{f=0} f && /model:/{print $2}' k8s/llm/gateway/configmap.yaml | sort -u | wc -l` gives 3 models. `grep -rnE 'tool-call-parser|enable-auto-tool-choice' k8s/llm | wc -l` gives 0. `grep -rln pydantic_evals src tests | wc -l` gives 0.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/devops-cli/20-model-failover-capability-cliffs-and-one-way-degradation-ratchets.md`, vibes `observations/devops-cli/06-rate-limits-and-anti-brittle-heuristics.md`, vibes `observations/devops-cli/05-harness-slots-and-subagent-offloading.md`
- [ ] **Recovery Transitions for Adaptive Knobs: Coalesced Decrease, Valkey Breaker, Failback (P2 - Medium, Issue #596)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: #430 added AIMD recovery to the embedding batch size, marked delivered in the "Capability-Gated Model Failover & AIMD Batch Recovery" item, but three one-way transitions remain. First, `_embed_ollama` fixes `chunk_batch_size` once per call (`src/devops_cli/ai/rag/embeddings.py:697`), and every slow batch in that call halves again (`:640`, `:653`) although none was sent at the reduced size. The increase fires on any batch at or under 2.0 s, with none of the cooldown or 0.5x margin vibes proposes (`embeddings.py:331-336`). Second, one Valkey error nulls the L2 client for the rest of the run (`embeddings.py:359,374`; `security/vulnerability_lookup.py:451,467`), pinned by `tests/test_security_threat_intel.py:228`, while `cache/tiered.py:202-222` retries and reconnects on every call during an outage (`valkey/pool.py:118-144`). Third, `trigger_failover` persists a `failover:*` route (`ai/gateway.py:487-526`) with no failback, and a healthy probe clears the breaker flag but keeps the route (`gateway.py:418`). #430 also left a backwards-compatible `_halve_batch_size` alias (`embeddings.py:327`).
  - *Deliverable*: Three small PRs. PR1 (before the v0.2.23 tag): pass the sent size into `_record_batch_latency` and the timeout path, skip a decrease when the sent size exceeds the current one, add a cooldown and a margin below the threshold before any increase, delete the alias, and migrate `tests/test_rag_embeddings.py:399-407`. Test it through `_embed_ollama` with a stubbed endpoint and two workers. PR2: adopt pybreaker behind one seam at the Valkey L2 boundary, route the engine, the threat-intel client and `TieredCache` through it, delete every `self._valkey = None`, and rewrite the pinning test to cover open, cooldown, half-open and closed. The v0.3.4 "Proportional API Rate Budgeting & GraphQL Circuit Breaker Guard" should reuse it. PR3: the route file feeds only `devops ai gateway status`/`routes`, while live fallback belongs to LiteLLM's `fallbacks` (`k8s/llm/gateway/configmap.yaml:118-137`). Make `status` report failover while any route is `failover:*`, and add `failover --reset` to restore default routes. List each knob, its trip, reset and test in the task file.
  - *Constraint*: A naive recovery is worse than the ratchet. Increasing on every healthy batch without a margin can climb straight back to the size that breached, and uncoalesced decreases then undo it in one call. Retrying Valkey on every call makes each call pay a connection attempt during an outage, which the disable avoids, so the breaker needs a real open state. Failback stays explicit: flipping routes on one healthy probe would call the local routes healthy regardless of LiteLLM, which owns traffic. A deliberate trip is recorded as one, not forced to recover.
  - *Measured*: `PYTHONPATH=src uv run --no-sync python probe/round_probe.py`: one `_embed_ollama` call with default parallelism and every batch slow drives the size from 32 to 1. `PYTHONPATH=src uv run --no-sync python probe/recovery_probe.py`: 32 healthy batches climb from 1 back to 32. `PYTHONPATH=src uv run --no-sync python probe/l2_probe.py`: after one transient Valkey read error, 0 of the next 100 reads reach L2.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/devops-cli/20-model-failover-capability-cliffs-and-one-way-degradation-ratchets.md`
- [ ] **Scaling Figures From Deployed Manifests and Discovered GPUs (`devops ai gateway scale`) (P2 - Medium, Issue #597)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `GatewayRouter.scale_vllm` and `scale_lightllm` (`src/devops_cli/ai/gateway.py:541-622`) multiply by a literal 24 GB per GPU and default tensor parallelism to literals. `--apply` only runs `kubectl scale --replicas` (`gateway.py:338`), yet both MCP tools claim to "Inspect or configure" (`ai/mcp/server.py:2764,2787`). The CLI drops `--tensor-parallel-size` for LightLLM and sends every other backend, `vllm-single` included, to the dual-GPU branch (`commands/ai_gateway.py:246-247`). vibes observation 20 §2c found the model id, TP=2 and 24 GB literals in the vLLM scaler. #457 has since moved vLLM identity into `DEFAULT_VLLM_*` (`config/defaults.py:86-90`), which is still a hand-written copy of the manifests. KV-cache discovery (`ai/gateway_bench.py:71`) and GPU discovery (`ai/gateway_tune.py:188-228`) already exist, but only `tune` uses them. The cluster runs no LightLLM or Portkey Deployment (`kubectl get deploy -n llm`).
  - *Deliverable*: PR1: remove `--tensor-parallel-size` from `scale` and both MCP tools. Reword the tool descriptions to "Inspect" and regenerate `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md` and the CLI reference. Reject an unknown `--backend`, add `vllm-single`, and report GPU memory from `deployment_gpus`, or "unknown", never 24. PR2: promote the manifest helpers in `tests/test_k8s_llm_gateway.py:41-55` into src, and parse container args with stdlib `argparse` `parse_known_args` so `--flag=value` works. Read live Deployments through `gateway_tune._kubectl` first, and use `kubectl kustomize k8s/llm` for tests and offline runs. The parser yields each profile's model, served name, TP, max-model-len, KV dtype and GPU limit. `scale` and #478 consume it, and `scale` stops reading `DEFAULT_VLLM_*`. A fixture test asserts that the report changes when a manifest's model or TP changes. PR3 (optional): a fit column comparing `engine_info` `kv_cache_tokens` with max-model-len. Point #453's Remaining note here. Whether to retire or deploy LightLLM and Portkey is a separate decision.
