LightLLM already has two: `gateway.py:88` routes it as `qwen2.5-coder:14b`, while its manifest serves Llama 3.3 70B. `DEFAULT_K8S_DIR` is relative to the working directory (`config/defaults.py:432`) and `k8s/` is not in the wheel, so files in the checkout do not show what is deployed. Do not gate on `estimate_hardware_requirements` (`security/aibom.py:73`). It puts the working 65536-token dual-GPU profile at 56.0 GB, against the 48 GB that `scale` reports, so a gate on either figure would reject a working deployment or pass a phantom one. Do not import vLLM's argument parser, which pulls in the whole engine. A weight estimate from safetensors headers would add `huggingface_hub`, which is absent from `uv.lock`, so it stays future work.
  - *Measured*: `CliRunner().invoke(ai_gateway.app, ["scale", "--backend", "lightllm", "--tensor-parallel-size", "4", "--format", "json"])` returns `tensor_parallel_size` 1. With `["scale", "--backend", "vllm", "--tensor-parallel-size", "4", "--format", "json"]` it returns `total_vram_gb` 96 and status `inspected`. `["scale", "--backend", "vllm-single", "--format", "json"]` returns the dual-GPU 32B model. `grep -cE '\b24\b' src/devops_cli/ai/gateway.py` returns 4.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/devops-cli/20-model-failover-capability-cliffs-and-one-way-degradation-ratchets.md`
- [ ] **Ollama Model Residency: Per-Pod Model Budget, Keep-Alive, ms/Token (P2 - Medium, Issue #598)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Ollama slot leases (`src/devops_cli/ai/client/network.py:169`) and pydantic-ai limiters (`src/devops_cli/ai/concurrency.py:31`) count per process; GitHub pacing is already cross-process (`src/devops_cli/github/rate_limiter.py:243`). `devops ai prewarm` sends `keep_alive: 1h` (`src/devops_cli/ai/client/ollama.py:60`). Chat and stream bodies omit it (`ollama.py:287`, `:463`), `devops ai chat` then calls through pydantic-ai (`src/devops_cli/commands/ai.py:1046`), and no manifest sets `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE`. A request without it resets expiry to the server default, so the lease is probably cut short; unchecked on a live server. Each of the 2 Ollama pods is routed 3 named models against `OLLAMA_MAX_LOADED_MODELS` 2 (`k8s/llm/ollama.yaml:66-67`); the direct embedding task adds another (#481). No ms/token metric exists, only a tokens/s span attribute on direct non-streaming Ollama calls (`src/devops_cli/ai/client/unified.py:69`), and nothing reads `/api/ps`. vibes `observations/systems/06` reports 45 to over 2,500 ms/token under eviction cycles, with no method; its claim that prewarm pins VRAM is contradicted above.
  - *Deliverable*: Measure first. Add a time-per-output-token histogram to `src/devops_cli/telemetry/instruments.py`, emitted beside the AI token counters (`src/devops_cli/ai/spend/ledger.py:485`) with the same backend labels, and unit and meaning taken from semconv `gen_ai.server.time_per_output_token`; add p50/p95 to #474's `devops ai cost report --by backend`. Wire or delete the unused `record_llm_metrics` (`src/devops_cli/telemetry/tracer.py:436`). Report `/api/ps` per pod (loaded models, `expires_at`, VRAM) from `devops ai gateway status`. Then check with `expires_at` whether a chat shortens a prewarm lease; if it does, send the configured keep-alive on direct Ollama bodies and the pydantic-ai path, and set `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` on the StatefulSet. Last, learn why the loaded-model limit is 2, then fit models per pod to it, with #481 choosing the embedding model. Document that `acquire_ollama_slot` is per-process by design.
  - *Constraint*: `keep_alive` is an idle timer; it does not stop eviction once the loaded-model limit is reached, so it cannot fix eviction cycles alone. A client-side cross-process semaphore adds a hidden queue ahead of Ollama's own (`OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL` 1), repeating #467, and host processes reach the pods directly (`hostPort`, `k8s/llm/ollama.yaml:95`) besides the gateway. `ValkeyTokenBucketRateLimiter` limits rate, not concurrency, and fails open (`src/devops_cli/valkey/rate_limiter.py:106`); wiring it in would report arbitration that is not happening.
  - *Measured*: `.venv/bin/python probe/xproc_slot.py process` (scratch probe, not in repo): 3 processes leasing one URL at `max_parallel=1` held it 3 at once; `thread` mode, 1. `.venv/bin/python probe/chat_payload.py` (scratch): chat body has no `keep_alive`. `awk '/model: ollama/{m=$2; getline; print $2, m}' k8s/llm/gateway/configmap.yaml | sort -u`: each of 2 Ollama backends routed 3 named models.
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/multi-agent-codebase-concurrency.md`; vibes `observations/systems/06-multi-agent-concurrency-shared-workspace-hazards-and-swarm-coordination.md`
- [ ] **Finish Reasons, Stream Completeness and Truncation Markers for Model Replies (P1 - High, Issue #599)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: No provider reads a finish reason, and the span hardcodes `gen_ai.response.finish_reasons` to `["stop"]` (`ai/client/unified.py:91`). A persona-review reply cut at its cap (`ai/review/runner.py:713`) is silently repaired, keeps its partial last finding, passes the validator and is cached (`unified.py:579`); failed validation resends the identical prompt (`unified.py:605-609`). Streaming: every provider reads `iter_lines()` (`ai/client/streaming.py:330`), the Ollama extractor never reports done (`:306-308`) and the Claude extractor handles only `content_block_delta` (`:269`). An early EOF or error frame is stored by `devops ai chat` (`commands/ai.py:954`) and billed as complete (`unified.py:816`). Repair enters through three functions (`ai/response_repair.py:51`, `ai/client/structured.py:57`, `core/serialization.py:23`); only `chat_structured`, which nothing calls, counts repairs (`structured.py:197,255`). vibes observation 15 shows retrying into the same token ceiling fails; vibes' RESP decoder returns `(None, 0)` until a whole frame arrives, tested over a frame-splitting socket.
  - *Deliverable*: (1) Byte-level characterization tests feeding `_consume_streaming_lines` from an httpx2 `MockTransport`: mid-line and mid-codepoint splits, raw U+2028 deltas, EOF without a terminal frame or mid-line, Ollama and Anthropic error frames, replacing whole-line `iter_lines` stubs. (2) Type `LLMResponse.finish_reason` as pydantic-ai `FinishReason` through `to_model_response`, mapping OpenAI `finish_reason`, Ollama `done_reason` and Anthropic `stop_reason` (`max_tokens` becomes `length`). Emit it on the chat and `gen_ai.stream` spans, never cache `length`, record it per spend-ledger row beside the backend (#474), and report truncation rate per stage and backend in `profile.json` (#473). (3) Read SSE with httpx-sse `EventSource`, pinned directly (already locked via `mcp`); split Ollama NDJSON from `iter_bytes()` on `\n` only. A stream completes only on `finish_reason`, `message_stop` or `done: true`; otherwise raise `AIClientError` with the provider message, which the chat loop already drops (`commands/ai.py:1106-1110`). (4) One `repair_json` seam over `json_repair.loads` returning `repaired`, counted per caller except `security/tflint.py:189`; with Lossless Structured Error Reflection, wire in or delete `chat_structured`. (5) Only if (2) shows a non-zero rate: pipeline-set `ReviewResult.truncated`, trailing-record drop on `length`, and re-chunking instead of identical retry. Budget sizing stays with #479.
  - *Constraint*: Truncation comes only from the provider reason; record unknown when absent. json-repair's log cannot stand in: a complete payload missing an inner `]` logs like a cut one. Read the reason before `str(res_obj)` (`runner.py:719`) drops it. Never drop a record for empty fields. Requiring `[DONE]` breaks Anthropic. The incompleteness error must not be an httpx2 transport exception, or the Ollama host loop (`ai/client/ollama.py:440-452`) would replay it on the next host mid-output. Keep yielding incrementally, enforcing `MAX_STREAM_BYTES` per event.
  - *Measured*: `.venv/bin/python probe/truncation_probe.py`: a review cut inside its final finding parsed to 5 findings, the last with description "de", and no truncation field. `.venv/bin/python probe/value_lens_log_fp_probe.py`: a trailing-comma repair logs 0 entries. `.venv/bin/python probe/split_frames.py`: all 4 early-end cases (no `[DONE]`, EOF mid-NDJSON line, Ollama and Anthropic error frames) return partial text without an exception. `grep -rn "\.chat_structured(" src | wc -l`: 0.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/devops-cli/15-llm-structured-output-repair-and-schema-reconciliation.md`, vibes `examples/valkey-l2-repomap-cache/repomap_cache.py`, vibes `examples/valkey-l2-repomap-cache/test_repomap_cache.py`
- [x] **Retract the False "Prompt Mutation Testing" Delivery Claim (P0 - Critical, Issue #600)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The "Next-Gen PydanticAI Agent Architecture" entry and `docs/RELEASE_NOTES.md:10` list "prompt mutation testing" as delivered, and `CHANGELOG.md:749-750` credits it to `devops ai prompt-eval`. No code in `src` or `tests` perturbs a prompt. The only thing shipped under that name was a "Prompt Mutation Benchmark" label, added in v0.2.4, over output that reported a constant accuracy of 1.0 (`src/devops_cli/ai/prompt_eval.py:7-12`). #385 fixed the command, and v0.2.22 removed the label. The claim lives on in `src/devops_cli/docs/compactor.py:25,35`, which writes it back whenever `devops docs compact` consolidates the v0.2 series. vibes' fuzzer applies five seeded classes (dilution, distraction, injection escape, truncation, complexity trap) and reports failures per class plus one resilience score.
  - *Deliverable*: Remove the phrase from both compactor bullet lists, the "Next-Gen PydanticAI Agent Architecture" entry and `RELEASE_NOTES.md:10`. Under `CHANGELOG.md:749-750`, add a correction that points to #385 instead of rewriting history. Add an assertion to `tests/test_docs_compactor.py` that the series bullets do not contain the phrase, so compaction cannot bring it back. None of this depends on a benchmark. Do not build a perturbation feature now. Instead, add one follow-on line to "Model-in-the-Loop Prompt Benchmarking": once its corpus replays, the candidate-prompt input may also accept seeded dilution variants of a persona system prompt. Each variant is sampled repeatedly and reported with its own figures, counting replies that needed `fix_llm_response` repair, and there is no exit threshold. That runs through the parent's harness. pydantic-evals is already installed with pydantic-ai 2.35.0, is unused, and offers `repeat=` and baseline reports. Do not port the vibes harness.
  - *Constraint*: Replacing the claim with a port of the vibes fuzzer would restore it in a new form. Its mock provider returns drifted code for any prompt containing `INJECTION_ESCAPE`, `SYSTEM OVERRIDE`, `COMPLEXITY_TRAP` or `monolithic` (`providers.py:248-254`), so its score measures vocabulary. It sends the pristine prompt as the system prompt and the mutated copy as the user turn (`fuzzer.py:508`), so truncation removes nothing the model sees. It draws one sample per class, so its 50.0 gate flips on a single reply. The other classes do not transfer. The distraction and complexity-trap snippets instruct code generation, but personas return findings. Truncation is a rule ablation, which is already the deliverable of "Generator-Side Rule Pruning". Injection inside reviewed content is covered by the open "Synthetic Prompt Adversaries for Injection Resistance".
  - *Measured*: On a scratch copy of the vibes fuzzer with its mock provider, `python3 fuzzer.py --json` reports resilience_score 80.0, and `python3 fuzzer.py --json -f AGENTS.md` reports 0.0 because this repository's AGENTS.md contains the trigger word (`grep -c monolithic AGENTS.md` = 2). At HEAD, `mutate_prompt(PERSONAS[p].system_prompt, TRUNCATION)` at intensity 0.5 drops between 0 and 3 of the 15 markers in `tests/test_personas.py:71`, depending on the persona.
  - *Source*: vibes `examples/prompt-mutation-fuzzer/fuzzer.py`, vibes `examples/prompt-mutation-fuzzer/providers.py`, vibes `examples/prompt-mutation-fuzzer/README.md`, vibes `docs/landscape/SURVEY.md`
- [ ] **One Verdict Writer, Cited Refutations and No Unverified Fix Claims (P0 - Critical, Issue #601)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Lines are on `release/v0.2.23`. `Finding` (`ai/review_schema.py:565`) accepts an INVALIDATED, reportable, verified record. A criteria-VERIFIED finding still goes to the model (`ai/review/verification.py:1512`), which overwrites it as `verified_by="llm"` (:1393); `ai/review/pipeline.py:2179-2193` restamps `llm` everywhere and drops `criteria_execution_results` and the relocated `location`. Debate-invalidated findings stay reportable (`ai/review/stages/adversarial_debate.py:57`), inflating `reported` (`ai/review/runner.py:1768`); `review verify --status UNVERIFIED` sets `verified=True` (`commands/review.py:968`); MCP `review_findings` sends an undefined `--mitigated` (`ai/mcp/server.py:171-174`). Requested line citations and mechanisms (`ai/tasks/verify_finding_system.md:7-8`) land in free-text `invalidation_reason` (`verification.py:1398`). `auto-fix` claims `test_verified=True` untested (`ai/review/auto_fix.py:77-85`); `apply-patch` prints "Staged patch", staging nothing (`ai/review/patching.py:58`); test-gen's `assert hasattr` (`ai/test_gen.py:72`) is labelled `SYNTHESIZED` (`config/defaults.py:536`).
  - *Deliverable*: (1) Delete `auto-fix` and `apply-patch` with modules, docs and tests; test-gen status becomes `UNEXECUTED`. Amend the v0.3.1 CEGIS item: pytest via `WorkloadSandboxEngine.exec` (`sandbox/engine.py:427`) under `timeout` and `exec_run(demux=True)` (`docker/engine.py:407`); a test fails unpatched, passes patched; counterexamples keyed by nodeid, exception type and message; candidates hashed by `ast.dump(include_attributes=False)`; vibes statuses (`tools/cegis_engine.py:110-118`) on stdlib; a pydantic-ai output validator raising `ModelRetry`; `git apply --check` on shadow worktrees. (2) Add `apply_verdict(finding, status, by, reason)`, writing status, verified, reportable, mitigated, verified_by and verified_at together, for criteria, deterministic checks (`deterministic:<check>`), the model, debate and `review verify`; `verified_by` becomes a `Literal`. Invariants: INVALIDATED is neither reportable nor verified; VERIFIED requires verified_by; UNVERIFIED has neither. MITIGATED stays reportable (`verification.py:1273-1279`). Executed verdicts are final; the pipeline copies the whole result back. Assert invariants at the findings.json write and in an every-writer test. MCP passes `--status` for all four states. (3) Add optional `citation_line` and `mitigating_mechanism` to `Finding` and `FeedbackRecord`, and per-adjudicator citation rates to `compute_verdict_distributions` (`review_schema.py:1313`). A missing or out-of-range cited line makes a refutation UNVERIFIED with a `verification_note`; an absent mechanism adds a note. Match identifier tokens, falling back to `ai/ast/engine.py`. Review personas run through devops-cli's own `PydanticAgent` and `LLMClient`, not `pydantic_ai.Agent`, so today's leak is `instrument_pydantic` at its default `record='all'`: validation spans carry the prompt and reply held in `CachedLLMResponse` and `AgentResponse`. Separately, every native `ai.llm.dispatch` span carries `gen_ai.prompt_preview` (ai/client/unified.py:372), every successful one `gen_ai.response_preview` (:92), exported unmasked (telemetry/tracer.py:871-888) with native export on by default (config/settings.py:263). `redact_text` (security/sanitizer.py:177) has no private-host pattern. vibes documents collector-side scrubbing its shipped collector config never implements.

Add `telemetry.capture_llm_content: bool = False`; while off, call `instrument_pydantic(record="failure")` and `instrument_pydantic_ai(include_content=False)` (the latter guards the exported `pydantic_ai` helpers, which have no runtime caller yet), pass `scrubbing=logfire.ScrubbingOptions(extra_patterns=PRIVATE_HOST_PATTERNS)` with that constant beside `_SECRET_PATTERNS`, and emit the two `gen_ai.*_preview` attributes only when capture is on. Leave `agent.prompt`/`agent.response`: only `devops telemetry test` sets them, with fixed strings. Cross-check pattern hits with `is_loopback_or_private_host` (core/validation.py:81), not a new IP check. Attribute names are unchanged.
  - *Constraint*: Logfire 5.1.0 exempts `gen_ai.input.messages`, `gen_ai.output.messages` and `pydantic_ai.all_messages` from scrubbing (`_internal/scrubbing.py:161-168`); once `pydantic_ai.Agent` gains a caller, `ScrubbingOptions` looks configured while message bodies pass intact, so `include_content=False` is the only switch. A broad hostname regex also matches dotted code identifiers and Logfire replaces the whole value on a match, so count hits over recorded spans before choosing patterns. A collector `redaction` processor is out of scope: traces export only to in-cluster Jaeger and logs only to `debug` (k8s/otel/values.yaml:91, :104), and it would never see Logfire's export.
  - *Measured*: Scratch probe mirroring logfire.py:206-217 around `PydanticAgent` + `LLMClient` on a stubbed driver: `python realpath_probe3.py late` gives 3 `pydantic.validate_python` spans carrying the marker in `input_data`/`result`; `P_SCRUB=1 python realpath_probe3.py late` (extra pattern) leaves 0 marker attributes; `P_FAIL=1 python realpath_probe3.py late` (`record="failure"`) emits 0 spans. `python scrub_probe.py default` (standalone `pydantic_ai.Agent`, extra pattern set): marker still in 2 exempt attributes.

The baseline reader (src/devops_cli/ai/review/category_metrics.py:114-124), `devops review stats` (src/devops_cli/commands/review.py:1122) and the feedback export (src/devops_cli/ai/review/exporter.py:139-147) each sum every session dir. Report sessions without profile.json as unkeyed. Never derive a key from directory names, and never drop these sessions.

With the current INVALIDATED session written in pipeline order it reads `50.0% (1/2)`, and with two byte-identical copies of the prior session added it reads `25.0% (1/4)`. In vibes, `ls docs/reliability/iterations/*.json | wc -l` gives 35 shards, and `md5sum docs/reliability/iterations/*.json | awk '{print $1}' | sort | uniq -c` shows 5 of them are byte-identical copies.

- [ ] **Order-Independent Review Finding Consolidation With a Permutation Test (P2 - Medium, Issue #608)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Follow-up to #512. `consolidate_duplicate_findings` (`src/devops_cli/ai/review_schema.py:1029`) is a greedy first-match fold over `_are_findings_duplicate` (`:866`), which is not transitive. `_merge_two_findings` (`:932`) keeps the first argument's title and location, joins personas and list fields in arrival order, takes the first non-empty `category` and `relocated_from`, and gives `description` and `fix` length ties to it. `sort_findings` (`:1048`) has no total tie-break. Worker completion order never reaches the fold (`ai/agents/pipeline.py:264`, `ai/review/pool.py:126`); model emission, persona and segment order do (`ai/review/pipeline.py:1838`, `:2405`; `ai/review/runner.py:457`; `ai/review/verification.py:1581` via `ReviewResult.merge`). Verification reads the folded list (`pipeline.py:2165`), so the kept anchor decides what gets checked. No test reorders the input. vibes' CRDT has the same blind spot: its one commutativity test swaps a single pair and passes.
  - *Deliverable*: First, a test that fails today: run the fold over `itertools.permutations` of four fixtures (the chained triple, a chain whose bridging finding sorts first, the #512 SQL-injection pair, the recorded `_watcher` session) and assert one distinct set of (title, location, severity, sorted personas) and, separately, one distinct sequence. Then pre-sort by a total key (parsed file and start line, lower-cased title, persona, full dump). Join a cluster only when `_are_findings_duplicate` holds against every original member (complete linkage), and merge after clustering. Keep the title and location with the highest severity, then the narrowest parsed span, then the key, so `_watcher` keeps `k8s/informer.py:44-112`. Sort list fields, pick scalars by the same key, and add location and title tie-breaks to `sort_findings`. Stdlib only. Measure by offline replay: capture the lists reaching the fold in one #415 corpus run and count those with more than one output under the old and new fold.

Connected components (networkx) merge the chain by definition. Conservatism comes from complete linkage; determinism from the order. A test comparing entry counts passes on `_watcher`, where the count is 3 in every order and only the anchor and output order move. Sorting raw location strings puts `a.py:10` before `a.py:9`, the same string comparison as the vibes CRDT `pos` sort. `FindingCluster.representative` (`security/normalization.py:237`) is no model: same tool and rank keep the first seen.
  - *Measured*: `uv run --no-sync python scratchpad/probe/consolidate_probe.py`: a chained triple gives 3 distinct outputs over 6 orders. `uv run --no-sync python scratchpad/probe/l302_perm_probe.py`: the #512 pair merges into 2 different findings over 2 orders. `uv run --no-sync python scratchpad/probe/l302_perm_probe2.py`: `_watcher` gives 12 sequences but 2 entry sets over 24 orders. `uv run --no-sync python scratchpad/probe/v436_value_probe.py`: bridging-first, the pre-sorted fold leaves 1 entry; complete linkage leaves 2.
  - *Source*: vibes `examples/ast-crdt-collaborative-editor/ast_crdt.py`, vibes `examples/ast-crdt-collaborative-editor/test_ast_crdt.py`, vibes `patterns/ast-crdts-for-real-time-agent-collaboration.md`, vibes `observations/systems/37-real-time-ast-crdts-and-concurrent-multi-agent-swarms.md`

### Reactive Workstation Command Center, Interactive TUI & Unified Operations Hub (v0.2.24 - Scheduled)
- [ ] **Reactive Multi-Workspace Textual TUI Architecture & Master-Detail Navigation (`devops dashboard`, `devops tui`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Modernizes the basic Textual dashboard from static read-only tables into a reactive, multi-workspace workstation command center with non-blocking async workers (`work()`), real-time push events, and master-detail ergonomic split screens (navigation tree/list on left, contextual detail inspector with YAML/logs/markdown on right).
  - *Unified Workspaces*: Seamlessly tabs between 7 domain workspaces: (1) `PR & Git Lifecycle`, (2) `K8s & Minikube`, (3) `Docker & Sandboxes`, (4) `SecOps & Vault`, (5) `GitOps & ArgoCD`, (6) `AI Constellation & Memory`, and (7) `Telemetry & Loki Logs`.
  - *Stateful Navigation*: Preserves active table selections, scroll positions, and filter queries across tab switches with URL-like hash routing.
- [ ] **Interactive GitHub Lifecycle, PR Monitor & Projects v2 Kanban Hub (`tab-github`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Embeds full GitHub engineering workflow visibility directly inside the terminal.
  - *Chronological FIFO PR Queue*: Live pull request queue prioritized from oldest to newest with status badges (CI check conclusion, Copilot review settling indicator, unresolved review threads counter). Selecting a PR opens an inspector pane with commit diff summary and failing check logs.
  - *Interactive Actions*: Instant keybinding triggers to run readiness checks (`p` -> `devops pr check-readiness`), view failing run logs (`v` -> `devops gh runs view`), or open PR in browser (`o`).
  - *Projects v2 Mini-Kanban*: Responsive columnar view showing active milestone issues across `Backlog`, `Ready`, `In Progress`, `In Review`, and `Done` with taxonomy labels.
  - *API Quota Gauge*: Live visual token-bucket meter displaying remaining GitHub REST/GraphQL rate limits and reset countdown (`devops gh rate-limit`).
- [ ] **Cloud-Native Cluster Runtime, Pod Inspector & Live Log Streamer (`tab-k8s`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Transforms the primitive 10-pod table into a live, interactive Kubernetes console.
  - *Multi-Namespace Filtering*: Namespace selector dropdown, search query bar (`/`), and status filters (`Running`, `Pending`, `CrashLoopBackOff`, `Failed`).
  - *Integrated Log Streamer Drawer*: Embedded `RichLog` drawer streaming live container logs with auto-scroll and follow mode (`l`), backed by non-blocking Stern/Kubernetes log tailing.
  - *Cluster Health & Minikube Overview*: Node resource allocation (CPU/Memory gauges), Minikube GPU status badge (`Active` / `Fallback`), and service ingress URL table (`devops k8s configure-urls`).
  - *Port-Forward Daemon Manager*: Real-time list of active background port forwards (`devops k8s port-forward --status`) with one-key start and terminate controls.
  - *Interactive Pod Actions*: Restart pod (`r`), stream logs (`l`), view manifest YAML (`y`), or inspect pod events (`e`).
- [ ] **Docker Containers & Multi-Tier Workload Sandbox Console (`tab-docker`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Comprehensive workstation container and sandbox observability hub.
  - *Multi-Tier Sandbox Inspector*: Dedicated view of running Docker and Kubernetes sandboxes (`devops sandbox status`) showing active network modes (`isolated`, `sandbox_namespace`, `public_whitelist`, `local_whitelist`), cgroup resource limits, and health probe states.
  - *Container Performance Metrics*: Live CPU %, memory RSS vs limit, block I/O, and network throughput sparklines.
  - *Interactive Sandbox Controls*: Terminate sandbox (`k`), stream sandbox diagnostic logs (`l`), trigger dynamic health probe (`p`), or prune dangling volumes and images.
- [ ] **Unified SecOps, Compliance & HashiCorp Vault Command Center (`tab-secops`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Consolidates all static and dynamic security scanners into an actionable vulnerability triage center.
  - *Scanner Severity Dashboard*: Aggregated threat summary cards (Trivy CVEs by severity, Gitleaks detected secrets, Semgrep SAST alerts, Checkov IaC failed checks). Selecting an alert displays CVE details, affected line numbers, CVSS scores, and remediation hints.
  - *Automated Remediation Queue*: Highlights vulnerabilities resolvable via `devops scan fix` with one-key preview of auto-remediation patches.
  - *HashiCorp Vault Secret Broker Status*: Visual broker health indicator, seal state, token lease TTL progress bar, and OS Keyring synchronization status (`devops vault status`).
- [ ] **GitOps Fleet, ArgoCD Rollouts & Cloud Cost Monitor (`tab-gitops`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Brings continuous deployment and cloud infrastructure FinOps into situational awareness.
  - *ArgoCD Application Fleet*: Tree view of deployed GitOps applications showing sync status (`Synced`, `OutOfSync`) and health status (`Healthy`, `Progressing`, `Degraded`). One-key app synchronization trigger (`s` -> `devops argo cd apps sync`).
  - *Argo Rollout Canary Visualizer*: Live step-by-step rollout progression with canary traffic weight and automated metric analysis results.
  - *IaC Drift & Infracost Spend Tracker*: OpenTofu workspace state overview, drift status badge, and monthly cloud cost forecast summary (`devops tf cost`).
- [ ] **AI Constellation Topology, RAG Health & Review Findings Studio (`tab-ai`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Deep inspection of local and distributed AI inference tiers and knowledge bases.
  - *Ollama Constellation Topology*: Node health matrix, active model weights, GPU VRAM allocation gauges, and failover circuit breaker status.
  - *RAG Vector Tier Health*: Qdrant collection size, indexed document count, semantic drift score, and Valkey L2 embedding cache hit ratio sparkline.
  - *Review Findings Studio*: Interactive hierarchical tree of AI code review findings with severity badges, collapsible rationale scratchpads, file diff snippet previews, and one-key finding status transitions (`MITIGATED`, `INVALIDATED`).
- [ ] **Logs Reach Loki Without Their Pod or Namespace (P1 - High, Issue #548)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: fluent-bit's Kubernetes filter cannot reach the API server (`kube api upstream connection error`). In an hour Loki held two streams: one pod labelled, and about 7,700 lines from every other pod with no namespace, pod or container. Logs cannot be queried per workload, which the TUI's LogQL streamer depends on. The logging NetworkPolicy is the first suspect.
  - *Deliverable*: fluent-bit reaches the API server and every stream carries namespace, pod and container; a check or alert catches the filter failing again.
- [ ] **Alerts Fire Into Nothing: No Alertmanager, and k3s False Positives (P1 - High, Issue #549)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The stack runs with `alertmanager.enabled: false`; 12 alerts fire with no receiver, and Grafana's Alertmanager datasource points at nothing. `KubeControllerManagerDown`, `KubeSchedulerDown` and `KubeProxyDown` are false: k3s runs those components in its server process, and their ServiceMonitors find no targets. A node offline for maintenance keeps node, kubelet and DaemonSet alerts firing.
  - *Deliverable*: An Alertmanager with a receiver chosen with the maintainer; k3s components scraped from k3s's embedded metrics, or their monitors and rules disabled; a maintenance silence or inhibition for planned node downtime.
- [ ] **Metrics and Logs Are Kept for Two Hours (P2 - Medium, Issue #550)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Prometheus keeps 2 hours (512 MB) and Loki 2 hours, leaving no history for capacity and routing analysis, #477's real-traffic cost, or looking back at an incident.
  - *Deliverable*: Retention sized to available storage, e.g. two weeks of metrics and a week of logs on persistent volumes, with an alert before a volume fills.
- [ ] **Metrics for the Rest of the Platform (P2 - Medium, Issue #551)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Beyond Kubernetes, Prometheus scrapes only Qdrant and the OpenTelemetry collector. ArgoCD, cloudflared, Valkey, the image registry, squid, and fluent-bit, Loki and Jaeger themselves run unmonitored.
  - *Deliverable*: A ServiceMonitor for each component's own metrics endpoint (ArgoCD, cloudflared, the registry, fluent-bit, Loki, Jaeger), a maintained exporter otherwise (Valkey, squid), and dashboards or alerts where they matter: ArgoCD sync and health, tunnel health, cache and proxy errors.
- [ ] **Centralized Loki LogQL Streamer, Prometheus Sparklines & Trace Waterfalls (`tab-telemetry`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Unifies the complete observability triad directly within the terminal UI.
  - *Loki Centralized LogQL Query Console*: Embedded LogQL query input bar with syntax highlighting and live streaming log console, querying cluster-wide Fluent Bit / Loki logs without leaving the TUI.
  - *Prometheus Performance Sparklines*: Terminal ASCII sparklines and gauges rendering real-time command execution latencies, LLM token throughput (tokens/sec), and cache hit ratios.
  - *Trace Waterfall Modal*: Visual breakdown of recent OpenTelemetry distributed traces and multi-persona review spans with service latency waterfalls.
- [ ] **Comprehensive DevOps CLI Grafana Observability Dashboard Suite & GitOps Provisioner (`devops grafana dashboards sync`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: While the Textual TUI provides immediate interactive terminal visibility, long-term trend analysis, multi-workstation telemetry aggregation, and cluster-wide observability require enterprise-grade Grafana dashboards backed by Prometheus, Loki, and Tempo/Jaeger.
  - *Unified Workstation & Agentic Observability Portfolio*:
    - **DevOps Workstation CLI & Agent Telemetry (`dashboards/devops-cli.json`)**: Subcommand invocation frequency, p50/p95/p99 execution latency histograms, exit code distributions, subagent task durations, and OpenTelemetry span waterfalls.
    - **AI Constellation & Priority Slot Leasing (`dashboards/ai-constellation.json`)**: Multi-node Ollama slot leasing concurrency, active leases vs. parallel limits, token generation throughput (tokens/sec), time-to-first-token (TTFT), vector cache hit ratios, and priority queue lengths (`high`, `normal`, `as_available`).
    - **Multi-Persona Code Review & Findings Quality (`dashboards/ai-review.json`)**: Review volume segmented by classification context (`documentation`, `configuration`, `code`), persona finding distributions (`devsecops`, `architect`, `qa`, `pm`, `auditor`), severity heatmaps, finding mitigation latency, and false-positive invalidation rates.
    - **GitHub Projects v2, PR Queue & API Quota (`dashboards/github-agentic.json`)**: FIFO pull request queue depth, PR readiness turnaround, Copilot review settling latency, and real-time REST/GraphQL token-bucket consumption gauges.
    - **Centralized Logging & Incident Triage (`dashboards/loki-incident-triage.json`)**: Loki LogQL error stream panels, trace-to-log correlation via trace ID exemplars, and SIEM audit logs.
  - *Automated K8s Sidecar & ConfigMap GitOps Provisioning*: Packages bundled dashboards into Kubernetes ConfigMaps labeled with `grafana_dashboard: "1"`, enabling automatic, zero-restart discovery and live reloads via the Grafana dashboard sidecar (`k8s/monitoring/prometheus-values.yaml`).
  - *Declarative Dashboard Linter & Exporter (`devops grafana dashboards validate`) (P1 - High)*: Automated schema validator and exporter verifying Grafana 10+ panel schema compliance, datasource parameterization (`${DS_PROMETHEUS}`, `${DS_LOKI}`), and uid idempotency across all committed dashboard definitions.
- [ ] **Turnkey Kubernetes Stacks Grafana Observability Dashboard Suite (`k8s/monitoring/dashboards/`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Each Kubernetes stack in `k8s/` operates as a dedicated microservice tier with specialized telemetry requirements. Providing turnkey, battle-tested Grafana dashboards pre-configured with PromQL queries, SLO alerts, and GitOps sidecar discovery ensures instant cluster observability.
  - *Complete Kubernetes Stack Dashboard Portfolio*:
    1. **LLM Inference Fleet & Distributed Model Mesh (`k8s-llm-mesh.json`)**: Token generation throughput (tok/sec), Time-to-First-Token (TTFT), prompt processing latency, GPU KV cache memory utilization, tensor parallelism synchronization latency, request concurrency, queue depth, AWQ vs. TokenAttention throughput comparison across Ollama, LiteLLM, Portkey, vLLM, and LightLLM.
    2. **AI Gateway & Failover Router (`k8s-ai-gateway.json`)**: LiteLLM and Portkey routing metrics, virtual model alias distributions (`devops-chat`, `devops-coder`, `devops-reasoning`, `devops-embedding`), routing distribution, circuit breaker trips, failover transitions, Valkey L2 cache hit ratios, and HTTP status codes (200, 429, 502, 503).
    3. **ArgoCD GitOps & Fleet Orchestration (`k8s-argocd-gitops.json`)**: Application sync status (`Synced`, `OutOfSync`), sync duration histograms, controller reconciliation phase rates, git repository fetch latency and caching, application health states (`Healthy`, `Progressing`, `Degraded`), resource count per app, auto-sync and prune events, ArgoCD API server request latency and error rates.
    4. **Cluster CoreDNS Resolution (`k8s-coredns.json`)**: Query latency percentiles (p50/p95/p99), request rates by protocol (UDP/TCP) and query type (A, AAAA, SRV, PTR), response code distributions (`NOERROR`, `NXDOMAIN`, `SERVFAIL`, `REFUSED`), DNS cache hit/miss ratios, upstream forwarder latency, plugin processing durations.
    5. **NVIDIA GPU Acceleration & Hardware (`k8s-gpu-hardware.json`)**: Real-time GPU compute utilization (%), streaming multiprocessor (SM) occupancy, GPU memory (VRAM) allocated vs. total, GPU temperature and thermal throttling states, power draw (watts) vs. TDP limits, PCIe bus throughput, GPU Feature Discovery (GFD) label allocation and pod-to-GPU binding.
    6. **Centralized Logging & Loki (`k8s-loki-logging.json`)**: Ingestion stream rates and bandwidth (MB/sec), chunk compression ratios, memory chunk pool utilization, query evaluation latency and throughput, storage write rates to volume storage, Promtail scrape targets, log line drops, error frequency breakdown by namespace (`llm`, `argocd`, `monitoring`, `squid`, `registry`).
    7. **Prometheus Monitoring & Storage Engine (`k8s-prometheus-engine.json`)**: Target scraping latency and failure rates, active time-series count, head chunk allocation and TSDB block compaction durations, WAL writes and memory buffers, query execution durations, Alertmanager notification latency, rule group evaluation timing and alerting state tracking.
    8. **OpenTelemetry Collector & Distributed Tracing (`k8s-otel-tracing.json`)**: OTel receiver span and metric ingestion rates, pipeline processor queue depth and memory ballast, exporter delivery latencies and HTTP retry rates, dropped span/metric counters, Jaeger/Tempo trace storage throughput, trace-to-metric exemplar links, sampling decision efficiency.
    9. **Local OCI Container Registry (`k8s-registry.json`)**: Image push and pull throughput (MB/sec), image manifest and blob layer request rates, HTTP response codes (200, 201, 404, 500), storage volume capacity utilization, garbage collection runtime and reclaimed storage, catalog size and repository count.
    10. **Squid Forward Proxy & Egress Perimeter (`k8s-squid-egress.json`)**: HTTP and HTTPS CONNECT egress request volume, client connection concurrency, bandwidth consumption (egress/ingress MB/sec), domain access classification (whitelisted vs. blocked), cache hit ratios for model weights and packages, DNS lookup latency via Squid helper, proxy response codes.
  - *GitOps Provisioning & Synchronization*: Integrated into `devops grafana dashboards sync`, packaging each stack dashboard as a labeled Kubernetes ConfigMap in `k8s/monitoring/dashboards/` for auto-reload by the Grafana sidecar.
- [ ] **Universal Terminal Command Palette & Fuzzy Action Launcher (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: High-velocity keyboard workflow allowing developers to execute any DevOps CLI command without exiting the TUI.
  - *Modal Launcher (`Ctrl+P` / `:`)*: Fast fuzzy-search command palette listing all CLI subcommands (`ci run`, `scan trivy`, `release status`, `argo sync`, `vault sync`, `sandbox iterate`).
  - *Non-Blocking Command Output Drawer*: Executes commands in a non-blocking background thread, streaming output directly into an ephemeral sliding terminal drawer.
- [ ] **FastMCP TUI Management Tools & Dynamic Dashboard Resources (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Exposes FastMCP tools (`dashboard_launch`, `dashboard_status`, `dashboard_switch_tab`) and dynamic system resources (`resource://dashboard/status`, `resource://dashboard/k8s`, `resource://dashboard/github`, `resource://dashboard/secops`) enabling AI assistants to query dashboard state, monitor workstation telemetry, and trigger UI focus programmatically.
- [ ] **Cluster Deployment Health Diagnosis (`devops k8s doctor`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: A review of live cluster logs surfaced failures that are individually obvious and collectively invisible, because nothing correlates them. A node left `NotReady,SchedulingDisabled` had `metrics-server` retrying it every 12 seconds — six restarts and a log filled entirely with one unreachable host — while a `PersistentVolume` delete had been timing out for three days behind a helper pod stuck `Terminating`. Each is a one-line symptom; none is reported anywhere an operator looks.
  - *Deliverable*: A diagnostic that correlates node readiness, pod restart counts, `Warning` events and container log error rates into a ranked list of deployment problems, naming the resource, the likely cause and the remediation. Ships as a command and as a dashboard panel.
  - *Constraint*: It must distinguish a cluster fault from a workload fault. Reporting `metrics-server` as unhealthy when the actual fault is a cordoned node sends the operator to the wrong place — which is precisely what the raw logs already do.
- [ ] **Startup Dependency Ordering for the Telemetry Stack (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The OpenTelemetry collector starts before Jaeger is accepting connections and logs a burst of `connection refused` against `jaeger.otel.svc.cluster.local:4317` until it recovers. The warnings are harmless and self-resolving, which is the problem: they are indistinguishable from a real outage when read later, and they were the first thing found when reviewing these logs.
  - *Deliverable*: Express the dependency rather than retrying into the log — a readiness gate on the collector, or an explicit backoff that reports once rather than per attempt.
- [ ] **Local-Path Provisioner Volume Reclaim Failures (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `VolumeFailedDelete` with `create process timeout after 120 seconds` leaves the helper pod `Terminating` indefinitely — three days, in the observed case — and the `PersistentVolume` is never reclaimed. Storage leaks silently and the only evidence is an event nobody is watching.
  - *Constraint*: Reclamation deletes data. Any automated remediation must confirm the volume is genuinely orphaned rather than merely slow to detach, so the first deliverable is detection and reporting, not deletion.
- [ ] **Output Format Adoption Across the Command Surface (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: v0.2.22 built the join point -- `emit_serialized` renders a payload in any supported format, YAML output is portable rather than Python-tagged, and the console wrapper no longer defeats Rich's colour negotiation. Adoption is the remainder. Of 306 registered commands, 53 expose `--json`, 17 expose `--format`, and the 10 that route through the join point get YAML at no cost. The rest each emit their own `json.dumps`, so a new format still means editing every one of them.
  - *Deliverable*: Move the remaining `--json` commands onto the join point, then add `--format` where a command has a serialisable result.
  - *Constraint*: "100% of subcommands" is the wrong target and was the original framing. An interactive chat, a port-forward daemon and a TUI have no result to serialise, and giving them a `--format` flag that emits an empty object is worse than not offering one. The deliverable is every command whose output is a value, which has to be enumerated rather than assumed.
- [ ] **Reachable `--dry-run` Previews for Delegated Commands (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Every command group is registered lazily as a module path, and the lazy proxy short-circuits under dry-run: it prints the command line you typed and returns without loading the target module. So no command in the CLI ever reaches its own dry-run branch. 160 of 306 registered commands contain one — detailed previews of exactly what would be written — and none of them is reachable from the command line. `devops k8s configure-urls --dry-run` had eight hardcoded NodePort URLs pointing at a minikube node nobody in this project uses, and the reason it went unnoticed is that the block could not be reached.
  - *Deliverable*: Delegate under dry-run so a command renders its own preview, with the generic line kept for commands that have none.
  - *Constraint*: The short-circuit is load-bearing. 146 registered commands have no dry-run branch, and delegating to those would execute them for real under a flag that promises the opposite. Each has to be classified — read-only, or needing a branch — before the switch flips, so the audit is the work and the switch is the last step.
- [ ] **In-Flight Work, PR Stagnation & Blocker Radar (`devops gh pm inflight`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Continuous surveillance of active in-flight work across the repository to eliminate PR starvation, review stagnation, and merge conflict decay.
  - *FIFO PR Queue Surveillance*: Enforces strict chronological (oldest to newest / FIFO) PR processing and surfaces older open PRs that are being starved or blocked by newer work.
  - *Stagnation & Bottleneck Detection*: Flags PRs with unresolved Copilot/peer review threads, failing CI checks, or unassigned reviewers exceeding configurable latency thresholds (e.g. >24h); emits actionable remediation nudges.
- [ ] **Defect PRs Carry Their Hardening: Readiness Blocker and Post-Merge Audit (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The rule is written down and nothing reads it. `AGENTS.md:98-100` requires defect fixes to add AGENTS.md guardrails when the cause cannot be fixed at source; `docs/SELF_IMPROVEMENT.md:58` says every unexpected failure 'is immediately codified into AGENTS.md'. No workflow references AGENTS.md (`grep -rln AGENTS.md .github/workflows` exits 1) and `devops pr check-readiness` (`src/devops_cli/commands/pr.py:1405-1406`) reads neither labels nor files. The taxonomy leaks: PR #485 carries `type/security` plus `scope/infra`, undefined in `.github/labels.yml`; `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml:3-4` mandates a `fix(<scope>):` title. vibes' `.github/workflows/recursive-hardening.yml` first real run saw 0 files instead of 12 and accused an author who had hardened (`tests/test_workflow_contracts.py:1-14, 249-263`; `recursive-hardening.yml:51`).
  - *Deliverable*: Slice 1 measures: `devops pr hardening-report [--pr N]` lists merged defect PRs (`type/bug`, `type/security`, or a `fix(`/`fix:` title) and reports per PR which declared surfaces gained lines (`AGENTS.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md`, `src/devops_cli/ai/tasks/*.md`, `src/devops_cli/ai/knowledge_base/`, `common_hallucinations.json`) from `gh pr view --json files`, ignoring labels absent from `labels.yml`, plus fix-titled PRs lacking `type/bug`; it also rewords `docs/SELF_IMPROVEMENT.md:58` to name the surfaces actually used. Slice 2, behind `--require-hardening`: `_check_hardening_blocker` in `_evaluate_pr_blockers` (`pr.py:1304`) requires additions to a declared surface, reporting 'touched but added nothing' separately from 'not touched'; default-on with `--allow-unhardened` only after slice 1 covers the full merged-defect population. Slice 3: a `pull_request: types: [closed]` job gated on `merged == true` plus the two labels, running `hardening-report --pr $PR_NUMBER --fail-on-missing` and opening an issue on failure via `create_repository_issue` (`src/devops_cli/github/issues.py:245`) with `labels.yml`-defined labels. Prerequisite: a new `tests/test_workflow_contracts.py` extending `tests/test_ci.py:537-628` to resolve each `uv run devops ...` in `run:` against the Typer app, assert triggers, and fail on a GitHub Actions expression inside `run:` (`update-prs.yml:74-86` violates this). Port only vibes' decisions: label gating (`recursive-hardening.yml:34`), two-way verdict (`tools/project_tooling.py:200-221`), empty file list is an error (`recursive-hardening.yml:74-76`), skip-not-fail for fork tokens (`pr-sentinel.yml:69-74`); not its range walk or `count_guardrail_lines` (`project_tooling.py:186`), since the files endpoint is merge-strategy independent.
  - *Constraint*: An AGENTS.md-only check accuses all 32 merged `type/bug` PRs though 25 wrote their lesson into `docs/ROADMAP.md`, so the surface set must be declared and the verdict must name it; `tests/` and `docs/agent/tasks/` (32 and 31 of 32) are vacuous. A `type/bug`-only gate misses the 29 of the last 100 merged PRs that are fix-titled without it. A job that never fires is indistinguishable from one that passes; `ci.yml:65-66` actionlint checks syntax only. Commit to slice 1 and the contract test first; slices 2-3 depend on its result.
  - *Measured*: `gh pr list --repo dan-petty/devops-cli --state merged --label type/bug --limit 100 --json number --jq length` = 32. Iterating `gh pr view <n> --json files` over those 32 and counting paths with `additions > 0`: `AGENTS.md` 0, `docs/ROADMAP.md` 25 (the `tests/` and `docs/agent/tasks/` counts in Constraint come from the same iteration). `gh pr list --state merged --limit 100 --json number,title,labels` filtered with jq for `fix`-prefixed titles lacking `type/bug` = 29.
  - *Source*: vibes `.github/workflows/recursive-hardening.yml`, `.github/workflows/autonomous-triage.yml`, `.github/workflows/pr-sentinel.yml`, `tools/project_tooling.py`, `tests/test_workflow_contracts.py`, `tests/test_project_tooling.py`, `patterns/root-cause-hardening.md`, `AGENTS.md`
- [ ] **Sandbox Enforcement Audit: Declared vs Engine vs Enforced (`devops sandbox audit`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Both sandbox runners declare hardening as Docker SDK kwargs — `cap_drop ALL`, `no-new-privileges`, `pids_limit 256`, `network_mode none` (`src/devops_cli/sandbox/engine.py:227-239`, `src/devops_cli/docker/sandbox.py:260-273`); `read_only` and a `noexec` tmpfs exist only in the deploy engine (`engine.py:234-235`; the workload runner's `read_only` only sets the bind mode, `docker/sandbox.py:254,264`). Tests check those kwargs at three sites (`tests/test_sandbox_lifecycle.py:311-315`, `tests/test_workload_sandbox.py:78-80,167-179`), only against a mocked SDK. Nothing queries the engine: `DockerEngineService` (`src/devops_cli/docker/engine.py:197`) has no `SecurityOptions` accessor, and the two dry-run summaries disagree (`commands/sandbox.py:183-188`, `docker/sandbox.py:155-172`). vibes hit this failure: `CISPolicyAuditor` scored the policy 100.0/100, 8/8, while the engine enforced 0 of 8; the fix scored the engine about to run, named each unapplied control, and tested that the scores differ (`examples/ephemeral-container-sandbox/sandbox.py:104,222-262`, `test_sandbox.py:206-231`, `observations/systems/19-self-consistency-is-not-conformance.md:27,115,132`).
  - *Deliverable*: A `devops sandbox audit` command and `sandbox_audit` MCP tool beside `sandbox_deploy` (`src/devops_cli/ai/mcp/server.py:1289,1336`), with per-control columns DECLARED, ENGINE, ENFORCED. Three sequential PRs. PR1: DECLARED derives from the production `_build_create_kwargs` (`sandbox/engine.py:207`, `docker/sandbox.py:252-273`); both dry-runs read those kwargs, replacing the literal at `commands/sandbox.py:183-188`. ENGINE comes from a new `DockerEngineService.security_options()` calling `client.info()['SecurityOptions']` via the `docker` SDK (`pyproject.toml:24`); an unreachable daemon reports 0 enforced plus the reason. PR2: `--probe` runs the runner's identical kwargs on `DEFAULT_SANDBOX_IMAGE` (`config/defaults.py:641`), recording EPERM/ENOSYS/success for `io_uring_setup`, `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)`, `ptrace`, `mount`, non-loopback `connect`, writes outside `/tmp`/`/workspace`, forks past `pids_limit`; an unprobed control reads `not probed`, never enforced. A mocked daemon reporting `profile=unconfined` must lower ENFORCED with DECLARED constant. PR3: a `seccomp_profile` field on `SandboxDeployConfig` (`sandbox/models.py:360`) and `WorkloadSandboxConfig` (`docker/sandbox.py:46`) reading an OCI profile, rejecting a missing or `SCMP_ACT_ALLOW` `defaultAction`, passing `security_opt: ["seccomp=<profile JSON>"]`. Do not port vibes `tools/seccomp_synthesizer.py`: the engine's builtin profile is the maintained default-deny artifact (Measured); tighter profiles come from a maintained tracer (`oci-seccomp-bpf-hook`), audit-confirmed. The harness shell tool (`ai/harness/shell.py:272`, process-group isolation only) is listed enforcing no container control.
  - *Constraint*: Reading kwargs and printing ENFORCED is the vibes 8/8 defect renamed; ENFORCED must come from a run against the kernel or say it did not run. Probe under the same network mode: `socket()` succeeded inside `--network none` (Measured); test egress by reachability, not syscall. PR1–PR2 land before v0.3.2's Automated Triage & Sandbox Repro Validator, the first to run issue-supplied code here; PR3 may slip to v0.3.4 if milestone load forces a cut. The user's host is rootless Podman; test ENGINE against both option sets via mocked `info()`.
  - *Measured*: Re-measured this session: `docker info` reported server version 29.8.1 with security options `name=seccomp,profile=builtin` and `name=cgroupns`, no `rootless`. `grep -c 'def test_'` → 37 (`tests/test_sandbox_lifecycle.py`) + 9 (`tests/test_workload_sandbox.py`). Run A, `docker run --rm --pull=never --network none --cap-drop ALL --security-opt no-new-privileges:true --pids-limit 256 --read-only --tmpfs /tmp:size=64m,noexec debian:sid-slim perl -e '<syscall probe>'` → `io_uring_setup` EPERM, `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)` EPERM, `socket(AF_INET)` fd 3. Run B, same plus `--security-opt seccomp=unconfined` → `io_uring_setup` fd 3, `unshare` 0. io_uring is closed by an engine default devops-cli neither requests, records nor checks.
  - *Source*: vibes `examples/ephemeral-container-sandbox/sandbox.py`, `examples/ephemeral-container-sandbox/syscall_filter.py`, `examples/ephemeral-container-sandbox/test_sandbox.py`, `tools/seccomp_synthesizer.py`, `observations/systems/19-self-consistency-is-not-conformance.md`, `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md`, `AGENTS.md`

No path knows a deferral: `sync_roadmap_to_issues` (`:396`) opens an issue with a Backlog task file (`:281`); `reconcile-roadmap` (`:518`) writes milestones only; `gh project sync` status mapping (`github/projects.py:128-140, 203-213`) lacks `blocked`. vibes `AGENTS.md` (section 9, item 6): unrecorded judgements are re-litigated every pass and blocking only the implement path was not enough; `tools/roadmap_ingest.py` parses `(blocked: reason)` into `blocked_reason`, which `tools/sdlc_project_manager.py` refuses in `_is_unblocked_candidate`.
  - *Deliverable*: The priority half landed separately in #583: an item's priority now comes only from its own tag. Grammar: trailing `(blocked: <reason>)` after the closing `**` (vibes `docs/ROADMAP.md:104`), where `_clean_item_title` (`roadmap_sync.py:93`) cannot eat it; add `RoadmapItem.blocked_reason: str = ""` from the header regex, porting vibes `_BLOCKED_RE` and its ingest tests onto the existing Pydantic model. Report before enforcing: `sync-roadmap` prints a Deferred table and `RoadmapSyncResult` gains `deferred`. Honour on all three paths: no new issue for a deferred item; `reconcile-roadmap` adds `status/blocked` so `gh project sync` moves the card via `projects.py:1002-1008`, removed when the annotation goes; `_generate_task_file_content` (`:265`) writes `**Blocked**: <reason>`; `_determine_section_status` gains `blocked`. Validation: bare `(blocked)` or empty reason fails a live-roadmap test or a `scripts/check_structural_invariants.py` check; a test pins that a `❌ Rejected` row in the Value vs. Effort Prioritization Matrix never becomes an issue.
  - *Constraint*: Blocking only the create-issue path repeats the vibes defect: `reconcile-roadmap` and `gh project sync` would leave an existing card in Ready (`projects.py:1019` default) or Backlog (`projects.py:220-222`); the v0.2.24 Blocker Radar and v0.3.2 `gh pm research`, `deps`, `reconcile` must take `blocked_reason` from their first version. Deferred items stay open in every sync output; checking off, deleting or lowering priority destroys the reason. Label reconciliation is one-way and never clears a person-set `status/blocked`: the task-file `**Blocked**:` line (`_build_task_file_issue_map`, `roadmap_sync.py:448`, maps by issue number) records tool-set labels; no new store, or Low effort fails. Sequence: parser, tests and the Deferred table first; reconciliation second; validator last, after the rule enters `devops_roadmap_governance_block` (`ai/instruction_generator.py:205`), AGENTS.md's source.
  - *Measured*: `grep -c '^- \[ \] \*\*' docs/ROADMAP.md` = 117 open items; `grep -c -i '(blocked' docs/ROADMAP.md` = 0; `grep -c '❌ Rejected' docs/ROADMAP.md` = 2. An in-memory `_parse_single_roadmap_item` probe: an annotated `(P1 - High)` header lost its annotation.
