- [ ] **Worktree Inventory & Safe Orphan Reclamation (`devops repos worktrees`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Nothing in `src/` runs `git worktree`; the only hit, `ci/cache.py:168`, parses status porcelain. `delegate_task` (`ai/harness/workflow.py:280`) hands a sub-agent no workspace and the harness `Shell` defaults to `cwd='.'` (`ai/harness/shell.py:110`), so subagents share the caller's checkout — yet worktrees still accumulate. An external harness left five `worktree-wf_*` checkouts under `.claude/worktrees/`, each clean, one commit ahead of `main`, with a tip on no other local or remote ref, and this tool cannot list them. git itself reports `locked` and `prunable` per entry and `prune --expire` handles missing directories; what it cannot say is whether a checkout still holds unmerged work. vibes `patterns/collision-free-worktree-fleet-allocation.md` names zombie-checkout accumulation as one of four hazards and prescribes leased, TTL-bounded worktrees with automated pruning.
  - *Deliverable*: A `devops repos worktrees` group, `status` first: parse `git worktree list --porcelain` (shell out through `core/process.run_subprocess`; GitPython 3.1.62 has no worktree API, only `WorkTreeRepositoryUnsupported`) and add per entry commits ahead of `main`, dirty-path count, whether any other ref contains the tip, and git's own `locked`/`prunable` flags. Then `prune`, dry-run by default: remove only entries that are clean and contained elsewhere, refuse everything else without `--force`, finish with `git worktree prune`. Use `git worktree lock --reason` as the owner marker so plain git sees it. Port nothing from `examples/worktree-swarm-arbiter/worktree_arbiter.py`: its lock wait is a fixed 5 x 50 ms loop and `process_group_id` is hard-coded 0, so its killpg branch never fires. The lease registry (pgid, expiry, heartbeat on the `SandboxRegistry._file_lock` seam, `sandbox/registry.py:40`) with SIGTERM-to-SIGKILL release via `_terminate_process_group` (`ai/harness/shell.py:293`) is a later v0.3.1 extension of `devops ai shadow`, and the `index.lock` retry belongs in `ai/retries` on `tenacity` as separate hygiene; both reuse this command's refusal rules.
  - *Constraint*: Reclamation deletes commits. Every orphan measured here holds a commit on no other ref, so a TTL-driven `remove --force` plus `branch -D` would have destroyed five commits; the refusal rule is the deliverable, not an option. Placement: `find_top_level_repo_root` (`core/repo.py:45-57`) returns `candidates[-1]`, so a worktree nested inside the repository resolves to the main checkout and `devops ci` run there would aim `_clean_coverage_artifacts` (`commands/ci.py:159`) at the main checkout's `.data/.coverage*`. New worktrees follow the outside-the-tree convention the numbered feature worktrees already use; changing the resolver is a separate decision. Data tier: unmerged `fix/553-shared-data-dir` deliberately shares one data directory across worktrees, the reverse of vibes' per-worktree `DEVOPS_CLI_DATA_DIR` recipe, which must not be ported.
  - *Measured*: `git worktree list --porcelain | grep -c '^worktree '` = 13 today; the count has differed in every session that measured it, so it stays out of the prose. For each `worktree-wf_*` branch, `git rev-list --count main..<branch>` = 1 and `git for-each-ref --contains <tip> refs/heads refs/remotes` lists 0 refs other than the branch itself. `git -C <worktree> status --porcelain | wc -l` = 0 for every one. `timeout 60 du -sh .claude/worktrees` timed out, so no size is claimed.
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/collision-free-worktree-fleet-allocation.md`, `patterns/multi-agent-codebase-concurrency.md`, `observations/systems/06-multi-agent-concurrency-shared-workspace-hazards-and-swarm-coordination.md`, `examples/worktree-swarm-arbiter/worktree_arbiter.py`, `examples/worktree-swarm-arbiter/README.md`
- [ ] **IPv6 Egress CIDR Fix and Deny-Class Property Tests for Sandbox Policy (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The policy boundary is a set of pure functions: `safe_resolve_subpath`, `validate_no_path_traversal` and `validate_path_parameter` (`src/devops_cli/core/paths.py:16-175`), the last guarding agent tool kwargs via `ai/ext_langchain.py:18` and `ai/agents/context.py:19`; `is_loopback_or_private_host` (`core/validation.py:81-98`); and the whitelist validators and ipBlock builders behind `devops sandbox network-policy` (`sandbox/models.py:55-215`). Tests use hand-picked inputs with no IPv6 or NUL case (`tests/test_consolidation_core_paths.py:128-171`, `tests/test_validation.py:190-216`); `hypothesis` is not a dependency. Defects found: `_resolve_public_host` and `_resolve_local_host` append `/32` to IPv6 addresses (`models.py:125,149,163,185`), so the docstring "strictly to validated whitelisted destinations" (`models.py:190`) is false; `_extract_host_or_ip` truncates IPv6 hosts (`models.py:63-65`); NUL makes `safe_resolve_subpath` raise `ValueError` past `error_cls` (`paths.py:56`); a substring `..` test rejects benign names; six metadata-name checks in five modules disagree (`core/validation.py:40,229`, `sandbox/probe.py:55`, `sandbox/models.py:84`, `telemetry/waterfall.py:127`, `ai/models/ollama.py:37`). vibes' campaign is a fixed event table with an unread seed (`lsm_gate.py:257-276`); its README accepts 0.85 containment, its pattern claims 1.0.
  - *Deliverable*: Three PRs. PR1, no new dependency: emit each address's full-length prefix (`ipaddress.ip_network(ip).with_prefixlen`) and parse bare and `[addr]:port` IPv6 with `urllib.parse`/`ipaddress`, with IPv6 regression tests. PR2: one metadata predicate on ollama's superset (`fd00:ec2::254`, bare `metadata`, trailing dot) at all six sites; NUL raises `error_cls`; a component-wise `part == ".."` check after one decode. PR3: add `hypothesis` to the dev group unless Reach Tally, Replay Gate and Hash-Seed Check for Parser Fuzzing already has, and write properties whose classes come from the oracle, not syntax: forbidden means resolving outside the base after exactly the decoding the consumer does, reaching a non-global or metadata address, or containing NUL (must raise `error_cls`); a benign generator must pass, so deny-everything fails; every emitted ipBlock carries its family's maximum prefix and contains the resolved address. Build addresses with `st.ip_addresses(v=..., network=...)` and a stubbed `getaddrinfo`. Service URLs stay with Structured Fuzzing of Parsers and Schema Boundaries; in-container escapes with Sandbox Enforcement Audit: Declared vs Engine vs Enforced.
  - *Constraint*: The sibling task's CI profile runs explicit examples only, so these tests need a per-test `@settings(phases=[Phase.explicit, Phase.generate], derandomize=True)`: derandomized generation is fixed per commit, not a lucky search. A property that encodes today's behaviour, such as denying `%252e%252e/etc`, which pathlib never decodes, passes forever and finds nothing. The metadata predicate widens what five call sites deny; test each. No floor below 1.0: a deterministic predicate that allows one forbidden input has a bug.
  - *Measured*: `uv run --no-sync python /tmp/claude-1000/-workspaces-devops-cli/cd5cb264-de6b-43bc-b9b0-5602359c9515/scratchpad/probe/rewrite/p.py`: with `getaddrinfo` stubbed to one AAAA and one A record, `_resolve_public_host` returned `2606:4700::1/32`, which covers 2**96 addresses; `_extract_host_or_ip("fd00:ec2::254")` returned `fd00`. Same run: `validate_path_parameter("file_path", ...)` denied `notes..txt`, `v1..2.diff` and `a..b/c` but allowed `~/.ssh/id_rsa` and `file:///etc/passwd` with `allow_absolute=False`; `safe_resolve_subpath(base, "a\x00b")` raised `ValueError`.
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/kernel-enforced-lsm-sandbox-containment.md`, `patterns/zero-trust-sandboxing-and-observability.md`, `examples/ebpf-lsm-kernel-gate/lsm_gate.py`, `examples/ebpf-lsm-kernel-gate/README.md`, `examples/ebpf-lsm-kernel-gate/test_lsm_gate.py`, `examples/ephemeral-container-sandbox/README.md`, `observations/systems/34-ebpf-lsm-kernel-gates-and-dynamic-containment-fuzzing.md`
- [ ] **Sandbox Egress Port Scoping, Collector Lane & In-Container Trace Context (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Sandboxes default to `--network=none` (`src/devops_cli/sandbox/models.py:228,281`). `devops sandbox network-policy` (`commands/sandbox.py:1004`, MCP `ai/mcp/server.py:1400`) emits deny-all for isolated; namespace and whitelist modes add a kube-dns 53 rule, and whitelist modes add resolved `/32` `ipBlock`s (`models.py:189-221`) with no `ports`, so an entry's explicit port is discarded and every port to that IP opens. `run_subprocess` children get `TRACEPARENT` when tracing is on (`core/process.py:264,404`); containers do not: both env builders pass only `config.env` (`sandbox/engine.py:230`, `docker/sandbox.py:222-250`) and `exec_in_container` takes no environment (`docker/engine.py:401`). No lane reaches the OTel collector, and nothing counts blocked egress: no `alert:` rule, Alertmanager disabled (`k8s/monitoring/prometheus-values.yaml:71-72`), and squid-exporter documents no denied counter (`docs/squid-failover-and-observability.md:62-69`). vibes allows DNS, collector and cache lanes and pages on any blocked attempt.
  - *Deliverable*: In order. First, port scoping: keep the resolved-`/32` allowlist (vibes' `0.0.0.0/0 except RFC 1918` form admits every public host) and emit each entry's port, defaulting to TCP 443 for `https` or bare hosts and 80 for `http`. Second, an opt-in collector lane: `otel` namespaceSelector plus the collector's podSelector on TCP 4317/4318. No 6379 lane: the only Valkey (`k8s/llm/valkey.yaml:7`) backs the shared LLM cache, and the `llm` perimeter admits only its own namespace and `monitoring` (`k8s/llm/networkpolicy.yaml:23-31`). Third, container trace context: `get_tracer().inject_trace_env` (`telemetry/tracer.py:613`) on a copy of the env in both builders, and an `environment` parameter on `exec_in_container` forwarded to docker-py `exec_run`, with a fresh context per exec. Fourth, measure only: add the `squid` namespace to the Fluent Bit tail path (`k8s/logging/fluent-bit-values.yaml:30`) so `TCP_DENIED` rows from Squid's JSON log (`k8s/squid/configmap.yaml:155`) reach Loki for the planned 'whitelisted vs. blocked' panel of the v0.2.24 dashboard suite. No alert rule until Alertmanager is enabled. Distinct from the drafted 'Sandbox Enforcement Audit', which compares configuration to engine state.
  - *Constraint*: A port-less rule is worse than it looks: an agent-supplied `https://host:8443` entry, via the MCP tool, opens every port and protocol to that host. A policy is only as real as the CNI: `devops k8s bootstrap` starts minikube with no `--cni` (`commands/k8s/cluster_runtime.py:94,99`) while its help claims Calico (`lang/en/help.py:260`); that false claim is a separate item, and until it is fixed a zero denial count proves nothing. Under `--network=none` a connect fails inside the container, so no host-side enforcer sees it; report such lanes as uncounted, never as zero. Select the collector by label, never a resolved `/32`.
  - *Measured*: Whitelist entry `https://1.1.1.1:8443` yields a bare `1.1.1.1/32` rule with no `ports` (`.venv/bin/python -c "from devops_cli.sandbox.models import SandboxNetworkConfig as C; print(C(mode='public_whitelist', public_whitelist=['https://1.1.1.1:8443']).to_k8s_network_policy()['spec']['egress'])"`); 0 trace-context injections in the sandbox and docker packages (`grep -rn 'TRACEPARENT\|inject_env\|inject_trace_env' src/devops_cli/sandbox src/devops_cli/docker | wc -l`); 0 alerting rules (`grep -rn 'alert:' k8s | wc -l`).
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/zero-trust-sandboxing-and-observability.md`, `resources/k8s/agent-sandbox/network-policy.yaml`, `resources/observability/prometheus-agent-alerts.yaml`
- [ ] **Board-Owned Project Status, Declared Field Sources and Truthful Project Sync (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Docs say `devops gh project sync` pushes task-file status to the board (`src/devops_cli/lang/en/help.py:746-748`, `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md:852`, `docs/agent/tasks/README.md:13`), but `sync_remote_project` only counts task items (`src/devops_cli/github/projects.py:1485`). `project reconcile` recomputes Status, Priority, Category, Value and Effort every pass (`projects.py:1169-1225`), and `_filter_differing_fields` (`:1154`) overwrites differing board values, so hand corrections revert, breaking `AGENTS.md:61`. Status matches labels by substring with a Ready fallback (`:1004-1021`): `status/ready-to-merge` and `status/triage` both yield Ready, and no `.github/labels.yml` label yields Backlog. Category/Value/Effort come from `TAXONOMY_CATEGORY_MAPPING` (`:1046`) keyed on the `type/feature` label roadmap sync puts on every issue (`github/roadmap_sync.py:121`), not the ROADMAP matrix; `_derive_scope` (`:77`) keyword-matches, which `AGENTS.md:21` prohibits. Nothing records which branch decided, results are counts (`projects.py:1431-1432`), and a failed issue fetch returns `[]` (`github/issues.py:118-140`). vibes `tools/sdlc_project_manager.py` hit the same faults; `tools/portfolio_balance.py` `classify()` returns `(kind, source)`.
  - *Deliverable*: Measure first: `project reconcile --dry-run` lists each change with field, old and new value, and source, and `reconcile_project_custom_fields` returns that list (live runs need `read:project`). Then the board owns Status: reconcile writes it only when unset or when issue/PR state forces Done, In Review or In Progress; other moves belong to the built-in workflows `project workflows list` audits. Status labels match exactly; `status/blocked` maps only if the template gains Blocked; `CONST_PROJECT_WORKFLOW_EXPECTATIONS` expects the template's Backlog, not Todo. Reconcile never overwrites a non-empty Category/Value/Effort with an inferred value. Once a join key exists (a test that every scheduled matrix row joins its item, or a key column), sync stamps Value/Effort from the matrix and Category through a declared category map; until then it stays unset. Sync reports the `scope/cli` fallback share. Fetch failures raise, fetches paginate past 200, and reconcile refuses a missing template board instead of defaulting to board 2 (`src/devops_cli/commands/gh.py:613`). Rewrite the three `project sync` texts. Separate: suffix priority (R1-16's first PR), priority refresh in `issues reconcile-roadmap`, promotion (planned `devops gh pm deps`), Ready ordering (Sprint Kanban `sort_by`), the literal-subset lint (Anti-Brittle Constant Elimination).
  - *Constraint*: Making task files write Status creates a second owner fighting the board and `Closes #N`. A reconciler that overwrites Status reverts manual triage; one that skips closed or PR-backed items leaves merged work outside Done. An inferred field that keeps writing is worse than an unset one: the board presents it as decided. A raised fetch error must stay distinct from a successful empty list, or a new repository never syncs.
  - *Measured*: `gh issue list --state all --limit 2000 --json number --jq length`: 265 issues, past the 200 fetched at `roadmap_sync.py:410`. `uv run python probe/dedupe.py`: 63 of 105 open roadmap items are untracked whether the issue list is empty, capped or full, so a silent `[]` goes unnoticed. `uv run python probe/matrix_vs_reconcile.py`: inferred Effort contradicts the matrix on 71 of 93 scheduled rows. `uv run python probe/value_matrix_join.py`: 50 of 93 rows join a body item by exact title. Scratch probes, run from the devops-cli root.
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/autonomous-sdlc-project-management.md`, `tools/sdlc_project_manager.py`, `observations/devops-cli/06-rate-limits-and-anti-brittle-heuristics.md`, `tools/portfolio_balance.py`, `tools/project_tooling.py`
- [ ] **PR Readiness Requires an Issue-Linked, Completed Task File (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `AGENTS.md:160` grounds every change in an issue and a `docs/agent/tasks/task-<issue>-<slug>.md` file, and `AGENTS.md:65` and `:178` require that file Done, every box `- [x]`, in the delivering commit. Since #489, `tests/test_agent_task_files.py:31-35` checks only the issue link, no `**PR**` field and the status enum. `_evaluate_pr_blockers` (`commands/pr.py:1304`) never reads issues or task files. PRs into release branches get no native issue link, so an issue closes only through the body's closing keyword (`github/issue_closure.py:241`). `project sync` parses every task file (`commands/gh.py:551`) and counts it as synced (`github/projects.py:1485`), but writes only issues and PR-derived fields; `lang/en/help.py:746`, `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md:70` and `docs/agent/tasks/README.md:13` repeat the claim. vibes claims 100% issue-to-commit traceability and warns of 'sycophantic completion'.
  - *Deliverable*: One PR. Honest sync: `sync_remote_project` reports only what it wrote; drop the task-file parse and `--task-file` from `project sync` and reword the three docs. Task-file test: a Done file holds no `- [ ]` (counted with `_ITEM_REGEX`, `github/projects.py:201`), deferred work is a plain bullet naming its follow-up issue, the 13 offending files are fixed, and `_generate_task_file_content` (`github/roadmap_sync.py:265`) emits `- [ ]`. Readiness: one blocker in `_evaluate_pr_blockers`, on by default, `--allow-ungrounded` for release, hotfix and roadmap-planning PRs. The body names an issue via `extract_linked_issues` (`issue_closure.py:100`) or a `Part N of #<issue>` line; the changed files (`gh pr view --json files`) include `task-<N>-*.md`; for an issue the PR closes, that file reads Done on the head branch. AGENTS.md cites the passing head-SHA check runs readiness already requires instead of typed counts; `_build_quality_checklist` (`commands/release.py:922`) ticks from them or drops its boxes. No maintained package fits.
  - *Constraint*: Not `_extract_linked_issue_numbers` (`projects.py:1251`): it skips non-OPEN PRs, takes a trailing `(#N)` title (the squash PR number on main) and scans code blocks; gate on the parser closure acts on. Keyword-only would block partials: #524 and #525 are 'Part 1/2 of #515'. Read task files at the head SHA, not locally. Leave `_determine_section_status` alone; it also classifies headings. Never require or cross-check a pasted verification log: the vibes sample credits task-218 with '24 passed' and '93.4% coverage', but `task-218-fix-release-draft-pr-description-generator.md:32` gives neither, and CI keeps nothing to compare (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:91` writes `.data/coverage.xml`; nothing uploads it or emits JUnit). Leave numeric criteria unjudged; complexity is ungated (per the 'Structural Pre-Commit Hook Inversion' entry). Coordinate with Defect PRs Carry Their Hardening (same blocker list and files fetch) and Roadmap Deferrals Recorded Once (extends the sync path removed here).
  - *Measured*: `gh pr list --state merged --limit 60 --json number,body,files,baseRefName,closingIssuesReferences`, scored with `extract_linked_issues`: every PR targets `release/v0.2.23` with empty `closingIssuesReferences`; 9 lack a closing keyword and 3 touch `src/` without a task file (#472, #485, #490). `parse_tasks_to_project_items(Path('docs/agent/tasks'))` on a clean HEAD checkout: 1094 items counted as synced, never written. `python3 probe/scan3.py`: 13 Done files hold unchecked `- [ ]` lines outside fences.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/devops-cli/03-autonomous-project-governance.md`, `observations/devops-cli/01-tdd-as-living-contract.md`, `artifacts/task-harnesses/structured-task-spec-template.md`, `artifacts/task-harnesses/sample-completed-task-spec.md`, `tools/project_tooling.py`
- [ ] **Correct Secondary-Limit Backoff and Retry-After Handling in `run_gh` (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `run_gh` (`src/devops_cli/github/rate_limiter.py:1646`) is already the single `gh` seam, with quota-derived pacing, a read cache and a retry loop. `calculate_backoff_delay` (`:1013`) never checks `remaining`: whenever a future reset is tracked it sleeps until the primary reset, and one almost always is, because missing or stale state is refreshed before a request is paced (`:711`). Otherwise it retries after 2 to 5 s with no secondary floor. `Retry-After` is never parsed (`:562`), and the only tracked-reset test (`tests/test_github_rate_limiter.py:791`) uses remaining=0. AGENTS.md's 'Zero Bare `gh` Invocations' claims jittered secondary backoff, and nothing enforces its bare-`gh` ban. The httpx2 GraphQL path raises on 403/429 (`graphql.py:780`). vibes records a 20-PR triage stalled for an hour by a retry loop.
  - *Deliverable*: PR1 (the P0): classify primary (remaining zero), secondary and `Retry-After`. Wait for the reset only on a primary limit. On a secondary limit wait a configured floor (default 60 s, PyGithub `GithubRetry`'s `DEFAULT_SECONDARY_RATE_WAIT`, as `client.py:42` gets), then tenacity `wait_random_exponential` under a local cap. Return a server `Retry-After` unchanged. Declare tenacity in `pyproject.toml` (today only transitive), and do not reuse `pydantic_ai`'s `wait_retry_after`, which clamps. Add throttle and wait counters to the persisted quota meta (`_save_disk_quota`) so `devops gh rate-limit`, a separate process, shows them. Correct the AGENTS.md sentence. P1 follow-ups: route GraphQL 403/429 through the same classifier via the existing `HTTPX2TenacityTransport` seam, and add an AST invariant to `tests/test_architectural_invariants.py` allowlisting only `devcontainer.py`'s `gh auth token` and `gh auth login --with-token` (credential migration, no API quota). P2: replace `ratelimit` (`:54`) and the hardcoded `AsyncLimiter(max_rate=60, time_period=60)` (`:650`), both contradicting the docstring at `:6`, with one configured pyrate-limiter ceiling (sync and async). Pre-flight bulk budgeting stays with Proportional API Rate Budgeting & GraphQL Circuit Breaker Guard.
  - *Constraint*: Clamping to `min(retry_after, cap)` retries early and turns a primary limit into a secondary one; sleeping silently through the primary window looks like a hang. When the required wait exceeds a configured maximum, raise `GitHubRateLimitError` stating the wait and reset time, as `GithubRetry`'s `max_rate_limit_wait` does when set (it defaults to None). Two tests pin both halves: a server `Retry-After` is returned unchanged, and the local cap bounds only the client's own growth; the remaining=0 test keeps passing. The invariant must be AST-based: grep flags docstrings and cannot tell whether a command list reaches `run_gh` or a raw subprocess.
  - *Measured*: `uv run python probe/rewrite_backoff.py`: after `update_quota('core', remaining=4999, limit=5000, reset_epoch=now+3000)`, `calculate_backoff_delay` on a secondary-limit message returns 3000.0 s. Same probe with no tracked quota: attempt 1 waits 2.2–3.0 s, attempt 2 waits 4.2–5.0 s. `grep -n -A6 '^name = "ratelimit"' uv.lock`: 2.2.1, upload-time 2018-12-17.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/devops-cli/06-rate-limits-and-anti-brittle-heuristics.md`, vibes `examples/fastmcp-token-bucket-gateway/README.md`, vibes `examples/fastmcp-token-bucket-gateway/gateway.py`, vibes `examples/fastmcp-token-bucket-gateway/test_gateway.py`
- [ ] **Reviewer-Aware Reply Detection for Thread Resolution and Oldest-First PR Updates (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: AGENTS.md:132 requires an in-thread reply before a thread is resolved, and AGENTS.md:240-245 require oldest-first PR processing. Nothing in `src/` enforces either. The reply test is `len(t.comments) > 1` (`commands/pr.py:1226`, `github/pr_threads.py:312`), so a reviewer's own follow-up counts as a reply. `--allow-replied-threads` then reports no blocker, and `--auto-resolve` and `threads resolve-all` (default `--only-replied`) close the thread. The thread query already fetches each comment's `author { login }` (`github/pr_threads.py:114`). `threads resolve` (`commands/pr.py:1747`) resolves with no check. `update --all` iterates `_fetch_open_prs` (`commands/pr.py:1552`, loop at 1610) in `gh pr list` order, which is newest-first. vibes `patterns/fifo-pull-request-shepherding.md:77` also demands the fixing SHA in every reply. No devops-cli incident supports that rule yet (see Measured).
  - *Deliverable*: Add one predicate, `has_non_opener_reply(thread)`: some comment after the first has an author login different from the first comment's. Use it at both call sites. Reword `lang/en/help.py:678-690` to "a reply from someone other than the thread opener", and add the probe case as a regression test. `threads resolve` refuses a thread without such a reply unless `--without-reply` is passed, and MCP `pr_thread_resolve` exposes that flag. Sort open PRs by ascending number in one helper, so `update --all` runs oldest-first. In-Flight Work, PR Stagnation & Blocker Radar can reuse that helper. Queue position in `check-readiness`/`wait` belongs to that entry, whose body is in v0.2.24 while its matrix row says v0.2.23. Ship this fix separately and first: MCP `pr_check_readiness` appends the removed `--require-ready` (`ai/mcp/server.py:2280-2281`), and the CLI rejects it. Drop the flag, then fix `tests/test_fastmcp_contracts.py:519,532` and `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md:1138`.
  - *Constraint*: Do not port the SHA rule yet. A reply's SHA stops pointing at a branch commit after a rebase or force-push, because the rebase rewrites that commit. Agents would then re-reply after every rebase or stop rebasing, and a reasoned decline would need an empty commit. Do not add a readiness blocker either: threads resolved under the old rule would fail open PRs. Revisit both only after a devops-cli thread is seen resolved without a reply.
  - *Measured*: A thread whose only follow-up is the reviewer's own "Still not fixed." yields 0 blockers under `--allow-replied-threads` and is selected by `resolve-all --only-replied` (`uv run python -B replied_probe.py`, GitHub stubbed). The last 100 merged PRs (#379-#577) carry 9 review threads. All 9 were opened and self-resolved by CodeQL with no reply (`gh api graphql` over `pullRequests(states:MERGED,last:100){reviewThreads}`). `gh pr list --state all --limit 5` returned [577,576,575,574,573].
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/fifo-pull-request-shepherding.md`, vibes `patterns/multi-agent-codebase-concurrency.md`, vibes `observations/systems/06-multi-agent-concurrency-shared-workspace-hazards-and-swarm-coordination.md`
- [ ] **Fail-Closed PR Check Verdicts From One Bucket Classifier (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: When `gh pr checks` exits non-zero, `devops pr checks` renders a REST fallback table and returns 0 (`src/devops_cli/commands/pr.py:383-385`). `tests/test_pr_cmd.py:715-755` asserts that exit 0 while `Analyze` fails, and `AGENTS.md:131` and `:250` tell agents to monitor CI with this command. When its single unpaginated check-runs call fails, `check-readiness` reports no check blockers (`pr.py:1241-1271`). A `blocked` state still stops failing required checks (`pr.py:1203-1212`), but under `unstable` or `--allow-blocked-state` it prints "satisfies merge readiness", the certification `AGENTS.md:244` relies on. `pr wait` passes `gh api --paginate` output to `json.loads` (`github/pr_monitor.py:413-443`), which rejects multi-page output, usually ending in a timeout rather than a pass. Four conclusion sets disagree (`pr_monitor.py:23-31`, `config/constants.py:412`, `pr.py:1269`, `pr.py:265`), and the only rollup parser, `_parse_check_runs` (`pr_monitor.py:240`), has no caller. MCP `pr_check_readiness` passes a `--require-ready` flag the CLI does not define (`ai/mcp/server.py:2280-2281`). vibes `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md` §8 records `gh pr checks --exit-status | tail` exiting 0 on a failure.
  - *Deliverable*: Two PRs. PR1 adds `github/check_verdict.py`, returning gh's buckets (pass, fail, pending, skipping, cancel) plus `unread`, read from `gh pr checks --json name,state,bucket,workflow,link`. `pr checks`, `pr ready` and `check-readiness` take their verdict from it: non-zero on `fail`, `cancel` or `unread`, exit 8 on pending as gh does, table still rendered. PR1 inverts the exit-0 test, adds a readiness test where check-runs fails under `unstable`, and drops the MCP `require_ready` argument. PR2 moves `pr wait` and `monitor` onto the seam, keeping REST polling of `/check-runs` via `gh api --paginate --slurp` plus `/status`, with exactly one conclusion-to-bucket table and a parity test against `gh pr checks --json bucket`. PR2 deletes the other conclusion sets, `_parse_check_runs`, `_parse_check_run_node` and the duplicate `_fetch_commit_check_runs` (`pr.py:986`). No workflow lint is added (see Measured). Must land before the v0.2.24 `tab-github` hub, which binds readiness to `check-readiness`.
  - *Constraint*: `gh pr checks --json` exits 0 even with a check in the `fail` bucket, so switching to JSON while keeping the exit-code logic would reproduce the defect; the verdict must come from the buckets. An empty, errored or partial read is `unread` and blocks. `--allow-pending-checks` excuses pending checks only; `pr ready --force` is the only override. Do not reuse `parse_paginated_json` (`github/client.py:394`): it stops silently at the first decode error (`client.py:414-415`), the same fail-open. Whether gh's rollup pages past its first query is unverified, so a test should assert the bucket count against REST `total_count`.
  - *Measured*: On PR 401, `gh pr checks 401` exits 1, while `gh pr checks 401 --json name,state,bucket` exits 0 with 1 of 7 checks in `fail`. On that PR's head, `json.loads` rejects `gh api --paginate '.../check-runs?per_page=3'` output with "Extra data". `SHELLCHECK_OPTS=--enable=check-extra-masked-returns actionlint` reports 3 SC2312 findings, all command substitutions, none carrying a gate verdict.
  - *Source*: vibes `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`
- [ ] **Scanner Outcome Status: Unavailable, Failed or Unqueried Is Not Clean (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Scanners and advisory lookups record work that never ran as clean. A missing binary, a non-zero exit with non-JSON output or an exception reaches `fallback_scan` (`security/base.py:93-97,111-117,132-134`), which returns `[]` unless overridden (`base.py:58-60`); `scan report` and `scan_all` store a raised error as `[]` (`commands/scan.py:831-834`, `security/registry.py:83-85`); `tools_run` names every attempted scanner (`security/pipeline.py:130`); SARIF runs come from surviving findings alone (`commands/scan.py:839`, `security/sarif.py:138-144`). #516 predicts review states with `check_binary` (`ai/review/pipeline.py:860-872`), so a crashing binary shows as "ran". OSV lookups return `[]` on any exception, and `query_package` caches it (`security/vulnerability_lookup.py:232-239,290-295`); `DependencySpec.severity` defaults to CLEAN (`models/vulnerability.py:14`), so `_check_scanned_clean_dependency` invalidates a correct CVE finding. A Dive efficiency score gates as a MEDIUM security result (`security/dive.py:146`); Popeye audits the kube context's cluster whatever the target (`security/popeye.py:66-73`). vibes `sarif_report.py` emits zero-result runs, none for a failed adapter.
  - *Deliverable*: Three PRs, in order. (1) `DependencySpec.severity` defaults to `UNCHECKED`; failed lookups are not cached, `query_batch` returns a status per key, and only a successful lookup yields CLEAN or lets the verifier invalidate. (2) `BaseSecurityScanner.scan` returns `ScanOutcome(status, findings, reason)`: `ran`, `built-in patterns`, `not_applicable`, `unavailable`, `failed` or `dry-run`, deciding applicability (Dive without an image, Popeye without a context) before `check_binary`. `ScanReport.outcomes` replaces `tools_run`; single-scanner commands say "not run", never "passed"; each `run_*_scan` wraps its registry scanner; observed outcomes replace #516's `_static_analyzer_states`. `--fail-on` exits non-zero on `failed` or when a `--scanner`-named tool did not run; otherwise a missing scanner warns. `gating: ClassVar[bool]` is False for Dive alone, and `exceeds` counts gating findings. (3) Before Publish Scanner Findings to Code Scanning uploads: `ran` emits a run, zero results included, with `invocations[].executionSuccessful` and start/end times; other outcomes emit no run, only a `toolExecutionNotifications` entry on the devops-cli invocation; non-gating results get `level: note`, `problem.severity: recommendation` and no `security-severity`. Tests validate against the vendored OASIS schema via `jsonschema.validators.validator_for`; `jsonschema`, locked only via `mcp`, joins the dev group.
  - *Constraint*: Not-applicable must differ from unavailable, or `scan report` goes red on every workstation without a cluster or image. No unavailable or failed scanner becomes an empty run, and checkov, gitleaks, kubeconform or tflint fallback findings never carry the real tool's driver name: once empty runs are uploaded, either closes the tool's open alerts. Code scanning's handling of `executionSuccessful: false` is unverified. Class is not severity: Dive is not demoted to LOW, and unregistered scanners and `from_sarif` input stay gating.
  - *Measured*: With only bandit of the 11 registered binaries on PATH (`command -v`), `devops scan report <empty-dir> --json --fail-on LOW --sarif out.sarif` exits 0, lists all 11 under `tools`, and writes 1 SARIF run, `devops-cli`, holding only `tool` and `results`. `devops scan sast <empty-dir>` without semgrep exits 0 with "No static AST pattern flaws detected". `uv run --no-sync python probe/osv_fail_clean.py`: on `ConnectError`, `query_batch` returns `[]` and a HIGH CVE finding comes back INVALIDATED, not reportable.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/systems/17-a-fuzzers-first-report-is-about-the-fuzzer.md`, `tools/sarif_report.py`, `examples/code-smell-quantifier/smell_quantifier.py`, `examples/go-leak-sentinel/go_leak_sentinel.py`, `examples/go-leak-sentinel/test_go_leak_sentinel.py`, `tools/metric_reference.py`, `observations/systems/09-multi-agent-epistemic-loss-and-delegation-boundary-distortion.md`, `artifacts/schemas/sarif-schema-2.1.0.json`, `tools/fuzz_harness.py`, `tools/resource_iteration_workbench.py`, `examples/code-smell-quantifier/README.md`, `examples/code-smell-quantifier/test_smell_quantifier.py`, `tools/reliability_slo.py`, `observations/systems/13-verify-the-finding-before-you-fix-it.md`
- [ ] **Vault Commands Use the Token `devops vault login` Stores (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `devops vault login` stores the issued token in the OS keyring by default (`--store`, `commands/vault.py:298-301`), but nothing in `src/` reads it back. `VaultSecretBroker` takes its token only from the argument, `VAULT_TOKEN` or `DEVOPS_CLI_VAULT_TOKEN` (`security/vault_broker.py:90-91`), so after a successful login `status`, `get`, `set`, `sync` and `leases` all build a token-less broker (`commands/vault.py:74,129,176,227,327`). `get` skips Vault and silently returns the keyring fallback (`vault_broker.py:134-141`), `VaultProvider` in the shared secret chain resolves nothing (`security/secrets.py:212-247`), and `leases --revoke` hands the missing token to `LeaseRegistry` (`token: str | None`, `security/vault_lease.py:166`), whose `revoke` swallows the rejection (`:257-271`); the user sees only "Could not revoke lease" (`commands/vault.py:347`). vibes `observations/polyglot/01` argues an unauthenticated client should not expose authenticated operations; the optional token is that gap here.
  - *Deliverable*: `VaultSecretBroker` resolves its token from the argument, then the environment and keyring `vault_token` via the existing `get_keyring_secret`, in the order `build_default_resolver` documents (`security/secrets.py:334-338`), stated in its docstring. `LeaseRegistry.token` becomes a required `str`: `login_approle` and `login_kubernetes` already return `VaultAuthResult` (`vault_lease.py:97-112,130-151`), and `mypy --strict` in CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:57`) then forces `vault leases` to handle the missing case. Commands that need Vault auth exit non-zero naming `VAULT_TOKEN`, `DEVOPS_CLI_VAULT_TOKEN` and `devops vault login`, the message style #465 used for AI keys. Tests: env unset with the keyring mocked to hold `vault_token` yields an authenticated broker; no token anywhere makes `vault leases --revoke X` name the missing token. Replace `test_registry_headers_omit_absent_credentials` (`tests/test_secret_resolution.py:698-702`). No new dependency: `hvac` is not one, and adopting it would not read this project's keyring entry.
  - *Constraint*: Do not port Rust type-state or add an AGENTS.md lifecycle rule: Python cannot consume `self`, and the other wrappers already connect on first use (`valkey/client.py:194-195`, `http/broker.py:59-68`). Keep `get`'s keyring fallback only after every token source has been tried; a fallback that hides an unread token is the current bug. Out of scope, as separate entries: `LeaseRegistry` has no producer and is per-process (`commands/vault.py:27-41`), so listing and `--renew` do nothing in a one-shot CLI; and `SqliteStepStore` raises a bare `AssertionError` after `close()` (`ai/durable.py:134,153,177`), where a lazy reopen would silently return an empty `:memory:` store.
  - *Measured*: `grep -rn vault_token src/devops_cli` returned 8 lines: one keyring write (`commands/vault.py:301`) and no keyring read. `grep -rn "\.track(\|\.track_auth(" src/devops_cli` returned 1 hit, inside `security/vault_lease.py` itself, so no command populates the lease registry.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/polyglot/01-rust-type-state-invariants.md`
- [ ] **Harness, Constellation and Telemetry Profile Report Only What Actually Ran (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `ai harness status` renders `AgentHarness.create_default()` (src/devops_cli/commands/ai_harness.py:60): `claude`/`claude-3-7-sonnet`, every slot `attached` (src/devops_cli/ai/harness/slots.py:709-764), not the configured `ollama`/`gemma4:26b` (src/devops_cli/config/defaults.py:63-64). `harness run` and `offload` call no model: `execute_tiered` templates Tier 1 "Decomposed" and Tier 3 "Verified" text (slots.py:785, 682-686), tokens are `len(text)//4` (slots.py:142), offload passes `frontier_tokens=0` (ai_harness.py:88), and `is_target_met` (slots.py:610) alone backs Principle 5's "85%+". `register_task` (src/devops_cli/ai/controller/manager.py:169) has no src caller, each CLI call builds a fresh manager (src/devops_cli/commands/ai_controller.py:119), and `is_quiesced()`/`get_active_route()` (manager.py:408, 412) have no consumer. `telemetry profile <command>` sets a child `TRACEPARENT` (src/devops_cli/commands/telemetry.py:349) that `run_subprocess` overwrites (src/devops_cli/core/process.py:264), reads only its in-process buffer (telemetry.py:361), and otherwise renders `sample.*` spans naming `qwen2.5-coder` (telemetry.py:362-367). `--last` (telemetry.py:299) is never read; the MCP tool's `--command` (src/devops_cli/ai/mcp/server.py:1118) is rejected. vibes `worktree_arbiter.py` `main()` likewise formats a default `SwarmFleetStatus()`.
  - *Deliverable*: First, a Typer CliRunner test per command that changes the source it should read (settings, `quiesce.json`, child spans) and asserts the output changes; these fail today. Harness: `status` shows the configured provider and model labelled "configured" and no unchecked `attached`; drop "sandbox state" from `ai_harness_status` (docs/MCP_TOOLS.md:20, server.py:2511). Delete `TokenSavingsSummary` and its output rows; `execute_tiered` reports the AST/glob search it ran and its match counts, naming no uncontacted model; Principle 5 stays an unmeasured target until paired runs are compared with `devops ai runs compare`. Constellation: default to deleting task registration and per-task fields, rewording quiesce/failover/resume as flag-only, unless a consumer is named; `ai_gateway_failover` already reroutes. Telemetry: `profile <command>` opens its span on the trace it hands the child, fetches child spans via `query_jaeger_trace` (src/devops_cli/telemetry/waterfall.py:159) with a bounded poll, and exits non-zero without a collector or a trace/command; delete the `sample.*` fallback and `--last`; the MCP tool passes the command positionally. Follow-ups: real model tiers; `trace_span` on logfire's OpenTelemetry `TracerProvider` with `W3CBaggagePropagator` (unowned since #319); per-span token and tier columns plus delegation spans (src/devops_cli/ai/harness/workflow.py:280), tier from `LLMResponse.served_by` (#474).
  - *Constraint*: Build-then-read-back tests prove only the serializer: `test_status_suspended_task_count_reporting` (tests/test_ai_controller.py:365) passes because it calls `register_task` itself. A zero-baseline percentage certifies the target where no model ran; a smaller figure or another hard-coded model keeps the false claim, and a waterfall cannot supply the baseline. AGENTS.md:382 allows `None` for sourceless scores; "wire it or remove it" for status fields is a new, stricter rule, stated as one. Port nothing from vibes' synthetic trace session. Out of scope: persona reply outcomes, `evaluation_duration_avg`, `TLSEnablementSummary.k8s_secrets`, `K8sSummary.services`.
  - *Measured*: `grep -rn '\.register_task(' src tests`: 11 calls, all in tests/test_ai_controller.py. `devops ai harness status --format json`: provider `claude`, model `claude-3-7-sonnet`, every slot `attached`. `devops ai harness offload --repo <probe repo> --symbol alpha --format json`: `frontier_tokens` 0, `savings_percentage` 100.0, `is_target_met` true. `devops telemetry profile --command "echo hi"`, the MCP tool's argv, exited 2; `devops telemetry profile "echo hi"` printed "No telemetry spans recorded for the specified trace."
  - *Source*: vibes `examples/worktree-swarm-arbiter/worktree_arbiter.py`, `examples/worktree-swarm-arbiter/README.md`, `examples/agent-telemetry-trace-generator/README.md`, `examples/agent-telemetry-trace-generator/generator.py`, `examples/agent-telemetry-trace-generator/test_generator.py`, `observations/systems/01-distributed-telemetry-and-agent-waterfalls.md`, `observations/systems/09-multi-agent-epistemic-loss-and-delegation-boundary-distortion.md`, `observations/systems/21-hierarchical-subagent-slot-offloading-and-epistemic-loss.md`, `tools/subagent_orchestrator.py`, `observations/systems/33-typed-epistemic-seams-and-lossless-subagent-handshakes.md`, `examples/epistemic-context-handshake/context_handshake.py` (cited as not ported)
- [ ] **Pinned Image Tags and Bounded Shared Valkey in the LLM Stack (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `k8s/llm/lightllm/deployment.yaml:27` and `k8s/llm/portkey/deployment.yaml:32` run `:latest` with `imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent`, so each node keeps whichever build it pulled first. `k8s/llm/valkey.yaml:40-47` starts the shared Valkey with no `--maxmemory` and no eviction policy under a 2048Mi limit. LiteLLM (`gateway/deployment.yaml:60`), Portkey (`portkey/deployment.yaml:46`) and Open WebUI websockets (`values-open-webui.yaml:47`) all write to it. The bundled knowledge base says to always set both (`valkey.md:132`), and `k8s/llm/valkey-runs.yaml:80-81` already does (`512mb`, `noeviction`). No test asserts either manifest. No CI step runs Checkov or kube-linter. The native fallback checks only Dockerfile `FROM` tags (`security/checkov.py:53`), and `devops k8s lint` prints "passed" when kube-linter is absent (`commands/k8s/security_audit.py:92`).
  - *Deliverable*: One PR. Pin both images to explicit version tags. In `k8s/llm/valkey.yaml`, add `--maxmemory` below the 2048Mi limit and `--maxmemory-policy volatile-lru`, mirroring `valkey-runs.yaml`. Assert the flag pair for both manifests in `tests/test_k8s_valkey_stack.py`. Commit `k8s/.checkov.yaml` (`framework: kubernetes`, `check: CKV_K8S_14`, `skip-path: coredns/deployment-patch.yaml`) and add one CI step that runs a pinned Checkov over `k8s/`. Checkov finds the file itself and exits non-zero on failure. No wrapper change is needed. `devops scan report --scanner checkov --fail-on` already exits 1 for local use (`commands/scan.py:851-852`), and `devops valkey info --section memory` already prints the memory fields.
  - *Constraint*: Routing CI through the devops wrapper would fail open. When the binary is missing, the wrapper silently runs the native fallback (`security/base.py:93-97`), which has no k8s tag check. That fix belongs with the delivered #516 analyzer states (`ai/review/pipeline.py:845`) and the *Gate Integrity* draft, not here. The coredns patch has no image and no kustomization renders it, so it is skipped by path. `allkeys-lru` could evict Open WebUI's websocket state. `volatile-lru` evicts only keys with a TTL and refuses writes when none remain, which is better than an OOM kill. Digest pinning (`CKV_K8S_43`) fails on every workload, and Dependabot covers only `github-actions` and `pip`. Digests therefore wait for *Finding Baseline for Incremental Scan Gate Adoption* and a Kubernetes-aware updater such as Renovate. A 0.0.0.0 rule is wrong here, because in-pod servers must listen on all interfaces. Host exposure comes from one `hostPort` (`k8s/llm/ollama.yaml:95`) plus `NodePort` services, most documented as local-minikube choices. Compose `${VAR:?}` and loopback checks have no target in devops-cli.
  - *Measured*: `uvx --from checkov==3.3.19 checkov -d k8s --framework kubernetes --check CKV_K8S_14,CKV_K8S_43 -o json`: `CKV_K8S_14` fails in 3 files (lightllm, portkey, coredns patch). `CKV_K8S_43` fails in 13, with none passing. The same Checkov over a copy of `k8s/` with the proposed `.checkov.yaml` gives 2 failures and a non-zero exit. `grep -rEn '^\s*type: NodePort' k8s | wc -l` gives 10.

### Multi-IDE MCP Scaffolding, Context Budgeting & Invariant Pinning (v0.2.25 - Scheduled)
- [ ] **Pipeline Stage Context Budgeting & Invariant Pinning (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The sequential `MultiAgentPipeline` (`pipeline.py:196-199`) accumulates context linearly without truncation — a 5-stage pipeline generates ~20K tokens of bloat. `AgentMemory` auto-summarization at 96K chars (`memory.py:26-28`) is lossy, discarding verbatim invariant constraint wording. System instructions (AGENTS.md rules, complexity caps) are progressively evicted from model attention as tool outputs and conversation history accumulate.
  - *Implementation*: Apply `ContextPacker` binary search truncation to inter-stage pipeline context with per-stage budgets (~4K tokens). Partition `AgentMemory` into a volatile conversation buffer (subject to auto-summarization) and an invariant constraint store (never summarized, re-injected verbatim at head of each turn). Inject a compressed invariant reminder block every N turns.
- [ ] **Structured Constraint Propagation Across Subagent Delegation (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Each agent delegation boundary loses ~15% constraint fidelity through prompt decomposition. Three layers of delegation retain only $0.85^3 \approx 61\%$ of the original intent. `SubAgents.delegate_task` constructs child prompts from flat text without structured constraint annotations.
  - *Implementation*: Annotate delegation prompts with typed constraint blocks (`invariants`, `budget`, `security`) that must propagate verbatim to all descendants. Replace free-text subagent results with `SubagentResult` schemas containing `status`, `warnings`, `constraints_verified`, and `constraints_violated`.
- [ ] **MCP Resource-First Data Access & Tool Output Sandboxing (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: MCP Resources (`resource://`) provide URI-addressable, cacheable, subscription-capable data endpoints — 3× cheaper than Tool calls for read-heavy patterns. Yet the `devops-cli` MCP server relies almost exclusively on Tools for all data access. Additionally, MCP tool return strings are untyped — an attacker controlling tool output can inject instructions interpreted as system directives.
  - *Implementation*: Convert read-heavy, stable-state inspection endpoints (`k8s_status`, `argo_status`, `docker_stats`, `vault_status`, `telemetry_status`, `gh_rate_limit`) from Tools to MCP Resources with URI subscriptions. Apply sanitization pipeline to all tool return strings — strip potential instruction injections, validate against expected output schemas, and cap output length.
  - *Extension, MCP Resource Correctness and Measured Tool-to-Resource Migration*: The open "MCP Resource-First Data Access & Tool Output Sandboxing" item says the server "relies almost exclusively on Tools", which is stale. `server.py` registers 20 `@mcp.resource` endpoints (1150-1266, 2821-2918), and most re-run a subcommand a tool still advertises (`config_show` :572 and `resource://config/active` :1160), so no schema leaves the listing. The done #318 item claims resource subscriptions stream state changes to IDE clients. None do: the server advertises `subscribe=False`, `ResourceSubscriptionManager` (`dispatcher.py:100`) has no production subscriber, and `_run_mcp_resource` (`server.py:1137`), used by 2 of 20 resources, notifies on read. `resource://tf/cost/latest` (:1251) promises the latest breakdown but passes `--mock`. `resource://k8s/logs/recent` (:1260) passes a literal `{}` pod, which `_is_logql_request` treats as a LogQL selector. `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md` lists no resources. vibes states the Resource-First Principle but only estimates the saving.
  - *Extension deliverable*: Correctness first: correct the #318 claim; delete `ResourceSubscriptionManager`, its export and tests (`tests/test_fastmcp_in_process.py:74`, `:164`), so resources call `_run_mcp_cmd`; make `tf/cost/latest` real or rename it as sample data; fix or remove `k8s/logs/recent`; generate a Resources section in `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md` from `mcp.list_resources()`. Then measure: add `--tokens` to the existing `devops mcp tools`, showing schema tokens (`context_budget.count_tokens`), whether the domain gate advertises the tool by default, and its resource mirror. Migrate only eager, parameterless tools (`ai_constellation_status`, `config_show`, `ai_harness_status` plus a new `resource://ai/harness`), after checking that the mirror returns identical output and that each supported client can reach resources. Keep `ai_gateway_status` and `ai_spend_report`, whose parameters no resource covers. An eager-listing ceiling belongs in the drafted Instruction Envelope Audit ratchet. Defer subscriptions: mcp 1.30.0 hard-codes `subscribe=False` (`mcp/server/lowlevel/server.py:211-213`) and FastMCP 3.4.7 has no subscribe API. Revisit when FastMCP ships one, a change source exists and a named client subscribes.
  - *Extension constraint*: A saving exists only for a tool the client is shown. `k8s_status` and `argo_status` are domain-gated (`CONST_MCP_EAGER_DOMAINS`, `constants.py:1716`), so migrating them saves nothing by default. A shared subcommand is not a mirror: `argo/fleet/status` adds `--json` and `tf/cost/latest` forces `--mock`. Tools are model-controlled and resources application-controlled; where only the user can attach resources, a withdrawn tool is lost to the agent, so the per-call saving is measured per client, not taken from vibes' estimate. A notification fired on read tells a client its copy is current when it may not be, which is worse than polling.
  - *Extension measured*: 16 of 20 resources re-run a subcommand an existing tool exposes (`.venv/bin/python probe/mirror_ast.py`). The default tools listing costs 3915 tokens in o200k_base (`.venv/bin/python probe/mcp_surface.py`). The three eager parameterless candidates cost 236 tokens together, and each resource entry 38 to 55 (`.venv/bin/python probe/value_mirror_saving.py`).
  - *Extension source*: vibes `observations/systems/10-mcp-protocol-boundaries-and-stateless-transport-constraints.md`
- [ ] **Automated Multi-IDE MCP Scaffolder & Health Watchdog (`devops ide configure`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Developers frequently switch across modern AI-assisted IDEs (VS Code, Cursor, Windsurf, Claude Desktop, Antigravity). Manually managing `.vscode/mcp.json` or global config paths across IDE updates and container rebuilds is prone to path mismatch and environment drift.
  - *Implementation*: Provide a unified `devops ide configure [--ide vscode|cursor|windsurf|claude|all]` command that auto-detects installed IDE configurations and writes standardized, hardened MCP server entries. Include a background watchdog detecting hanging stdio subshells and automatically restarting the FastMCP server when deadlocks or high-memory leaks occur.
- [ ] **Path-Specific Copilot Instructions & Executable Prompt Scaffolder (`devops ai instructions scaffold`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Modern GitHub Copilot and VS Code Agent mode support hierarchical path-specific instructions (`.github/instructions/**/*.md`) and executable prompt templates (`.github/prompts/*.prompt.md`). Monolithic instruction files (`AGENTS.md`) can overwhelm model attention with irrelevant rules when editing specialized subtrees.
  - *Implementation*: Generate scoped instruction files matching file globs (e.g. `.github/instructions/k8s.md` for Kubernetes manifests, `.github/instructions/tests.md` for structural tuple assertions and complexity caps, `.github/instructions/security.md` for zero-trust egress and POSIX isolation). Provide pre-packaged prompt templates: `.github/prompts/k8s-triage.prompt.md`, `.github/prompts/pr-review.prompt.md`, `.github/prompts/adr-generate.prompt.md`.
- [ ] **Deterministic Test Selection From Changed Sources (P1 - High)** — index landed in #446, wiring remains:
  - *Correction to the original entry*: it said "Coverage data already records which tests touch which modules". It did not. `select_tests_for_sources` (v0.2.22) selects by filename convention unioned with a regex grep for an import of the changed module — no coverage involved.
  - *Why that matters, measured*: per-test coverage contexts were generated over the whole suite and the textual selector scored against them. 387 source files, 317 test files: **mean recall 65.0%, median 66.7%, 10th percentile 11%, and 21 files (5%) for which it selects zero covering tests.** A change to `core/cli.py` — the Typer subclass every command is built on — is covered by 38 test files and the grep finds none, because tests import commands rather than the class underneath. `output/formatters/tables.py` misses 39, `ai/text_utils.py` 24. Narrowing a gate on that basis reports green having run nothing that exercises the change.
  - *Delivered*: `core/coverage_index.py` builds the reverse index from a `--cov-context=test` database. Scored against the same ground truth it reaches **100% recall with zero blind spots**, because the mapping is observed rather than inferred. It fails closed in five directions: an unindexed source, a new test file since the build, a `conftest.py` / `pyproject.toml` / `uv.lock` / `.python-version` change, a path outside `src/`, and any unreadable or older-format index all return "run everything".
  - *Remaining*: generate the index as part of the full run, store it under `.data/`, and have `devops ci test` consult it. Keep the textual selector as the fallback when no index exists.
  - *Constraint, unchanged and now quantified*: under-selection ships an untested change. The index may be authoritative only where its own staleness rules hold; CI should keep running the full suite until the index has been observed correct over a meaningful number of commits.
- [ ] **Structured Fuzzing of Parsers and Schema Boundaries (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: The failures found by hand this release were nearly all boundary cases in parsing and path handling: `Path.relative_to` accepting `..`, `fnmatch` reading `[host]:port` as a character class, `lstrip("./")` stripping a character set, porcelain output shifted by one stripped space. Each was found by reading code. Each is the kind of input a property test generates in seconds.
  - *Deliverable*: Property-based tests over the parsing surfaces — ignore rules, `known_hosts` entries, service URLs, SARIF documents, git porcelain output, review JSON — asserting the invariants those parsers claim rather than specific outputs. Generated corpora persist so a discovered failure becomes a regression case.
  - *Constraint*: A property test that asserts what the implementation does rather than what the format requires passes forever and finds nothing. Each property has to come from the specification: gitignore(5), the `sshd` manual, SARIF 2.1.0.
  - *Extension, Reach Tally, Replay Gate and Hash-Seed Check for Parser Fuzzing*: `hypothesis` is absent from the dev group (`pyproject.toml:48-60`) and `uv.lock`; no `PYTHONHASHSEED` under `src/`, `tests/` or `.github/`; the `devops ci` gate (`src/devops_cli/commands/ci.py:302-401`; pre-push hook `.pre-commit-config.yaml:61-68`) has no replay or determinism step; no scheduled workflow runs tests (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:27,73`). The four deterministic oracles, `run_complexity_scan` (`security/complexity.py:202`), `from_sarif` (`security/sarif.py:311-342`), `repair_json_string`/`fix_llm_response` (`ai/response_repair.py:45-276`) and `parse_review_response` (`ai/review_schema.py:911`), have only hand-written examples, none re-run in a second process. vibes' first campaign: 0 findings across 320 cases, two of eight instruments unreached; its first cross-seed check: 10 divergences, all from the harness's own scratch path (`observations/systems/17-a-fuzzers-first-report-is-about-the-fuzzer.md:5`); the check exists because a detector returned 15, 16 and 17 findings on three runs of unchanged files (`tools/fuzz_harness.py:565-570`).
  - *Extension deliverable*: Adopt `hypothesis` (vibes `tools/fuzz_core.py:535-587` hand-rolls its line shrinking). One three-PR series. (a) `hypothesis` in the dev group; properties plus explicit `@example(...)` inputs for the four parsers; outcomes labelled with `hypothesis.event()` so `pytest --hypothesis-show-statistics` shows unreached targets. CI profile `settings(phases=[Phase.explicit], derandomize=True)` runs in the existing `devops ci` test step; exploration profile adds generate and shrink. `Phase.reuse` joins CI only with a read-only `GitHubArtifactDatabase` fed by the scheduled workflow's artifacts. (b) `fuzz.yml`: `schedule:` plus `workflow_dispatch`, seed from `github.run_number`; minimized reproducers go to `actions/upload-artifact`, never a commit; a finding becomes a regression case as a pull-requested `@example(...)`. (c) `devops ci determinism` in `_run_all_checks_async`: one subprocess per `PYTHONHASHSEED` (vibes: 0, 1, 524287) runs the committed examples through the four detectors, normalizes scratch path before memory addresses, re-runs all seeds on disagreement, reports only confirmed divergence and asserts byte-identical output. Port only `_seed_fingerprint` (`tools/fuzz_harness.py:576`), `_confirmed_answers` (627) and `_fingerprint`'s normalization order (330-350).
  - *Extension constraint*: The random search never gates: a must-pass campaign fails whichever commit it got lucky on. Only committed examples gate, each shown to fail before its fix and pass after. Reach first: a clean campaign over an unreached target is silence, not health. Never commit `.hypothesis/` as the corpus: hash-named, unreviewable entries. The determinism step is the cheap replay only; vibes' 40-cases-per-seed cross-check is probabilistic (injected hash-order defect caught in 3 of 25 cases, missed at 4) and hunts a class absent here: `complexity.py` and `sarif.py` use no sets; those at `response_repair.py:190,196,294` and `review_schema.py:218,510` are membership or dedup guards, never iterated into output. Add it to `fuzz.yml` once a detector iterates a set into findings, reporting the 3-of-25 sensitivity. Every property comes from the format's specification, not the implementation.
  - *Extension measured*: `PYTHONHASHSEED=0`, `1` and `524287` each ran `uv run devops scan complexity src/devops_cli/security --json`; all three outputs were 20197 bytes with an identical SHA-256 (prefix 068c019e88f2bc60), exit 0. `from_sarif` on a hand-built three-result SARIF document under the same three seeds returned the same three rule ids in the same order. Two detectors, one directory and one document, not a corpus; the response repairer and review parser were not measured, and reach against all four is unknown until the tally exists.
  - *Extension source*: vibes `tools/fuzz_harness.py`, `tools/fuzz_core.py`, `.github/workflows/fuzz.yml`, `artifacts/fuzz-corpus/README.md`, `observations/systems/17-a-fuzzers-first-report-is-about-the-fuzzer.md`, `patterns/invariant-grounded-recursive-self-improvement.md`, `AGENTS.md` §10a.
- [ ] **Extend the Ruff Rule Set to the Correctness Families (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `[tool.ruff.lint] select` is `["E", "F", "I", "N", "W", "UP"]` — style, imports, naming and modernization. The families that catch defects rather than preferences are absent. Measured against the current tree, enabling `B`, `SIM`, `RUF`, `BLE` and `ASYNC` reports 23 duplicate `__all__` entries, 39 exceptions re-raised without `from`, 6 `zip()` calls that truncate silently on length mismatch, and 6 mutable class attributes shared across instances in `src/` alone. None of these are hard to detect; nothing is detecting them.
  - *Deliverable*: Adopt the correctness families in `pyproject.toml`, repair what is mechanical, and where a rule genuinely does not fit this codebase, disable it in configuration with the reason recorded. Silence from an unselected rule and silence from a deliberately disabled one look identical in the terminal and are opposites in fact.
  - *Constraint*: `ruff check --fix` must not be trusted blind. An unused-import pass will delete re-exports that no module in the file uses but consumers import, which is a failure that surfaces as an unrelated test module ceasing to collect. Apply fixes in one batch, then run the full gate set — lint, types and tests together.
- [ ] **Blind Exception Handling Policy (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: 501 `BLE001` sites exist across `src/`, of which 77 are `except Exception: pass`. Some are legitimate best-effort cleanup; the problem is that nothing distinguishes those from the ones swallowing a defect, and a handler that has never been entered is indistinguishable from one that works. A handler in the workbench of the companion `vibes` repository referenced an undefined name and could only ever raise `NameError`; it survived precisely because no test forced the branch.
  - *Deliverable*: Enable `BLE001`, triage the surface, and require every retained blind handler to carry an explicit `# noqa: BLE001` with a justification and to narrow its exception tuple where the failure modes are known. Every handler written to degrade gracefully needs a test that forces it.
  - *Constraint*: Triage before enforcement. Turning the rule on against 501 sites without a policy produces one large suppression commit, which is the same silence with more ceremony.
- [ ] **Anti-Brittle Constant Elimination (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Eliminates arbitrary string list subsets across configuration modules, replacing heuristic prefix/suffix matching with structural git invariants or explicit functional routing logic.

  - *Context & Rationale*: `NormalizedFinding.fingerprint` (`src/devops_cli/security/normalization.py:149`) already hashes tool, rule, repository-relative path and message, not line number (`tests/test_security_sarif.py:224`), and `to_sarif` emits it as `partialFingerprints` (`src/devops_cli/security/sarif.py:92`). Its only consumer, the suppression `fingerprint:` rule (`src/devops_cli/security/suppression.py:40`), lapses only by date (`expired_rules`, `suppression.py:138`); nothing records findings in bulk, and an unmatched rule is never reported, hiding a reintroduced defect. `ScanReport.exceeds` (`pipeline.py:65`) counts every surviving finding, so `--fail-on` cannot gate a repository that does not already pass. vibes `tools/finding_baseline.py` keys entries by the producer's fingerprint, partitions `new`/`known`/`resolved`, fails CI on stale entries (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:39`), keeps its own baseline empty (`tests/test_finding_baseline.py:147`) and found absolute-path fingerprints made one checkout's baseline suppress nothing on another (`docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md:158`).

Port vibes `tools/finding_baseline.py` semantics (`partition`, `prune`, `Partition.is_stale`), not its file format; Microsoft's `sarif-tools` `diff` matches on location and line, not `partialFingerprints`, so it is not the seam. `from_sarif` (`sarif.py:311`) discards a producer's `partialFingerprints`; keep it as an optional stored digest on the frozen `NormalizedFinding` (`normalization.py:122,149`), emitted by `to_sarif` when set. CI wiring waits on the Publish Scanner Findings to Code Scanning item, which first produces scan SARIF in CI: run `--baseline --strict` there and add the baseline-stays-empty test to `tests/test_architectural_invariants.py` only then.

Staleness is not expiry: only fingerprint rules can be stale; an unmatched wildcard rule is not a defect. `--fail-on` alone decides whether new findings fail the build; strict adds no second verdict. `scan sarif` ingests the producer's URI as written, so a baseline recorded from absolute-path SARIF matches nothing; say so rather than report zero new findings. GitHub code scanning already partitions on `partialFingerprints`; this serves the pre-upload gate, targets without it and `--fail-on` in CI. `devops scan complexity` (`src/devops_cli/security/complexity.py:202`) is unregistered (`registry.py:53` loads only eleven built-ins) and emits no SARIF; route it through the pipeline before the Cyclomatic Complexity Debt Reduction ratchet, sequenced separately.
  - *Measured*: `grep -n 'def test_' tests/test_security_sarif.py | wc -l` reports 117 tests over normalization, SARIF, suppression and the report commands; `grep -n partialFingerprints src/devops_cli/security/sarif.py | wc -l` returns 1, the emission site only. `wc -l` gives 342 lines for vibes `tools/finding_baseline.py`. `grep -i sarif pyproject.toml uv.lock` matches nothing, so no SARIF library is a dependency today.

PR 3: the hook stays stdlib, nesting-only: delete its complexity arithmetic and line-118 note; `test_complexity_is_reported_without_blocking` (`test_structural_invariant_hook.py:42-52`) becomes an assertion that no complexity number is printed.

The report changes both ways: over-cap in `src/` moves from 243 to 312, and the 16 findings radon passes vanish (`ai/harness/workflow.py:276` `get_tools` 26 vs 3). radon 6.0.1's classifiers stop at Python 3.9 though it ran on 3.14.7 here; the 'Outward-Facing Capability & Upstream Maturity Survey' item should record its release cadence first. Harness rejections are invisible: both `validate_args` silently drop unknown keys (`src/devops_cli/ai/agents/tools.py:43-57`, `src/devops_cli/ai/tools/__init__.py:145-162`), unknown tool names hit `continue` (`src/devops_cli/ai/agents/agent.py:675`), and `validation_error`/`already_called` return (`runner.py:122-132`) before hooks (`:134`), despite `AGENTS.md:75-77` (forbid undefined parameters). MCP rejections are unrecorded: FastMCP 3.4.7 refuses undeclared keys (`unexpected_keyword_argument`) but only logs them, and the sole middleware, `DomainGateMiddleware` (`src/devops_cli/ai/mcp/server.py:3017-3031`), filters listings. Error payloads are prose (`runner.py:125,142,145,158,170`). vibes defines IFI = schema rejections / invocations, DAI = structured error tokens / total error tokens (`patterns/bi-directional-metric-feedback-and-ax-scoring.md:120-135`).

(2) `_execute_single_tool` records status and rejected keys under a reserved `ToolCall.metadata` key; `agent.py:675` records `unknown_tool`, `already_called` a duplicate. (3) Fill `Instrumentation` plus a new `on_tool_error`, counting invocations, rejections, duplicates, retries, failures and structured-vs-prose errors into `GLOBAL_METRICS` and the OTLP delta counter (`src/devops_cli/telemetry/tracer.py:694`). (4) MCP: adopt FastMCP's `ErrorHandlingMiddleware(transform_errors=False, error_callback=...)` behind a seam in `server.py`, no custom `on_call_tool`; the callback maps pydantic `__cause__.errors()` and `clientInfo` to `mcp_tool_argument_rejections_total{tool, kind, key, client}`, a resource beside `resource://telemetry/status` returns session counts, and a `fastmcp.Client` test pins one increment per undeclared key. (5) A per-run `friction` block on `AgentResponse`, the run span and `devops ai harness run --format json`, naming the worst tools. (6) Native path: pydantic-ai `InstrumentationSettings` bound to the project tracer (`src/devops_cli/ai/pydantic_ai_bridge.py:268-283`) only when Logfire's `instrument_pydantic_ai()` (`src/devops_cli/telemetry/logfire.py:217`) is off.
  - *Constraint*: Step (1) precedes harness counting: a dropped key is no rejection until refused. The default `transform_errors=True` would tell the model `Internal error` instead of its bad argument. Record key names, never values (pydantic carries `input_value`); keys other than short identifiers go to `other`. Count every registered tool, not only advertised ones. MCP carries no model identity, so key by client. DAI scores structure, not error presence. Raw counts and IFI/DAI only: no vibes 0.40/0.30/0.30 composite, 0.75 threshold or CI gate, and no port of the AX evaluator or tool-contract verifier. No cross-run store (`ai_spend_records` has no session column) or automatic issue filing before v0.3.2 `devops gh pm triage`. Rename a parameter only when its key recurs across sessions and clients. With telemetry disabled, counts last one session.

The generator (`src/devops_cli/ai/instruction_generator.py:162`) is a fixed template with no section, token or gate model; nothing in tests, workflows or pre-commit bounds the file. Exact counting exists (`src/devops_cli/ai/context_budget.py:37`) beside two chars/4 estimators (`harness/slots.py:140`, `inspection.py:143`). Gates are enumerable (`commands/ci.py:304-395`, ruff `select` at `pyproject.toml:76`, `structural-invariants` hook) but unmapped to sections.
  - *Deliverable*: `devops ai instructions audit [--repo] [--json] [--max-envelope-tokens N]`, creating the `instructions` group; `devops ai agents` (`src/devops_cli/commands/ai.py:796`) stays. PR1: split AGENTS.md into heading sections with `markdown-it-py` (locked via Rich), count each with `count_tokens`, report per-section tokens and each injection site's payload, emit `--json`, and add one test asserting the injected size (whole file, baseline 20695) stays under `--max-envelope-tokens`. PR2: map each mechanical gate (`devops ci` check names, ruff `select` families, `structural-invariants`, bandit) to the headings restating it; classify sections as fully covered (condensation candidate) or unenforced prose (claim); report the declared kernel's total against the ceiling. The kernel ceiling binds only once a follow-up makes both uncapped sites inject it. Port only vibes' `RuleAttribution` shape and report, not its word-count `estimate_tokens`, hardcoded `CORE_INVARIANTS`/`DOMAIN_OVERLAYS` or threshold `AblationAuditor`. `Path-Specific Copilot Instructions & Executable Prompt Scaffolder` consumes this `--json` inventory; this lands first. Retiring both chars/4 estimators behind `count_tokens` is a separate PR.
  - *Constraint*: Measure before enforcing: the first ceiling is today's injected size, ratcheted, not the 2,000-token kernel of `patterns/just-in-time-instruction-decomposition-and-ablation.md`, whose 89.9% reduction no vibes tool produces. An unused kernel ceiling is gameable via overlays the injection sites ignore, so the binding test is on what those sites send; the kernel is reported, not asserted, until they change. Counterfactual ablation belongs under `Model-in-the-Loop Prompt Benchmarking`, not here: `devops ai prompt-eval` (`src/devops_cli/ai/prompt_eval.py:138`) never calls a model. Coverage means the gate as it runs (AGENTS.md line 14 claims complexity enforcement; `C901` is absent from `select`, the hook reports without failing); attribution to a non-running gate is a P0 finding, never a condensation candidate. `count_tokens` defaults to o200k_base (`config/defaults.py:324`), not the personas' tokenizer; the report labels it relative, not a bill.

On the native `pydantic_ai.Agent` path (`ai/pydantic_ai_bridge.py:262-282`), a BLOCK aborts the whole run instead. Nothing in `src/` creates either capability. Secret paths are defined three times: `ai/harness/constants.py:34` (write-only, no `.ssh` or `id_rsa`), `config/constants.py:976` (sandbox mounts) and `ai/review/verification.py:41`. vibes `hook_sentinel.py` checks every call before execution and returns one typed ALLOW/DENY.

Its `_resolve_safe_path` should call `core/paths.validate_subpath`, which already denies on `OSError`/`RuntimeError`. Sandbox mounts keep their own list. Port vibes' verdict shape and test corpus, not its matchers. Two later steps belong to existing items: a report-only FastMCP `on_call_tool` check of declared path parameters (with Tool Output Sandboxing), and the planned `devops ide configure` rendering the spec as Claude Code `permissions.deny`.

Do not scan stringified arguments for private addresses. Do not add a `PreToolUse` hook that starts `devops` on every call; CLI start-up alone takes seconds. A denial must reach the model as a failed result, not abort the run.

On `pydantic_ai.Agent(TestModel())` the run raised `PermissionError` and the tool body did not run (`.venv/bin/python native_guard.py`). `devops --version` took 7554-8533 ms over 3 runs (`time .venv/bin/devops --version`).
