Neither contains the child. The sync path calls `subprocess.run` without `start_new_session` (266), and the async path calls `proc.kill()` (421), against the intent of `AGENTS.md:44-46` (worded for `Popen`). A timeout therefore kills only the direct child. `core/process_pipeline.py:84` repeats this and has no production callers. `ai/harness/shell.py::_run_sync` (239) has no session either. `ai/review/review_environment.py:253` (#574) repeats vibes `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md` §6: SIGTERM, then an unbounded `communicate()` (268), with a fabricated `exit_code = -1`. Five raw `subprocess.run` sites bypass the helper and inherit `os.environ`: `ai/harness/agents.py:295`, `ai/rag/drift.py:54`, `ai/gateway.py:351`, `ai/gateway_tune.py:287`, `ai/review/template_sweep.py:109`. Nothing lints routing (`pyproject.toml:89`). Open item 'Background Shell Pipe Deadlock Fix & Bounded Ring Buffers' shipped in #449; tick it.
  - *Deliverable*: PR1 has four parts. Land the grandchild probe as a strict `xfail`. Port vibes `test_a_child_that_ignores_sigterm_still_returns_within_the_grace_period` as a guard, which the criteria executor fails today. Harden both helpers in place: `Popen(start_new_session=True)` (never `preexec_fn`), then on timeout `killpg` SIGTERM, a bounded grace, SIGKILL and a reap. Re-raise `TimeoutExpired` with partial output, because callers and `tests/test_process.py:79` depend on it. The helpers take a caller-final env. Route `_run_sync` and the criteria executor through them. Delete `ProcessExecutionPipeline` and the mocked-`subprocess.run` branch (`process.py:361`), and migrate the test patches. PR2 routes the five bypass sites and types `_kubectl`'s timeout as `float`. It enforces routing with Ruff `banned-api` (TID251) on `subprocess.run/Popen/check_output/call` and `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec`. `core/process.py` and `tests/**` are exempt, and the shell background `Popen` and both daemons carry a reasoned inline `noqa`. Coordinate with 'Extend the Ruff Rule Set to the Correctness Families'. Head+tail output capping is out of scope because no runner caller consumes it.
  - *Constraint*: `start_new_session` takes the child out of the terminal's foreground group, so Ctrl-C no longer reaches it and `/dev/tty` prompts fail. Terminal-attached calls (`gh auth login`, `commands/config.py:218`; `kubectl logs --follow`, `commands/k8s/cluster_context.py:283`) need an explicit `interactive=True` opt-out. The runner must also `killpg` on `KeyboardInterrupt`/`CancelledError`, or it creates the orphans it exists to prevent. Calling `killpg` on a child without its own session signals the CLI's group (`k8s/port_forward_daemon.py:55` guards this). Group kill therefore applies only after a session was created, and only on POSIX. The detached daemons (`commands/devcontainer.py:1001`, `commands/k8s/networking.py:631`) stay outside the runner.
  - *Measured*: Against `sh -c 'sleep N & sleep N; true'` with a 0.5 s timeout, `run_subprocess`, `run_subprocess_async` and `ProcessExecutionPipeline.run` each left 2 grandchildren alive (`python probe/value_orphan_probe.py`). A 0.5 s criterion timeout on a SIGTERM-ignoring child returned after 3.00 s (`python probe/rewrite_subproc/criteria_stall.py`). There are 121 helper call sites outside `core/process.py` (`grep -rn 'run_subprocess(\|run_subprocess_async(\|run_json_subprocess(' src | grep -v core/process.py | wc -l`). TID251 reports 17 hits in `src` (`ruff check --config probe/tid251/ruff.toml src`).
  - *Source*: vibes `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md`, `examples/agentic-ide-hook-sentinel/hook_sentinel.py`, `examples/agentic-ide-hook-sentinel/test_hook_sentinel.py`, `patterns/agentic-ide-lifecycle-hooks-and-lsp-oracles.md`, `observations/systems/05-rootless-container-sandboxing-and-process-group-containment.md`
- [ ] **`ci_run` Reports Failed Checks; Structured Type-Check Diagnostics for Agents (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Agents get no diagnostic back from a type check. `devops ci typecheck` runs whole-tree `mypy --strict src` through `_run`, which does not capture output and returns only the exit code (`src/devops_cli/commands/ci.py:139-152`, `:818-841`). Through MCP the output is lost: `ci_run` dispatches in-process via Typer's CliRunner (`src/devops_cli/ai/mcp/dispatcher.py:228-250`), the uncaptured child writes to the server's real stdout, which is the stdio JSON-RPC channel, and a probe with a failing check got back `Command exited with status 1:\nSuccess`. The review verifier's mypy probe also keeps only the return code (`src/devops_cli/ai/review/verification.py:971-990`). Nothing parses mypy or ruff output. vibes' hook sentinel records the rule ingestion needs: a server declining to grade its own finding read as a passing file.
  - *Deliverable*: Prerequisite, and the P0 part of this task: `_run` captures and re-emits the child's output, so `ci_run` stops answering `Success` for a failed check and stops writing into the JSON-RPC stream (test, lint and format share the path). Then adopt the checkers' structured output, `mypy --output json` and `ruff check --output-format json`, parsed into `NormalizedFinding` (`src/devops_cli/security/normalization.py:122`) extended with a column rather than a parallel model. `devops ci typecheck --json [paths]` always checks the whole tree on the warm incremental cache, then splits diagnostics into those in the given paths (caller-supplied, as `devops ci test [paths]` takes them, or git-derived as `find_changed_test_files` does at `src/devops_cli/commands/test_cmd.py:41-59`) and those elsewhere, with prescriptive text naming the first error's file, line and message. Expose it as an `ai_typecheck` MCP tool beside `ci_run`. No dmypy, no LSP client.
  - *Constraint*: A parser that skips what it cannot read reports clean: `mypy --output json` on an unreadable file exits 2 with zero JSON lines. The exit code is authoritative, so a non-zero exit with no parsed error becomes a synthesized error carrying stderr. Severities are pinned per producer: mypy `error` and `note` (a note never fails and attaches to the preceding error), ruff `error`; any other value, or none, counts as error, and `normalize_severity`'s unknown-to-MEDIUM fallback (`normalization.py:39-47`) stays off this path. Lines are one-based in both; mypy columns are zero-based, ruff's one-based. Narrowing the checked files would miss callers broken by a signature change; splitting a whole-tree result does not. Leave the SARIF level-less default (`src/devops_cli/security/sarif.py:217-221`) alone.
  - *Measured*: Under concurrent host load, with a scratch cache: `.venv/bin/mypy --python-version 3.14 --strict --cache-dir <scratch> src` took 141534 ms cold and 8562 ms warm; the same command on `src/devops_cli/security/sarif.py` took 15466 ms warm, so narrowing the file set saves nothing over the warm whole tree. The sub-second typecheck stage budgeted in Cascading Fast-Fail Verification Battery & Token Governor is not reachable with mypy as configured.
  - *Source*: vibes `patterns/agentic-ide-lifecycle-hooks-and-lsp-oracles.md`, `examples/agentic-ide-hook-sentinel/hook_sentinel.py`, `examples/agentic-ide-hook-sentinel/test_hook_sentinel.py`

Out of scope: sanitizer state and complexity parse errors (drafted "Thinking-Stream Sanitizer" and "Gate Integrity"), `except Exception` triage (open "Blind Exception Handling Policy"), SARIF property tests (open "Structured Fuzzing of Parsers and Schema Boundaries").

Safest is not always right: an absent severity defaulted to CRITICAL would flood `--fail-on` gates, so MEDIUM stays, following the SARIF specification. Real timeouts must stay recognisable as 124 plus `timed_out`, as `core/process_pipeline.py:96-98` does. A broad `inet_aton` pass would read `3.14` as 3.0.0.14 in scanned text.

The second half is false: an unknown key returns pydantic's raw `Unexpected keyword argument` text, and the body helpers (`src/devops_cli/ai/mcp/server.py:79-114`) raise on the first breach only. Validate against FastMCP's published schema, never a hand-written copy. Seven private helpers re-implement it with two string caps (256 in five; 1024 in `sandbox/models.py:499` and `dry_run/models.py:15`), while `CONST_MAX_ERROR_DETAIL_LENGTH` (`config/constants.py:476`) is used only in `ai/client/structured.py`. Thirteen sites in `commands/gh.py`, `commands/pr.py` and `github/secrets.py` truncate before masking, which leaks a token prefix.   - *Deliverable*: First, mask-then-bound `bound_text`/`bound_details` in `security/sanitizer.py`, defaulting to `CONST_MAX_ERROR_DETAIL_LENGTH` with a 128-char received-value constant beside it (the vibes verifier's split); callers needing 1024, item or depth limits pass them. Fix the 13 sites, bound `details` in `DevOpsCLIError.__init__`, and replace the seven helpers and the hand-built details in `ai/inspection.py`. Then an `ErrorEnvelope` Pydantic model (`error_code`, `message`, optional `expected_value`, `observed_value`, `location`, `remediation`, bounded `details`), reusing the pair #435 added to review findings, rendered as bounded JSON by `_execute_single_tool` and offered to "Lossless Structured Error Reflection for Schema Retries" as its format; `docs/ERRORS.md` documents the fields and caps. Last, `_run_mcp_cmd` raises `ToolError(<envelope JSON>)` on non-zero exit so clients see `isError`, then `mask_error_details=True`. The MCP output-size cap stays with "MCP Resource-First Data Access & Tool Output Sandboxing", via FastMCP's `ResponseLimitingMiddleware` on the existing `mcp.add_middleware` seam (`server.py:3053`). Measure before and after: longest model-facing error string over fixed failing fixtures, and the share of MCP failures returned with `isError`.

A cap on the wrong field removes what the agent needed: a pytest assertion cut at 256 chars can lose both values, so `expected_value`, `observed_value` and `location` carry their own caps, and `ModelRetry` messages are not cut to 256, which would truncate the five-field-path retry feedback. `remediation` stays absent when nothing knows it. Enabling `mask_error_details` before the envelope exists turns a verbose error into none. Operator-facing output is out of scope: `telemetry/tracer.py:417` spans and `devops ci` terminal output; the model reaches CI only through `ci_run` → `_run_mcp_cmd`.

It gives two reasons: this keeps tests under the M<=10 cap and keeps pytest's element-level diffs. No cap applies to tests. The `structural-invariants` hook (`.pre-commit-config.yaml:52-57`) blocks only on nesting and prints complexity as a note (`scripts/check_structural_invariants.py:71-79`). `tests/test_architectural_invariants.py:146-155` scans `src/` at `max_complexity=100`, and `pyproject.toml` has no `C901`. The diff reason fails for boolean checks: `(msg.startswith('h'), 'zz' in msg) == (True, True)` reports only an index and loses the operands. `ai/instruction_generator.py` does not emit the mandate. `github/roadmap_sync.py:289`, ROADMAP principle 16 and the planned `.github/instructions/tests.md` repeat it. vibes `observations/devops-cli/11` derives the convention from the same +1 rule. Its example of a literal padded into both tuples (`test_worktree_arbiter.py:43-55`) is rare here. The common form is a boolean-valued left element compared to `True`.

Ruff `PT018` (`and`-chains) belongs to 'Tests That Cannot Fail Correctly'. This item does three things. First, add a report-only stdlib-`ast` check in `tests/test_architectural_invariants.py`. It lists tuple-equality asserts where a left element is boolean-valued (`Compare`, `BoolOp`, `not`, `isinstance`/`hasattr`/`all`/`any`) and is paired with a bool literal. Tuples of values pass. No maintained rule covers this form. Second, reword AGENTS.md:15-17: compare values as tuples, write boolean checks as separate asserts, and drop both claims. Generate the text from the instruction generator or delete it, and align the three copies. Third, rewrite the existing hits with a one-off LibCST codemod, then fail on new ones.
  - *Constraint*: Separate asserts raise the scanner's count. If the Debt item enforces an assert-counting metric on tests, the cheapest way past it is the opaque form again, so this item depends on that choice. A detector keyed on the expected side alone would flag real value comparisons such as `(chunk, is_done) == (None, False)`.
  - *Measured*: `python3 <scratchpad>/probe/opaque_tuple_probe.py` over `tests/`: 277 tuple-equality asserts pair a boolean-valued left element with a bool literal, 67 in every position. `python3 <scratchpad>/probe/tuple_assert_probe.py`: 257 of 265 test functions above M=10 drop to 10 or below when asserts are not counted. `pytest -q <scratchpad>/probe/pt/test_opaque_msg.py`: the tuple form reports `(True, False) == (True, True)` and an index; separate asserts report `assert 'zz' in 'hello'`.

It re-checks size with `fstat` on the fd, reads at most cap+1 bytes with `errors='replace'`, and returns the content or a skip carrying `skip_reason` and error text truncated to 256 chars. Walkers skip and count; single-file commands exit non-zero with the `skip_reason`. Raising inside a walker aborts a whole repomap, and returning `None` hides the loss. `O_NOFOLLOW` on the given path would reject the in-root symlinks the seam must allow. String-level `..` checks stay as defence in depth; vibes observation 29 records a verifier wrongly crediting symlink rejection with blocking `../` climbing.   - *Context & Rationale*: The session fixture claims to "Guarantee that tests never hit external APIs or endpoints" (`tests/conftest.py:58`), and AGENTS.md:374 forbids live endpoints in test suites. Yet the guard passes every loopback connect and connect_ex through (`tests/conftest.py:73-93`), and the isolated config enables telemetry at `http://localhost:4318` (`tests/conftest.py:189`, `:197`). On a workstation holding `kubectl port-forward`s, loopback leads to the cluster. The suite reaches the OTLP exporter at `telemetry/tracer.py:1009`, Valkey at `valkey/client.py:133`, Ollama at `ai/rag/embeddings.py:613` and `:186`, and the Jaeger UI through `commands/k8s/networking.py:210`. CI declares no `services:`, so it only ever runs the connection-refused branches. The only per-client seam is the pytest check at `ai/rag/embeddings.py:255`. vibes gives its Valkey client a probe-then-dict fallback (observations/systems/04 §4).
  - *Deliverable*: Close the boundary once, in the existing guard. Wrap `socket.socket.listen` to record the ports the test process owns. A loopback `connect` to any other port raises `ConnectionRefusedError(ECONNREFUSED)`, and `connect_ex` returns `ECONNREFUSED`. Keep the non-loopback `RuntimeError`. Every machine then takes CI's offline path. In the same PR, delete the check at `embeddings.py:255-256` and `DEVOPS_CLI_TEST_LIVE_VALKEY`, and rewrite the tests that set it (`tests/test_rag_embeddings.py:584`, `:603`). Optionally, print denied loopback attempts by port in the terminal summary. `pytest-socket` was evaluated and does not fit: `--allow-hosts` takes hosts or networks, not ports, and its errors subclass `RuntimeError`. Covering the online branches with doubles (`httpx2.MockTransport`, qdrant-client `:memory:`, the existing `TieredCache(backend=)` and `ValkeyCache(client=)` injection points) is a separate follow-up. It is justified only by branches that the CI coverage report shows as uncovered.
  - *Constraint*: The denial must look like a refused connection. `ValkeyClient` maps only `OSError` to `ValkeyConnectionError` (`valkey/client.py:168-174`), and `ValkeyCache.is_available` catches only `ValkeyError` (`ai/cache/valkey_cache.py:60`). A `RuntimeError` escapes both and sends tests down paths no user reaches. Copying vibes' probe-then-fallback would be the wrong transfer: the test path would still depend on whether a daemon answers, which is today's defect, and production clients would carry test logic. Do not use respx, because it requires httpx, not the httpx2 fork.
  - *Measured*: Measured with a probe plugin that refuses loopback connects to ports without an in-process `listen()`, run on a clone of 16ad8e93: `PROBE_LOG=deny.jsonl PYTHONPATH=lbdeny python -m pytest -p loopback_deny -p no:cacheprovider tests -q -o addopts='' -n logical`. It refused 5217 connects to the port-forwarded OTLP collector, 26 to Valkey and 21 to Ollama, and the suite still passed 5732 tests.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/systems/04-content-addressed-two-tier-caching-and-embedding-drift-audits.md`, `examples/valkey-l2-repomap-cache/repomap_cache.py`, `examples/valkey-l2-repomap-cache/test_repomap_cache.py` (the probe-then-fallback pattern is deliberately not adopted; the justification is devops-cli's own measurement)

Pin off-lockfile installs: `pre-commit` at `.devcontainer/Dockerfile:35`, installer URLs at `commands/devcontainer.py:697,709`, tag `rev`s at `.pre-commit-config.yaml:6,37` (`pre-commit autoupdate --freeze`). Stop labelling external `http://` URLs "✓ Safe" (`security/reference_extractor.py:1048`). (3) Add a contract test extracting every `devops …` invocation from workflows, composite actions and pre-commit entries, including `ARGS+=` tokens (`update-prs.yml:92`), resolving each statically via `introspect_all_groups` (`docs/generator.py:347`) and `list_commands` in one shared resolver, not a new walker.
  - *Constraint*: `ci` is a `FileOrSubcommandGroup` (`commands/ci.py:30`) that treats an unknown token as a file, so `devops --dry-run ci tset --help` exits 0; resolution must be static. Typer 0.27.2 ships its own click, so an `isinstance(click.Group)` walk misses subcommands. A path may end at an `invoke_without_command` group (`.pre-commit-config.yaml:72`); assert a non-zero invocation count. Blocking every zizmor rule would fail: cache-poisoning contradicts `tests/test_ci.py:520`. Don't gate on vibes' `is_external_endpoint`: it flags only in-cluster service names, help-text hosts and the `http://github.com/` prefix (`config/constants.py:212`), none egress. Tree-sitter floor drift (`pyproject.toml:42-54`) stays with `uv tree --outdated`; vibes' never-fired audit is not ported: skips are by design (`update-prs.yml:37-42`).
  - *Measured*: - `rg -n '^\s*-?\s*uses:' .github/workflows .github/actions | grep -v 'uses: *\./' | wc -l`: 19 remote refs, all SHA-pinned.; `uvx --offline zizmor@1.30.1 --offline --format json .github/`: 5 High-confidence High `template-injection` findings, no `unpinned-uses`.; `.venv/bin/python probe/wf_probe.py`: 13 `devops` invocations in workflows and pre-commit, all resolving.; `.venv/bin/python probe/wf_probe_isinstance_check.py`: an `isinstance(click.Group)` walk leaves 9 unresolved.
  - *Source*: vibes `tools/supply_chain_audit.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `tests/test_workflow_contracts.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `README.md`, `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md`
- [ ] **Type Escape-Hatch Ratchet: Blanket Ignores Banned, Explicit `Any` Baselined (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `devops ci typecheck` and CI run `mypy --strict` over `src` only (`src/devops_cli/commands/ci.py:333-347` and `:819-842`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:57`); strict already enables `warn_unused_ignores` and `warn_redundant_casts`. Nothing else guards type escapes: ruff `select` has no `ANN` or `PGH` (`pyproject.toml:89`), `[tool.mypy]` sets no `disallow_any_explicit` or `ignore-without-code` (`pyproject.toml:92-95`), and `AGENTS.md:99` bans suppressing warnings with no gate behind it. Every `type: ignore` and `noqa` in `src` carries a code today by convention only. Explicit `Any` density is flat (Measured), so this prevents a regression rather than repairing one. vibes `observations/polyglot/02-typescript-cst-and-type-gymnastics.md` names `as any`/`@ts-ignore` the agent's path of least resistance (line 41); its 80% figure is credited to CST extraction (line 5), not to a lint gate.
  - *Deliverable*: (1) Lock the clean state inside Extend the Ruff Rule Set to the Correctness Families: select `PGH003`/`PGH004` (which also cover `tests/`, never type-checked) and mypy `enable_error_code = ["ignore-without-code"]`, pinned via `_ruff_enforces` (`tests/test_architectural_invariants.py:446`) under its own constant, not `CONST_TEST_ASSERTION_LINT_RULES`. (2) Adopt mypy-baseline, pinned in the dev group, behind the `typecheck` seam in `commands/ci.py`, with `ci.yml:57` calling the same seam: one mypy run with `--disallow-any-explicit`, piped through `mypy-baseline filter` against a committed baseline holding only `explicit-any` lines, so every other strict error still fails. mypy's `explicit-any` already flags parameter `Any`, `cast(Any, ...)` and `dict[str, Any]`, so no `ANN401` parse, grep or merge-base is needed. A justified new `Any`, or code moved between files, enters through `mypy-baseline sync` as a reviewable baseline diff. (3) One line naming type weakening in `.devops/review.md`; the shared persona prompts stay project-agnostic (`.devops/review.md:4-5`). No TypeScript adapter: devops-cli has no ESLint integration. `diff-quality` has `mypy` and `ruff.check` drivers but judges touched lines, not counts. Cyclomatic Complexity Debt Reduction should reuse a tool-native baseline rather than grow a second ratchet.
  - *Constraint*: Banning one hatch moves the agent to the next. A new `# type: ignore[explicit-any]`, a `[[tool.mypy.overrides]]` block or a flag disabling `disallow_any_explicit` would hide new `Any` from the baseline, so one invariant test in `tests/test_architectural_invariants.py` must fail on each. The gate reads mypy-baseline's exit status, treats mypy exit 2 (crash) as failure and never passes `--allow-unsynced`, so the count only moves down. Enabling `ANN401` or `disallow_any_explicit` outright would be the large suppression commit Blind Exception Handling Policy warns against.
  - *Measured*: `ruff check --no-cache --select PGH003,PGH004 --statistics --exit-zero .`: 0 hits. `mypy --python-version 3.14 --strict --disallow-any-explicit --enable-error-code ignore-without-code src`: 2563 errors, every one `explicit-any`. `ANN401` per 1000 `src` lines, from `ruff check --isolated --select ANN401 --statistics src` over `find src -name '*.py' | xargs cat | wc -l` on `git archive <tag> src`: 6.19 at v0.2.21, 6.06 at HEAD 16ad8e93.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/polyglot/02-typescript-cst-and-type-gymnastics.md`, vibes `pyproject.toml`
- [ ] **Per-Test Resource-Leak Gate: Tracer Shutdown, Hang Timeouts, Thread/FD Deltas (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `tests/conftest.py` has 11 autouse fixtures, and none counts what a test leaves running. `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` (`pyproject.toml:97-101`) sets no per-test timeout, so only the 30-minute job limit (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:75`) bounds a hang, and it ends without a stack. The test-only `reset_tracer` (`src/devops_cli/telemetry/tracer.py:1118-1121`) runs before and after every test (`tests/conftest.py:185`, `:205`) and drops the client without calling `shutdown` (`:1030`), orphaning the `devops-otel` workers and their sockets. Tests export to `localhost:4318` (`tests/conftest.py:189`), which the network guard allows as loopback, so a local port-forward receives test spans; a `check_logfire_token` thread died on that guard, reported only as a warning. Production is unaffected (`atexit.register(shutdown_tracer)`, `:1256`). vibes records that `go test` passes code leaking goroutines while `goleak` fails it (`observations/polyglot/03`, `docs/landscape/SURVEY.md:119-122`).
  - *Deliverable*: Slice 1 fixes root causes: `reset_tracer` calls the client's `shutdown()` with the drain skipped and the executor joined, since `shutdown` alone uses `wait=False` (`:1043`), which left threads alive in a scratch patch. Export is disabled outside the telemetry tests, and the review tests stub `logfire.configure` (`telemetry/logfire.py:206`). Adopt pytest-timeout (pytest-dev, maintained), set above the slowest measured test; it already dumps every thread's stack, so no `faulthandler_timeout`. Slice 2 measures: one conftest fixture compares threads (`threading.enumerate()`), child processes and open fds (psutil, a new dev dependency) before and after each test inside each xdist worker, writing a per-worker JSON report with each thread's name, target and frame. Slice 3, once the report reads zero: the fixture fails the test, so the existing `devops ci` `test` check enforces it. Do not adopt pytest-threadleak (no release since 2022); port nothing from vibes' Go stack parser.
  - *Constraint*: Enforcing before slice 1 would turn hundreds of tests red over one defect. A name-prefix allowlist (`Otel*`) or long teardown `join` would hide the next leak, and the allowlist breaks the AGENTS.md ban on brittle subset matching; baseline the threads alive before the first test instead. A naive `shutdown()` pays the 1.0s drain (`config/defaults.py:476`), which noticeably slowed a scratch full-suite run. Drop the asyncio-task check: pytest-asyncio 1.4.0 runs each test in `asyncio.Runner`, which cancels leftover tasks before a fixture sees them. The fd count varies with local listeners, so gate on per-test differences, never a fixed total. `ResourceWarning` escalation stays with the drafted Zero-Warning Test Enforcement task; a leaked thread emits no warning.
  - *Measured*: Scratch per-test probe (`PYTHONPATH=<probe> pytest -p leakprobe -p no:cacheprovider -n logical -W always::ResourceWarning tests`, tallied from its JSONL): 475 of 5744 tests left a new thread after teardown, nearly all `devops-otel_N`; none left a child process. 689 ResourceWarnings point at `tracer.py:1121` (`grep -o '[^ ]*\.py:[0-9]*: ResourceWarning' <log> | sort | uniq -c`). With a local OTLP listener, a HEAD run logged 2251 unclosed loopback sockets to it, none after `reset_tracer` called `shutdown()` (`grep -c "raddr=('127.0.0.1', 4318)" <log>`).
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/polyglot/03-go-goroutine-leakage-and-context-lifecycles.md`, `docs/landscape/SURVEY.md`, `examples/go-leak-sentinel/go_leak_sentinel.py`
- [ ] **Single-Source CI Gate Definitions, Test Reachability and a Lockfile-Pinned Ruff (P2 - Medium)**:

Bandit runs `-ll -ii` in CI (`ci.yml:60`) but `-ll -s B608` in `commands/ci.py:358`, `:879` and `ai/tools/builtin_tools.py:320`. `build_bandit_cmd` (`config/commands.py:125-127`) passes `-x B608`, a path exclusion, and only its test calls it. `devops devcontainer validate` runs only in CI (`ci.yml:69`), and no test compares the argv. Ruff is pinned twice (`pyproject.toml:65`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml:36-43`), and `.github/dependabot.yml` tracks only `pip` and `github-actions`. That is the drift vibes records (`CHANGELOG.md:166`). vibes' frozen `ci.yml` hid 157 of 192 tests.

(2) Beside `tests/test_architectural_invariants.py:403`, assert: every tracked `tests/**/test_*.py` yields an item in the in-process `request.session.items` (only `tests/`: `src/devops_cli/core/test_selection.py` matches the glob); the `ci.yml` pytest step and `devops ci` test and coverage argv (`ci.py:305-316`, `:752-761`) name directories or nothing; a reasoned scope table covers every top-level directory of tracked `.py` files. (3) Retire the exceptions: one argv builder per check in `commands/ci.py` serves `_run_all_checks_async` and the subcommands, which `ci.yml` calls in check-only mode; devcontainer validation joins the local gate or allowlist. (4) Bandit runs `-r src -ll` everywhere, with reasoned `# nosec B608` at `ai/instruction_generator.py:221` (a markdown f-string) and `commands/k8s/stack_lifecycle.py:213`; delete `build_bandit_cmd` with its re-export, test and `DEFAULT_BANDIT_EXCLUDE`. (5) Last, ruff-pre-commit becomes `repo: local`, `language: system` hooks: `uv run --no-sync ruff check --fix --force-exclude` and `ruff format --force-exclude`.

Its `nosec` reason says that, not "not SQL". Land the `nosec` lines before dropping `-ii` and `-s B608`. Reachability skips narrowed sessions (paths, `-k`), and `devops ci test <paths>` is exempt from no-enumeration. Parity and scope data never feed the cache key ('Per-Check Input Scoping for the CI Cache'). Pre-push checks the working copy, untracked files included (`src/devops_cli/ci/cache.py:168-172`), so never document "local green means CI green". Do not port vibes' pre-push `pytest --collect-only` hook (vibes `.pre-commit-config.yaml:45-53`), because the full suite collects every module. Files-examined counts wait for 'Gate Integrity: Missing Targets Fail, Checked Files Counted'.
  - *Measured*: `bandit -r src -ll -f json -q`: 2 findings, both B608 MEDIUM/LOW. `-ll -s B608` and `-ll -ii` report none, so the policies differ but neither fails today. All 361 tracked `tests/**/test_*.py` files contributed an item (`pytest --collect-only -q -p no:xdist -p no:cacheprovider -o addopts=""` against `git ls-files 'tests/*'`). 365 tracked `.py` files sit outside the `src` scope of mypy and bandit (`git ls-files '*.py' | cut -d/ -f1 | sort | uniq -c`). `bandit -r <missing> -ll` exits 0, so a stale path certifies clean.

- [ ] **Declared MCP ToolAnnotations Gating Autonomous Invocation of Mutating Tools (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: All 156 tools in `src/devops_cli/ai/mcp/server.py` use a bare `@mcp.tool()` with no `annotations=`, so a client cannot tell `tf_apply` (:667, `auto_approve=True` by default), `valkey_flush` (:2501), `k8s_teardown_stack` (:299) or `vault_set` (:2056) from a read-only tool. `devops ai pipeline` hands every stage `get_default_tools()` (`commands/ai.py:1208`, `tools/registry.py:63`, `agents/pipeline.py:103-119`), and the bridge (`tools/mcp_bridge.py:12`) returns each component's raw `fn`, resources and prompts included. The agent loop already turns `ApprovalRequired` into `DeferredToolRequests` (`agents/agent.py:665-700`, `agents/runner.py:159`), but no production tool sets `requires_approval`: `add_tool` (`agents/agent.py:538-562`) omits it, `require_approval_toolset` (`toolsets/__init__.py:429`) has no caller, and only tests read `ToolSlot.is_mutating` (`harness/slots.py:212-298`). vibes' tool-manifest schema requires a `safety_tier` on every tool.
  - *Deliverable*: Adopt MCP `ToolAnnotations` via FastMCP's `annotations=` instead of porting `safety_tier`. PR1: annotate every tool; add a test beside `test_fastmcp_tool_docstrings_and_parameters` (`tests/test_fastmcp_contracts.py:196`) that iterates `mcp._list_tools()` and fails when `readOnlyHint` is unset; carry the hints into `export-schemas` (`commands/mcp.py:142`), `MCPToolInfo` and `MCP_TOOLS.md`. PR2: the bridge builds agent tools only from `FunctionTool` components and sets `requires_approval` unless `readOnlyHint` is true; `devops ai pipeline` gets a `HandleDeferredToolCalls` capability that prompts on a TTY and denies otherwise; `ToolSlot` reads the annotation instead of `mark_tool_*`; delete `require_approval_toolset` and its re-exports. Do not wrap with pydantic-ai's `approval_required`: `extract_tools_from_toolset` drops every tool behind it. Skip vibes' `rate_limited`; `run_gh` (`github/rate_limiter.py:1646`) and `run_gh_async` (`:1716`, aiolimiter) already pace calls.
  - *Constraint*: A wrong `readOnlyHint: true` is worse than no hint: it lets an agent run a mutator unasked. The spec defaults a missing hint to not-read-only and destructive, so an unannotated tool must require approval and a non-interactive run must deny. `requires_approval` is a static bool (`agents/tools.py:38`), so argument-dependent tools (`review_pr(post=True)`, `gh_label_sync(dry_run=False)`, `tf_apply`) are annotated for their most dangerous arguments; flipping `tf_apply` to `auto_approve=False` belongs in PR1. The spec says clients must not trust annotations from untrusted servers, so this protects only devops-cli's own loop. "Reachable `--dry-run` Previews for Delegated Commands" classifies CLI commands, these hints only MCP tools; add a consistency test for tools that wrap a `devops` command rather than one shared list.
  - *Measured*: `mcp._list_tools()` returns 156 tools, 0 with annotations; the public `mcp.list_tools()` returns 37 because of DomainGateMiddleware, so the registry test must use the former. `extract_tools_from_toolset(require_approval_toolset(FunctionToolset([f])))` returns 0 tools. All from `uv run python probe/mine.py`.
  - *Source*: vibes `artifacts/schemas/fastmcp-agent-tool-manifest-spec.json`
- [ ] **Typed Step Journals for Partially Failed Mutating MCP Tools (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Mutating MCP tools return prose. 155 of 156 tools in `src/devops_cli/ai/mcp/server.py` are typed `-> str`, and `_run_mcp_cmd` (`server.py:45`) reduces an outcome to an exit-status prefix. `deploy_stack` (`commands/k8s/stack_lifecycle.py:536-574`) prints `Failed to install` per failed Helm release, then "deployed.", and exits 0, so `k8s_deploy_stack` reports success. The shipped knowledge base says it uses `--atomic` (`ai/knowledge_base/devops_cli/tasks/k8s_stack_deployment.md:73`); `_build_helm_upgrade_cmd` (`stack_lifecycle.py:431-454`) passes no rollback flag. The `tf_plan`/`tf_apply` MCP tools (`server.py:656-677`, `auto_approve=True`) pass no `--out` or `--plan-file`, although the CLI has both (`commands/tf.py:139,184`). `argo_fleet_sync` (`models/argo.py:51-68`) and `argo_rollout_analyze` (`RolloutAnalysisResult.action_taken`, `models/argo.py:88`; `argo/rollouts.py:151-170`) return per-step status as `--json` text, with no pending steps or input digest. vibes `observations/systems/10` (§4a) proposes an agent-side transaction journal kept in the agent's context; returning it from each mutating tool is this task's extension.
  - *Deliverable*: Prerequisites, filed separately as Quick Wins because they fix present bugs: `deploy-stack` exits nonzero on any failed release and `_build_helm_upgrade_cmd` adds `--rollback-on-failure` (Helm v4) or `--atomic` (v3); the MCP tf tools expose `--out`/`--plan-file` and stop defaulting `auto_approve=True`. This task: a bounded `StepJournal` of ordered steps reusing the Argo models' `Pending`/`Synced`/`Failed` and `action_taken` vocabulary, an input digest (plan-file SHA-256 for tofu) and the rollback flag each release ran with. `devops k8s deploy-stack --json` and `devops tf apply --json` emit it, and their MCP tools return it typed; FastMCP 3.4.7 derives the output schema from the return type. Typed results for mutating tools belong here; MCP Resource-First Data Access & Tool Output Sandboxing then validates them. Tofu steps come from `tofu apply -json` events, Helm steps from per-release exit codes. Whether Helm's rollback succeeded is uncertain from exit codes alone and may need a `helm status` read-back. vibes ships no code.
  - *Constraint*: A wrong rollback command is worse than none, because an agent carries out what its context tells it. The vibes example, `tf destroy -auto-approve`, destroys every resource in the workspace, including ones that existed before the failed apply. So the journal emits no generated rollback commands: Helm's rollback stays inside `helm upgrade`, and OpenTofu, which has no rollback primitive, gets none. Steps come from tool events and exit codes, never from matching human-readable output, or the journal becomes a second source of false success. State stays agent-side, with no server session store.
  - *Measured*: Infra stack with every `helm upgrade` mocked to fail: 4 of 4 releases failed, yet the command exited 0 and printed "deployed." (`uv run --no-sync python probe_deploy_partial.py`). 155 of 156 MCP tools are annotated `-> str` (`python3 -c` AST walk of `@mcp.tool` return annotations in `server.py`). Local Helm is v4.3.0 and offers `--rollback-on-failure` (`helm version --short`; `helm upgrade --help`).
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/systems/10-mcp-protocol-boundaries-and-stateless-transport-constraints.md`

### Cognitive Information Foraging & Epistemic Synthesis (v0.3.0 - Scheduled)
- [ ] **Syntopical Dialectical Synthesis Engine (`devops ai research syntopical`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Implements Mortimer Adler's 5-stage syntopical reading taxonomy across multi-file and multi-repository contexts. Constructs cross-repository bibliographies, identifies consensus propositions, maps dialectical conflicts, and generates comprehensive architectural synthesis reports.
- [ ] **Agentic Information Foraging & Scent Tracker (`devops ai research forage`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Based on Pirolli & Card's information foraging theory. Dispatches autonomous subagent foragers calculating dynamic information scent cues along symbol call graphs, dependency edges, and git commit topologies to minimize search latency in unfamiliar codebases.
- [ ] **Living Mental Model Synthesizer & Causal Graph Distiller (`devops ai research model`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Distills sprawling repository architectures into living, inspectable causal DAGs. Models causal relationships across config flags, environment variables, subsystems, and failure propagation domains.
- [ ] **Ground-Truth Source Triangulator & Provenance Auditor (`devops ai research triangulate`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Validates assertions against verifiable source artifacts (AST definitions, runtime logs, git history, test results). Flags hallucinations, unreferenced assertions, and outdated architectural documentation.
- [ ] **Active Marginalia & Epistemic Scratchpad (`devops ai read annotate`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Provides an ephemeral, persistent marginalia layer allowing agents and developers to attach structured cognitive annotations, assumptions, and hypotheses directly to source AST nodes without modifying source files.
- [ ] **Socratic Inquiry & Knowledge Gap Formulator (`devops ai research socratic`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Formulates targeted, metacognitive inquiry batteries probing ambiguous requirements, hidden coupling, and unstated assumptions prior to implementation.
- [ ] **Cross-Domain Analogical Pattern Retriever (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Leverages vector embeddings and AST structural fingerprints to discover analogical design patterns, refactoring precedents, and defect fixes across disparate modules and sister repositories.
- [ ] **Information Scent Trail Visualizer & Breadcrumb Tree (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Terminal and Markdown visualization rendering exploration pathways, cue strengths, and backtracking decision points for deep agentic investigations.
- [ ] **FastMCP Cognitive Research Tools & Epistemic Resources (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Native FastMCP tools (`research_syntopical`, `research_forage`, `research_model`, `research_triangulate`, `research_socratic`) and dynamic system resources (`resource://research/bibliography`, `resource://research/causal-dag`, `resource://research/marginalia`).
- [ ] **Outward-Facing Capability & Upstream Maturity Survey (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Every work generator here looks inward. `devops ci`, the review pipeline, the scanners and the roadmap reconciler all answer "what is wrong with what exists" or "what did we say we would build". None can observe that a capability is behind comparable tooling, or that one of the thirty pinned runtime dependencies is no longer maintained. `uv tree --outdated` reports version freshness, which is not the same question: a package can be current and abandoned, and the last release of an archived project is always the newest one.
  - *Deliverable*: A survey that scores upstream maintenance from mechanical signals — last commit, release cadence, archival status, licence — for every pinned dependency, and compares this project's capabilities against comparable tools, emitting the differences as roadmap items.
  - *Constraint*: A survey is a work generator, and one that guesses manufactures a backlog out of its own ignorance. A capability claimed for a comparable project must carry a citation, and a feature nobody has assessed must record as unknown rather than absent, so that silence can never become scheduled work.
  - *Extension, Survey Gap Disposition and Marker-Keyed Emission Against Declared Dependencies*: #425, the Outward-Facing Capability & Upstream Maturity Survey, ends by "emitting the differences as roadmap items". That answers "build" before asking whether to reuse, and never says how a re-run recognises earlier proposals. vibes `docs/RETROSPECTIVE.md` rejected that framing after finding reimplemented declared dependencies. The same happens here despite AGENTS.md:25. `aiolimiter` is declared and used (pyproject.toml:41, `github/rate_limiter.py:30`), yet `ai/review/pool.py:17` hand-rolls a token bucket whose only production call is `await acquire(1.0)` (:119), and `ai/governance.py:50` holds another. `rate_limiter.py:31` also imports `ratelimit`, which the roadmap never mentions. Agents add roadmap items by hand (AGENTS.md:113-125).
  - *Extension deliverable*: First, survey-free fixes: replace pool.py's bucket with `AsyncLimiter` (map test-only `try_acquire`, `tests/test_ai_review_pool.py:26`, to `has_capacity`), and drop `ratelimit` or seam a maintained limiter. The `sync-roadmap` priority fix landed in #583. The limiter cases become regression fixtures. Second, a report-only declared-dependency audit through one deps.dev v3 adapter keyed by PyPI package and version (licence, publish date, repository, Scorecard checks), snapshotted at an explicit reference time. Third, once the cited capability manifest exists, rank gaps: integrate if the package is declared, adopt if it is marked adoptable and passes Scorecard's Maintained and License checks, else build. Read declarations via `extract_dependencies_from_text` (`security/reference_extractor.py:1551`), canonicalising both sides with `packaging.utils.canonicalize_name`. Fourth, with the survey's first write, render each gap as `- [ ] **Title (P2 - Medium)**:` with *Finding*, *Evidence* and `*Survey*: landscape-gap:<capability>/<feature>`. Skip markers found in ROADMAP.md or `git log -S'<marker>' -- docs/ROADMAP.md`. Splice the rest at the named milestone's end via `_ROADMAP_SUBSECTION_REGEX` and the Section 2 bounds (roadmap_sync.py:184-201). Dry run unless `--write`, which lists added and skipped markers.
  - *Extension constraint*: Naive vibes ports err twice. `declared_dependencies` reads only `project.dependencies` and extras, dropping `[dependency-groups]` (pyproject.toml:60), so integrate becomes adopt. Its push-time score rates ratelimit Mature because a recent `pushed_at` masks an old commit and upload. Dispositions use the manifest's package field, never a repository name. Key on the marker: `sync-roadmap` matches issues by `(#NNN)` or normalised title (roadmap_sync.py:221-240) and task files by slug prefix (:243-251), so a proposal reworded before filing is filed twice. Compaction rewrites delivered milestones (docs/compactor.py:232-278), so the skip needs full history, and shallow clones refuse `--write`. Each proposal needs a `(Pn - ...)` tag, which alone sets its priority label (#583). Proposals stay unsized until the prioritizer ("Cognitive Feature Research & Roadmap Prioritization Engine") exists. No maintained package does marker-keyed insertion, so port the logic, not vibes' shared module.

- [ ] **Capability vs. Quality-Instrument Investment Balance Report (`devops gh pm balance`) (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Every work generator here is defect-shaped; nothing reports whether recent work built capability or tightened gates, and no git-log reader passes `--numstat`. `devops gh` has no `pm` group (src/devops_cli/commands/gh.py:97-106) until `devops gh pm inflight` lands in v0.2.24. vibes `tools/portfolio_balance.py` reports both ratios from a declared-kind manifest with a location fallback; `observations/systems/18-a-correction-inherits-the-frame-it-corrects.md` records why: location-only classification held capability at 350 lines across a 512-line application factory (6% by location, 15% by declaration), and counting touched files reported 52/48 for a window 7% to 93% by added lines (`tools/portfolio_balance.py:197-203`).
  - *Deliverable*: `devops gh pm balance --window N [--json]`: two ratios that may disagree, portfolio (declared entries by kind) and investment (added lines by kind over the last N commits), each with declared share, fallback table and window echoed, and a zero-capability warning. This task owns the path-kind manifest (`kind: capability|quality` per `path`/`paths` entry, vibes `tools/portfolio_balance.py:120-139`); the Upstream Maturity Survey defines none and consumes it. Match patterns with `pathspec` (pyproject.toml:30) behind one narrow function, most-specific-wins ported from vibes tests/test_portfolio_balance.py:153; undeclared paths use a small location table, undeclared kinds stay `undeclared`. Under `fail_under = 90` (pyproject.toml:96) capability commits land with tests, so a test file takes its covered source's kind via src/devops_cli/core/coverage_index.py (#446), else a `tests` bucket outside the ratio, never quality. Extend `build_git_log_cmd` (src/devops_cli/config/commands.py:59-66) with `numstat: bool`, parsing rows (renames included) behind one function, one subprocess per window; GitPython 3.1.62 (pyproject.toml:17) `Commit.stats` diffs per commit. Port per-line counting and vibes tests/test_portfolio_balance.py:87, 222, 252; add tests that a capability module plus its test file is 100% capability and that gateway tune (#484) is capability. AGENTS.md gets the rule as steering text; `devops gh pm report` (v0.3.2) embeds the JSON later.

Count added lines, not touched files. `devops review`, `devops scan` and `devops ci` are capability and instrument at once, so the first manifest declares them, never defaults them. With tests at 40% of recent added lines, filing tests/ as quality reports roughly 40% instrument investment for pure feature work. Land the manifest before the survey: one listing only instruments proposes only instrument features (vibes: 4 of 4 entries linters, 13 linter gaps).

Same command with the location table (src/ and k8s/ capability except src/devops_cli/ai/review/; tests/, .github/, scripts/, .pre-commit-config.yaml and src/devops_cli/ai/review/ quality): 3,283 quality lines, nearly all of them those tests. Same command, its one rename row `k8s/llm/{old => new}`: 43 added lines, which a parser that skips rename rows drops.

### Autonomous Trial-and-Error Synthesis, MCTS & Delta-Debugging (v0.3.1 - Scheduled)
- [ ] **Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) & Tree-of-Thought Solution Exploration Engine (`devops ai explore`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Guided MCTS and Upper Confidence bounds applied to Trees (UCT) exploring branching patch strategies in parallel. Balances exploitation of high-probability fixes against exploration of novel architectural approaches.
- [ ] **Ephemeral Shadow Worktrees & CoW State Snapshots (`devops ai shadow`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Instantaneous provisioning of git shadow worktrees with Copy-on-Write (CoW) state isolation, allowing subagents to compile, execute, and verify code modifications without altering the developer's working directory.
- [ ] **Counterexample-Guided Inductive Synthesis (CEGIS) Loop (`devops ai cegis`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Formulates formal verification loops where an inductive synthesizer proposes code patches and a verification oracle extracts falsifying counterexamples, refining patch candidates iteratively until all constraints pass.
- [ ] **Hierarchical Delta-Debugging & Patch Minimization Engine (`devops ai patch-minimize`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Applies Zeller's delta-debugging algorithm over AST diffs to ruthlessly prune extraneous modifications, isolating the minimal syntactic change required to pass failing tests and satisfy requirements.
- [ ] **Multi-Objective Pareto Solution Ranker (`devops ai rank-solutions`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Evaluates candidate solution branches across conflicting objective dimensions (cyclomatic complexity, execution latency, memory footprint, diff size, test coverage) and presents the non-dominated Pareto frontier.
- [ ] **Valkey L2 Trial Invalidation Cache & Experience Replay (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Caches failed trial paths, negative constraints, and falsifying counterexamples in Valkey L2 memory, pruning unproductive search branches instantaneously across agent turns.
- [ ] **Cascading Fast-Fail Verification Battery & Token Governor (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Hierarchical verification pipeline executing fastest checks first (AST syntax < 10ms, typecheck < 500ms, lint < 1s, unit tests < 5s, full integration < 30s) to abort unviable branches early and preserve token quotas.
- [ ] **Canary Resilience & Chaos Fault-Injection Verifier (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Automatically injects network partitions, process terminations, and latency spikes into shadow sandbox environments to verify candidate patch resilience.
- [ ] **FastMCP Solution Discovery Tools & Dynamic Resources (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Native FastMCP tools (`explore_mcts`, `explore_cegis`, `patch_minimize`, `rank_solutions`) and dynamic resources (`resource://explore/tree`, `resource://explore/pareto`).

The Triage & Quality Table view sorts by Priority, and the Value vs Effort Priority Matrix sorts by Value then Effort (`.github/project-template.json`; `sort_by` at `src/devops_cli/github/projects.py:57`). `devops gh issues triage` (`src/devops_cli/commands/gh.py:1067`) is a compliance audit. Nothing reads issue dependencies: `src/devops_cli/github/` fetches no `blockedBy`, `blocking` or sub-issues, and no module imports `graphlib`. The only work-item dependency check is the harness planner's `depends_on`, which rejects only self edges and direct reciprocal edges (`src/devops_cli/ai/harness/planning.py:578-590`). vibes `tools/sdlc_project_manager.py:154-201` ranks with an additive score: priority base, label modifiers, +150 per item unblocked, PR modifiers, ROI, aging capped at 200, and x0.2 when blocked. It finds cycles by DFS (`:117-142`), and `patterns/autonomous-sdlc-project-management.md:49-66` describes the method. On this backlog its dependency and aging terms have no effect (see Measured), so the score reduces to a Priority sort.

Fetch GitHub's native issue dependencies (GraphQL `blockedBy`/`blocking`) and build the graph with stdlib `graphlib.TopologicalSorter`. Print a ready list of open, unblocked, non-epic issues ordered by a documented key (Priority field, `createdAt`, number), followed by the blocked issues with their open blockers named. Add JSON output second. Take vibes' "top unblocked issue" rule but not its constants, and correct this item's matrix resource from NetworkX to graphlib. Parse no `Blocked by #N` lines from issue bodies and add no weights. Revisit a weighted score only once the backlog has dependency edges and a recorded case where the key chose wrongly. Promotion and Status write-back remain later slices. PR stagnation stays with In-Flight Work, PR Stagnation & Blocker Radar (`devops gh pm inflight`).

A cycle fails the command as a finding. `CycleError.args[1]` names only one cycle per `prepare()`, so the message must say more may exist. The cycle must never become a 0.2 multiplier, which would leave every member stuck at the bottom for good. Eight uncalibrated weights in config would look like measured policy when they are not. A second edge source parsed from prose would disagree with the native one.

A `gh api graphql` query of `issueDependenciesSummary{blockedBy blocking}` and `subIssuesSummary{total}` finds 0 of them with a dependency edge or sub-issue. A regex for `blocked by|depends on|blocks #N` over their bodies matches 0.

Port `tests/test_reliability_slo.py`, not the CLI or collectors. slo-generator (PyPI 2.6.0, 2024-03-27) needs a metrics backend Actions runners lack, so the arithmetic stays in-repo. History is derived: `gate_pass_rate`/`feedback_latency` from check runs over the last N `main` commits; `nesting_compliance`, `complexity_compliance` (M <= 10), `headroom_saturation` (M < 7) from `analyze_file_complexity` per commit tree. `devops ci budget` renders per-objective budget state, phase and a gating-only release verdict, with `--json`. PRs in order: (1) models, arithmetic, tests; (2) collectors and the read-only command, exit code zero until one full window is observed; (3) documents rewritten to the policy table. `ci.yml` wiring and `devops gh pm` consumption are separate follow-ups.

Nesting: 0 breaches, zero-budget target, `min_valid_events` 1. Complexity: target = observed ratio at adoption, raised only by explicit commit, never lowered during a breach. Latency and headroom steer only: vibes `CHANGELOG.md:246` fixed a `toil_containment` red build by `gating`, not the threshold. The `FeedbackRecord` JSONL feeds prompt evaluation (`src/devops_cli/ai/prompt_eval.py:108`), not work tracking. vibes exports non-positive feedback as P1/P2/P3 backlog records (`tools/resource_iteration_workbench.py:1318`), with advisory smells excluded upstream (`:1026-1080`); six of its seven exported items were missing docstrings, numbered `1000 + idx`, a warning about generator output, not validation.

First slice, schedulable ahead of the rest: one adapter mapping complexity and verified review findings to `NormalizedFinding` (`src/devops_cli/security/normalization.py:123`), so `to_sarif`, fingerprinting, suppression and the drafted "Finding Baseline for Incremental Scan Gate Adoption (`devops scan baseline`)" apply unchanged; that draft leaves this routing unsequenced. `measured`, `threshold` and `excess` go in `result.properties`; fix prose stays in rule `help.text` (`sarif.py:57`), and the `security` tag is emitted for security tools only (`sarif.py:64`). Second slice: `devops gh issues sync-findings`, dry-run by default with `--limit`, reading verified review findings by default; scanner and complexity SARIF are opt-in, since code scanning owns their alerts. Create and close via `create_repository_issue` and `close_repository_issue` (`src/devops_cli/github/issues.py:245,526`), dedup by the `partialFingerprints` value stored in the issue body under a dedicated label, priority from normalized severity. A complete run closes issues whose fingerprint vanished; a partial run closes nothing.

A finding gates only if it is new against a recorded baseline or its producer exits non-zero on it, so complexity contributes zero today; synced unfiltered it would open 119 issues (one per file) restating "Cyclomatic Complexity Debt Reduction". The mapped rule_id must be fixed per metric and the message free of the measured value: `fingerprint` hashes tool, rule_id, path and message (`normalization.py:149`), and `extract_rule_id` digests the title (`:103-114`), so the `(N > 10)` at `complexity.py:177` would reopen issues on every change and escape the baseline. Optional-analyzer failure paths belong to "Blind Exception Handling Policy".

- [ ] **Automated Pre-Rebase Merge Conflict Dry-Runner (`devops pr check-readiness --dry-rebase`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: In-memory git three-way tree merge analysis (`git merge-tree`) to proactively detect conflicting hunks before initiating PR rebases or merges.
  - *Extension, False vs. Real Conflict Classification With mergiraf*: `devops pr update` calls GitHub's update-branch API and on failure prints only the API message (`src/devops_cli/commands/pr.py:1514-1549`). Readiness says "has merge conflicts" for `dirty`/`conflicting` (`pr.py:1196`) and `pr wait` says "Merge conflicts with base branch" for `dirty` (`github/pr_monitor.py:532`). `update_branch_conflict` (`lang/en/messages.py:480`) is defined and never used. Nothing in `src/` runs `merge-tree` or a merge driver. The parent dry-runner would run `git merge-tree`, but alone it reports two branches appending different functions as a content conflict, repeating GitHub's verdict. vibes separates additive from same-symbol conflicts, but its reconciler re-emits code through `ast.unparse` and drops every comment. Observation 35's 68.2% false-conflict rate is unreproduced, and the file gives two branch counts (50 and 120).
  - *Extension deliverable*: Lands after the parent dry-runner. Adopt mergiraf, a maintained tree-sitter structured merge driver, as an optional binary behind a seam modelled on `ai/diff/difftastic.py` and `core/binaries.py`; do not port the vibes reconciler. PR 1: after the plain `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only` against a freshly fetched base, re-run it with mergiraf set for that invocation only (`-c core.attributesFile=<temp> -c merge.mergiraf.driver=...`), the temp file built from `mergiraf languages --gitattributes`. Label each conflicted file `resolved-structurally` (clean on the second pass), `true-conflict` (mergiraf parsed it and still conflicted) or `unsupported` (no mergiraf grammar, e.g. Markdown such as `docs/ROADMAP.md`, or a rename/delete conflict), and report per-PR counts per label. Never write the working tree or push. Local CLI only; CI use is a follow-up. PR 2: name collided symbols through `src/devops_cli/ai/ast/engine.py`.
  - *Extension constraint*: mergiraf is GPL-3.0-only (crates.io metadata, 0.19.1) and devops-cli is MIT: invoke a user-installed binary; never vendor, bundle, declare it as a dependency or add it to the devcontainer image. When it is missing, print the install command and the version checked, and show the plain result. "Resolves structurally" means the text merges, not that the code is right. The result is advisory: no readiness pass, auto-resolve or new blocker (see "Defect PRs Carry Their Hardening: Readiness Blocker and Post-Merge Audit"). A driver that always succeeds, like git's `union`, labels every conflict false, so beyond subprocess-mocked unit tests one CI job runs a pinned, checksum-verified mergiraf on an additive pair (expect `resolved-structurally`) and a same-symbol edit (expect `true-conflict`), reporting skipped, never passed, when the binary is absent.
  - *Extension measured*: Probe repo on git 2.55.0, two branches each appending a different function: `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only ours theirs` exits 1 with `CONFLICT (content)`; adding `-c core.attributesFile=<tmp> -c merge.probe.driver=...` invoked the driver, exited 0 and left `git status --porcelain` empty. A same-line edit (`return 42` vs `return 99`) under `*.py merge=union` via `git -c core.attributesFile=<tmp> merge-tree ...` exits 0. `python3 acc_mgr_rec.py` (vibes `reconcile_3way`, two appended functions plus 2 comments) returned `COMMUTATIVE_MERGE` keeping 0 of 2 comments.
  - *Extension source*: vibes `patterns/3-way-ast-semantic-reconciliation.md`; `examples/ast-semantic-reconciler/ast_reconciler.py`; `examples/ast-semantic-reconciler/README.md`; `examples/ast-semantic-reconciler/test_reconciler.py`; `observations/systems/35-3-way-ast-semantic-reconciliation-and-worktree-collision-arbitration.md`

### Cloud Web Agent, Copilot Extension & Autonomous CI Self-Healing (v0.3.5 - Scheduled)
- [ ] **GitHub Models Zero-Setup Inference Provider (`devops ai models github`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Routes inference through the GitHub Models catalog (`models.inference.ai.azure.com`) utilizing developer `GITHUB_TOKEN` or Copilot credentials, eliminating third-party API key setup.
- [ ] **Autonomous GitHub Actions Self-Healing & PR Triage Workflows (`devops-action`) (P0 - Critical)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Reusable GitHub Actions workflows executing closed-loop diagnosis and self-healing across PRs and issues. Diagnoses failing test assertions, computes atomic patch fixes, and commits remediations directly.
- [ ] **GitHub Copilot Extension & Web Agent (`@devops-cli` on GitHub.com) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Exposes `devops-cli` as a first-class GitHub Copilot Extension / GitHub App, enabling `@devops-cli` invocations in web PR comments, issues, and discussions.
- [ ] **Autonomous Self-Healing Agent Pipeline & Incident Triage (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Closed-loop diagnostic engine discovering cluster incidents, generating corrective patches, running CI quality gates, and executing automated rollbacks.
- [ ] **Ephemeral Sandboxed Evaluation Harness (`devops scratch eval`) (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Secure, isolated in-memory Python runtime evaluator replacing ad-hoc terminal probing with structured telemetry and complexity enforcement.
- [ ] **Mutation-Driven GitHub Cache Invalidation Hooks (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Automatic invalidation of cached GitHub REST and GraphQL responses on edit, patch, and reconcile operations.

---
- [ ] **Publish Scanner Findings to Code Scanning (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `devops scan` already normalizes Trivy, Gitleaks, Semgrep, Checkov and Kubeconform output and writes SARIF 2.1.0 (`src/devops_cli/security/sarif.py:write_sarif`, called from `commands/scan.py:839`). Nothing uploads it. `security-events: write` appears in exactly one workflow — `codeql.yml` — so the only findings that reach a pull request are CodeQL's, and five scanners' results terminate in a CI log or a local file. A log is where a finding goes to be ignored: nobody opens it unless the build is already red, and an advisory finding never turns it red.
  - *Deliverable*: Upload the scan SARIF from CI under its own `category`, so each scanner's alerts are attributed and de-duplicated across runs. The emitter exists; this is the publication step alone.
  - *Constraint*: A run that finds nothing must still be uploaded. Code scanning resolves an alert only when the tool that raised it reports again without it, so a scanner that stops appearing leaves every alert it ever raised open indefinitely. Fingerprints must also exclude the line number, or an unrelated edit above a finding re-alerts it.
- [ ] **OCI Container Image Packaging, Standardized Metadata & GHCR Package Integration (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: Container images built and published to GitHub Container Registry (GHCR) (such as workstation devcontainers and standalone CLI runner images) lack standardized Open Container Initiative (OCI) image annotations and build metadata, preventing GitHub from automatically connecting packages to the repository, displaying package details, and managing repository-level access permissions.
  - *OCI Metadata & GHCR Specification Alignment*: Adopt standard [OCI Image Spec labels](https://docs.github.com/en/packages/working-with-a-github-packages-registry/working-with-the-container-registry#labelling-container-images) (`org.opencontainers.image.source=https://github.com/dan-petty/devops-cli`, `org.opencontainers.image.description`, `org.opencontainers.image.licenses=MIT`, `org.opencontainers.image.title`, `org.opencontainers.image.revision`, `org.opencontainers.image.version`, `org.opencontainers.image.created`, `org.opencontainers.image.documentation`) across all `Dockerfile` manifests and GitHub Actions container build workflows (`docker/metadata-action`, `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`, and `.github/workflows/release.yml`), automatically linking published GHCR packages directly with `https://github.com/dan-petty/devops-cli`.
  - *Multi-Architecture Packaging & Provenance*: Integrate Buildx multi-platform compilation (`linux/amd64`, `linux/arm64`) for both the devcontainer base image and standalone `devops` CLI container; generate cryptographic SLSA provenance attestations and CycloneDX/SPDX SBOMs during package publication; enable layer caching via GitHub Actions cache backend.
  - *Refactoring Potential & Automated Verification*: Consolidate container build definitions in `.devcontainer/` and `.github/workflows/release.yml`; add automated pre-publish validation (`devops devcontainer validate` / OCI label inspection) ensuring `org.opencontainers.image.source` matches the canonical repository URL before any push to `ghcr.io`.

### Major Visionary Themes (v0.4.x & v0.5.x)

#### Enterprise Fleet Intelligence, Autonomous Distributed Swarms & Air-Gapped Sovereign Ops (v0.4.x)
- **Multi-Agent Swarm Consensus Protocols**: Decentralized Byzantine-fault-tolerant and Raft-style consensus algorithms enabling heterogeneous subagent swarms to independently validate architecture proposals, patch candidates, and security policies without single-agent bias.
- **Air-Gapped & Sovereign Enclave Operations**: Turnkey execution mode operating in strictly disconnected, classified, or air-gapped network enclaves. Enforces zero egress policies, local cryptographic hardware token signing (PKCS#11, YubiKey), and local air-gapped vector/model indexing.
- **Enterprise Policy as Code & Semantic Commit Gates**: Integration with Open Policy Agent (OPA), Gatekeeper, and Kyverno for continuous semantic policy enforcement on every commit, PR, and Kubernetes manifest.
- **Heterogeneous GPU & Accelerator Fleet Orchestration**: Dynamic workload dispatch and migration across heterogeneous hardware pools (NVIDIA CUDA, Apple Silicon Metal, AMD ROCm, Intel Gaudi), auto-tuning quantization ($4\text{-bit}$, $8\text{-bit}$, $16\text{-bit}$) and batch sizes per device architecture.
- **Differential Privacy Federated Knowledge Mesh**: Secure, privacy-preserving cross-organization knowledge federation allowing multi-repo teams to share learned architectural patterns, defect remediations, and performance profiles without leaking intellectual property or proprietary source code.

#### Self-Evolving Autonomous Systems Engineering, Neurosymbolic Synthesis & Continuous Formal Verification (v0.5.x)
- **Neurosymbolic Program Synthesis & SMT Proof Verification**: Unifies large language model generative heuristics with rigorous Satisfiability Modulo Theories (SMT) solvers (Z3, CVC5) to mathematically prove the correctness, termination, and memory safety of generated critical-path algorithms.
- **Continuous Evolutionary Codebase Mutator & Self-Optimization**: Autonomous background engine continuously applying genetic programming, AST mutations, and empirical benchmarks to discover optimal data structures, zero-allocation memory layouts, and algorithmic micro-optimizations.
- **Full-Lifecycle Autonomous Product Engineering**: End-to-end autonomous discovery, specification, implementation, formal verification, canary deployment, and operational monitoring of complex software capabilities from high-level natural language intent to production stability.
- **Formally Verified Kernel & Sandbox Isolation Proofs**: Machine-checked mathematical proofs (via Coq or Lean 4) establishing formal containment, non-interference, and information flow security for all workstation sandbox runtimes and dynamic code execution environments.

---
- [ ] **Falco Runtime Alerts Gated Through SARIF (`devops scan runtime`) (P2 - Medium)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `devops k8s security-stream` (`src/devops_cli/commands/k8s/diagnostics.py:284`) tails Falco pod logs into `FalcoAlert` (`src/devops_cli/k8s/security_stream.py:50-96`) and filters by Falco priority; `--output` writes a `SecurityStreamResult` JSON with `alerts[]` (`diagnostics.py:262-270`). Nothing gates on it: no severity exit code, and no alert becomes a `NormalizedFinding`, although `devops scan sarif --fail-on` (`src/devops_cli/commands/scan.py:855`) gates any SARIF document. Text-format lines get a synthetic rule name and no `output_fields` (`security_stream.py:63-81`). Issue #113 promised escalation, sensitive-read and egress detection, but delivered Falco pass-through; "Real-time kernel syscall anomaly detection" stays unchecked (`docs/agent/tasks/task-113-falco-ebpf-runtime-security.md:35`). `k8s/` holds no Falco manifest. vibes `tools/ebpf_tracer.py` runs five Python rules (EBPF001-005) over a flat event JSONL (`event_type`, `comm`, `daddr`; not native Falco or Tetragon layouts) with a pid-keyed `finding_hash`; its overhead and interception figures are authored, not reproduced.
  - *Deliverable*: Adopt Falco and its maintained `falcosecurity/rules` as the detection layer; port no vibes rules, write no BPF. Build a narrow adapter, `devops scan runtime <capture>`, reading the `SecurityStreamResult` JSON or raw Falco JSON lines, mapping each alert to `NormalizedFinding` (rule id = Falco rule, severity from Falco priority via `CONST_FALCO_SEVERITY_LEVELS`) and reusing `report_from_findings`, `to_sarif` and `--fail-on`. Identity is the existing `NormalizedFinding.fingerprint` (`src/devops_cli/security/normalization.py:149-158`), hashed from tool, rule, path and canonical message; `from_sarif` never reads `partialFingerprints`, so no bespoke hash survives the round-trip. Set `path` to workload plus executable from `output_fields`; build `message` from rule, process basename and normalised target, never pid, IP-port or timestamp. Sandbox policy (allowed binaries, egress allowlist from `to_k8s_network_policy`, `src/devops_cli/sandbox/models.py:300`) ships as a generated Falco rules override of lists and macros. Follow-ups, not this item: Falco via the upstream Helm chart in `k8s/`; Tetragon JSONL with `TracingPolicy` enforcement; tracing sandbox runs, which needs a Kubernetes backend because `WorkloadSandboxEngine` is Docker-only (`src/devops_cli/sandbox/engine.py:168-169`).
  - *Constraint*: An empty capture must exit with a distinct "no observation" code decided before `to_sarif`, which renders an empty list as a clean scan by design (`src/devops_cli/security/sarif.py:131-137`); otherwise a Falco that never ran certifies the workload. `_canonical_message` does not strip numbers despite its comment (`normalization.py:33-34`, `97-100`), so an unnormalised message fingerprints per event and defeats suppression. Alerts without `output_fields` are counted and reported, never dropped. Runtime SARIF is gated locally and in CI, not uploaded to code scanning: pathless findings get no location (`sarif.py:69-72`) and a transient workload is not repository state. Until Falco runs in the cluster the gate is inert.
  - *Measured*: 25 tests cover the Falco stream (`uv run pytest tests/test_k8s_security_stream.py --collect-only -q`) and 155 the SARIF layer (`uv run pytest tests/test_security_sarif.py --collect-only -q`); `security_stream.py` is 315 lines (`wc -l src/devops_cli/k8s/security_stream.py`). In this devcontainer `unprivileged_bpf_disabled` is 2 (`cat /proc/sys/kernel/unprivileged_bpf_disabled`), so the CLI cannot attach a tracer itself.
  - *Source*: vibes `observations/systems/24-ebpf-process-tracing-for-agent-sandbox-introspection.md`, `tools/ebpf_tracer.py`
- [ ] **Schema-Valid AIBOM, Truthful Gating Claims and Model-Loader Rules (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `devops scan aibom` (`src/devops_cli/security/aibom.py`, 7 tests) hand-builds a CycloneDX 1.5 dict its own schema rejects: licences carry `id` and `name` together (`aibom.py:327`) and the `pkg:ai` purl type is unregistered (`aibom.py:293`). Only `*.json` files are hashed (`aibom.py:283`), `safe_weights` is true when a `.safetensors` sits beside a pickle `.bin` (`aibom.py:275`), and the status line goes to stdout (`src/devops_cli/commands/scan.py:558`), so `> bom.json` is not JSON. `trust_remote_code=True` is only a BOM property, read from `.py` files beside a model manifest (`aibom.py:125`); nothing becomes a finding, and the command exits 0 whatever it finds. Yet CHANGELOG 0.2.7 claims CycloneDX compliance and SHA-256 weight hashes, and `src/devops_cli/ai/knowledge_base/devops_cli/architecture.md:150` says gating rejects `trust_remote_code=True` before GPU provisioning. Bandit 1.9.4, already run by `devops scan report`, covers B614, B615 (which accepts 7+ hex characters) and B301; SARIF emission exists (`security/sarif.py`, #363). Both vLLM Deployments serve Hub ids without `--revision` (`k8s/llm/vllm/deployment.yaml:69`). vibes adds remote-code, plaintext-HTTP and pickle-weight rules, but its CycloneDX 1.6 output is also schema-invalid.
  - *Deliverable*: Report-only, in order. First soften the gating claims: `architecture.md:33` and `:150`, `it_domains/topics/model_governance_routing_and_curation.md` (31, 62, 71, 148), ROADMAP principle 6, and the `scan_aibom` MCP docstring's "datasets". Then rebuild `generate_aibom` on `cyclonedx-python-lib` with a `JsonStrictValidator` test, send the status line to stderr, list each weight file with its format and SHA-256 instead of one `safe_weights` flag, never emit `id` and `name` together, and use `pkg:huggingface` for Hub ids. Pin both vLLM Deployments to a 40-hex `--revision`. Then add one Bandit plugin via the `bandit.plugins` entry point for `trust_remote_code=True`, plaintext-HTTP model sources and short hex revisions, dropping the `.py` walk in `detect_trust_remote_code`; nothing installs the Semgrep binary `security/semgrep.py` wraps. Detect pickle weights with `picklescan` behind `BaseSecurityScanner` (`modelscan` declares Python <3.13), so findings reach the existing SARIF path and Publish Scanner Findings to Code Scanning. Gating belongs to Finding Baseline for Incremental Scan Gate Adoption (`devops scan baseline`). Deferred: SafeTensors/GGUF headers, Ollama digests, dataset and model-card components.
  - *Constraint*: Consumers reject a schema-invalid BOM or silently drop fields; a second hand-rolled exporter repeats vibes' failure. A hashing size cap must mark a file unhashed, not omit the hash, or large checkpoints look verified. Do not re-implement B614/B615: two rules on one call duplicate findings, so flag only the short hex revisions B615 accepts. Softening lands first, because agents act on a gate claim that has no gate.
  - *Measured*: `uv run bandit -ll -s B608 -f json loaders.py` on a probe gave 5 findings (B615, B614, B301); `trust_remote_code=True`, `revision="abc1234"` and an `http://` model id raised none of their own. `uv run devops scan aibom m1 -o bom.json`, with `config.json`, `pytorch_model.bin` and `model.safetensors` in `m1`, gave 1 hash and `safe_weights: true`; with `> out.json`, `json.load` fails at the first character. `JsonStrictValidator(SchemaVersion.V1_5)` under `uv run --no-project --with 'cyclonedx-python-lib[json-validation]'` rejects `licenses`.
  - *Source*: vibes `tools/aibom_scanner.py`, vibes `tests/test_aibom_scanner.py`, vibes `docs/ROADMAP.md`, vibes `AGENTS.md`
- [ ] **Attested DevContainer Image Releases and Digest-Pinned, Verified Scaffolds (P1 - High)**:
  - *Context & Rationale*: `devops docker sign`/`verify` already wrap cosign, including `verify-attestation --type` (`src/devops_cli/docker/cosign.py:96`, `src/devops_cli/config/commands.py:341`), but are only mock-tested and never applied to this project's output. `.github/workflows/release.yml:24-28` grants no `id-token` or `attestations` permission; `:144-150` pushes the devcontainer image as `<tag>,latest` unsigned, and the wheel, sdist and same-origin `SHA256SUMS.txt` also ship unsigned (`:80-81`, `:115`). The image is the real distribution channel: `.devcontainer/Dockerfile:39-42` installs devops-cli from source. `devops devcontainer init` writes mutable `:latest`: `src/devops_cli/commands/devcontainer.py:136-138` treats any published-prefix `--image` as published and `src/devops_cli/templates/devcontainer.json.j2:3-4` hard-codes `:latest`, silently dropping an explicit version tag. `--published` (`:95-101`) is never read and cannot be turned off. vibes names release attestation a human-held boundary (`docs/MANIFESTO.md:92`) but ships no signing mechanism.
  - *Deliverable*: 1. Pass an explicit `--image` through unchanged; delete the dead `published` parameter and its help string (pre-1.0: no shim); test that `init --image <published>:vX.Y.Z` writes that exact string (`tests/test_devcontainer.py:66` asserts only a substring). Land this first. 2. In `release.yml`, add `id-token: write` and `attestations: write`, then run `actions/attest-build-provenance` on the pushed image digest (`push-to-registry: true`) and on `dist/*`, in the same PR. Image provenance moves here from **OCI Container Image Packaging, Standardized Metadata & GHCR Package Integration**, which keeps labels, multi-arch builds and SBOMs. 3. `init` verifies through one seam, `gh attestation verify oci://<image>:<tag> --repo dan-petty/devops-cli --signer-workflow dan-petty/devops-cli/.github/workflows/release.yml --format json`, and writes `image@sha256:…` from the verified subject; evaluate `--bundle-from-oci`. Report-only until an attested release exists. Refreshing a pin means re-running `init --force`; no updater or self-update command. Commit and tag signatures stay with **Zero-Trust Git Commit & Tag Cryptographic Verification**.
  - *Constraint*: Verifying a tag then pulling it checks two different sets of bytes: the scaffold must name the verified digest and never write one it did not verify. The verifier must bind repository and signer workflow, or any valid Sigstore signature passes anyone's image. Do not reuse `CosignRunner` (`build_cosign_verify_cmd` exposes `insecure_ignore_tlog`); no option may skip identity or transparency-log checks. Enforcing now would fail every new `init`, since no release has been attested. `init` also runs outside this devcontainer, so a missing or unauthenticated gh warns and keeps the tag, never crashes or silently passes. vibes has nothing to port: `tools/living_standard_certifier.py:207` hashes a name/commit/score/timestamp string, not an artifact, and its supply-chain pillar returns `passed=True` unconditionally (`:139-148`).
  - *Measured*: `grep -cE 'id-token|attestations:|attest-build-provenance|cosign' .github/workflows/*.yml` returns 0 for all five workflows. `grep -c '^def test_' tests/test_docker_cosign.py` returns 31. `devops devcontainer init <dir> --image ghcr.io/dan-petty/devops-cli/devcontainer:v0.2.21` wrote `…/devcontainer:latest`. `devops devcontainer init <dir> --no-published` fails with 'No such option'.
  - *Source*: vibes `docs/MANIFESTO.md`, vibes `tools/living_standard_certifier.py`
